# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project

This is an interactive 3D relief map of Japan showing every place featured on the TikTok account @random_japan_place. It is a static site in `site/`, hosted on GitHub Pages. The owner is French and not a developer: talk to them in simple French. Code identifiers and comments are in French too, so keep that convention.

The **source of truth for places is a Google Sheet**, not the repo. The owner adds or edits rows there, and the site reads the sheet on every page load. So adding a place never needs a code change or redeploy. `LISEZMOI.md` is the owner-facing guide.

## Commands

There is no build step, bundler, package.json, linter or test suite. The site is plain ES modules, and its libraries come from CDNs.

```bash
python -m http.server 8123 --directory site
```
This serves the site locally. It is also the `carte-japon` entry in `.claude/launch.json`, for the Browser pane via `preview_start`. The page must be served over HTTP: ES modules do not load from `file://`.

- `python outils/sauvegarder_tableau.py`: refreshes the fallback CSVs from the live Sheet. The nightly GitHub Action runs the same thing.
- `python outils/preparer_logo.py`: regenerates `site/img/logo.jpg`, `favicon.png`, `icone-180.png` and `partage.jpg` from the images in `outils/logo-source/`. Needs Pillow.
- `python outils/fabriquer_masque.py`: rebuilds `site/data/masque-voisins.geojson` (the neighbours' land) from Natural Earth. Not loaded by the site any more: `fabriquer_houle.py` reads it. `japonais()` says which land counts as Japan. Since 2026-10-21 it follows the owner's choice, like Japan's official maps: Japan (Natural Earth puts the Senkaku there), plus the four Northern Territories (Kunashiri, Etorofu, Shikotan, Habomai) and Takeshima. The rest of the Kurils and Sakhalin are foreign.
- `python outils/fabriquer_cote.py`: rebuilds `site/data/cote-japon.geojson`, the coastline used for the water-lines and the islands' shadow (Japan by `japonais()`).
- `python outils/fabriquer_houle.py`: rebuilds two files from that coastline and from the neighbours' land.
  - `site/data/distance-cote.png` is the distance-to-coast image behind the swell and the ships.
  - `site/data/zone-japon.png` is the Japan zone (see Map): 2048 × 2084 over the same `BORNES`, 39 KB.
  - Run it after `fabriquer_cote.py` or `fabriquer_masque.py`. Needs numpy and Pillow.
- `python outils/fabriquer_eaux.py`: rebuilds `site/data/eaux-japon.geojson`, the lakes and big rivers.
  - The data comes from OpenStreetMap through the Overpass API (ODbL: the map credits "© OpenStreetMap"). Natural Earth only had Lake Biwa and three Japanese rivers.
  - It keeps river relations wider than `RIVIERE_GARDE` (50 km) and lakes, lagoons and reservoirs that have a Wikidata entry and are wider than 3.5 km. `distance-cote.png` tells Japan from its neighbours.
  - Overpass is often busy (504 and 429 errors): the script retries, asks for geometry in small batches, and caches every answer in the system temp folder (`carte-japon-eaux`), so a rerun resumes.
  - Keep the fetch threshold (`RIVIERE_MIN`) at 40 km: changing it changes the batches and downloads everything again. Change `RIVIERE_GARDE` instead.
  - Needs Pillow.
- `python outils/fabriquer_villes.py`: rebuilds `site/data/villes.geojson`, the outlines of the big cities (see Big cities: the 20 biggest plus Takayama), from OpenStreetMap (Overpass, same cache as `fabriquer_eaux.py`): the municipality relation (admin_level 7), Tokyo as its 23 special wards. In OSM a coastal Japanese municipality reaches far out to sea (Fukuoka: 1,725 km² instead of 343), so it is cut along OSM's `natural=coastline` (polygonize the outline + coast, keep the faces on land per the map's own DEM: a Mapterhorn z12 tile, ≥ 0.6 m), then simplified (~25 m), islets under 0.15 km² dropped. Checked: every city's land area within 1 % of the official one. The pin sits on the city hall (the relation's `admin_centre`). Needs Pillow and shapely (`pip install shapely`).
  - `--nouvelles` only adds the cities the Sheet robot added (see Big cities): each row of `secours-villes.csv` with a `Coordonnées GPS` and no outline yet gets the municipality containing that point (Overpass `is_in(lat,lng)` → `rel(pivot)` at admin_level 7), cut the same way, appended to `villes.geojson`. The nightly deploy runs it. Checked on a made-up row (Kurashiki: 353 km² on land, official 356; a second run adds nothing).
- `python outils/fabriquer_frontieres.py`: rebuilds `site/data/frontieres-japon.geojson`, the land borders between prefectures, from OpenStreetMap (Overpass, same cache and retries as `fabriquer_eaux.py`).
  - In OSM, Japan's prefecture relations (admin_level 4) are made of offshore lines (`maritime=yes`) plus the land borders. A land border is a way shared by two prefectures and not tagged `maritime=yes`.
  - Ways are stitched per prefecture pair and simplified to about 60 m. `n` is `r` for a border between two big regions (`REGIONS` is copied from `regions.js`), `p` otherwise. On `r` borders, `g` and `d` are the regions on the left and right of the line's direction (found by testing points 1 km to each side against `prefectures.geojson`), so the map can paint each side's colour.
  - It also prints a label point per prefecture (the point farthest from the edges). `site/noms-regions.js` keeps those points, several moved by hand off the legends: rerunning does not change the labels.
- `python outils/fabriquer_pages.py`: builds the place pages for Google (see Place pages below) into `site/en|fr|ja/`, plus `site/sitemap.xml`, `site/robots.txt` and `site/pages/japon.svg`. These outputs are git-ignored; the deploy workflow rebuilds them on every run. Stdlib only.
- `python outils/tuiles_vides.py`: lists the open-ocean DEM tiles Mapterhorn does not have (404) into `site/data/tuiles-vides.json`, so the map never requests them (see Gotchas). Stdlib only. A one-off: rerun only if 404s come back.
- `python outils/photos_jeu.py`: copies a free Wikimedia Commons photo of each place that has no Sheet photo into `site/photos/jeu/`, with its credit in `site/data/photos-jeu.json`, for the game (see Guess-the-place game). Only the missing ones. Needs Pillow. The nightly run does it too.
- `python outils/preparer_musique.py`: builds the music player's tracks (see Music player) into `site/musique/*.m4a` and `site/data/musique.json`, from the hand-picked list `CHOIX`. Only the missing ones; a track removed from `CHOIX` is deleted. Needs ffmpeg (the system one, else `pip install imageio-ffmpeg`).
- `python outils/video/tourner.py`: shoots the 30 s promo film (9:16) in the real map and edits it with music into `videos/` (git-ignored). `--film voyage|zoom|pov` shoots one of the three other films. See Promo film. `--brouillon` makes a quick draft. Shoot one film at a time: two at once both timed out waiting for the map.
- `python outils/telecharger_couvertures.py`: downloads each place's TikTok cover (oEmbed `thumbnail_url`, converted to a JPEG at most 540 px wide) into `site/photos/tiktok/<video id>.jpg`, only the missing ones, plus those listed in `site/data/couvertures-montage.json` (a TikTok cover replaces them and leaves the list). Needs Pillow. The nightly run does it and commits them.
- `python outils/couvertures_montage.py`: since 2026-10-20 TikTok gives no cover any more (oEmbed has no `thumbnail_url`, its embed page answers 429), so new places had a blank card. A TikTok cover is just one frame of the video, centre-cropped to 3:4 across the full width (checked: Ena Gorge's is the frame at 14 s of the owner's file, Naegi's at 15 s; the owner picks it in the app, mid-ranked on any measure, so it can't be guessed). For each place without a cover, the script finds its video in the owner's montage folder (`VIDEOS_LIEUX`, as `tourner.py`; `.mp4`/`.mov`; same name as `Nom (EN)` without accents, case or punctuation, else the only file name contained in the other, among files no other place claims: recent files are named like the Sheet, half of the old ones are not), picks the best frame after the first 4.5 s (Google Earth dive) by sharpness, colourfulness, exposure and stillness, leaving out shots whose colour histogram is far from the rest of the video (`A_PART`: Lake Kagami's video has a Mount Fuji shot that scored first), saves it like a TikTok cover (540 × 720) and notes it in `site/data/couvertures-montage.json`. By hand: `"Lake Kagami=31.5"` (that frame), `"Udo Inari Shrine=Inari shrine.mp4@12"` (that file); a contact sheet of the 6 best frames goes to the system temp folder; then commit and push. Needs Pillow, numpy and ffmpeg (imageio-ffmpeg here).
  - **Automatic (asked by the owner on 2026-10-20).** The videos are only on this PC, so a Windows scheduled task, **« Vignettes carte Japon »** (current user, at logon and every 30 min, `pythonw`, no window), runs `couvertures_montage.py --publier` in a separate clone, `D:\Business\Claude Code\Maps Japon - vignettes auto\depot` (blobless clone, marked by `.git/vignettes-auto`: `--publier` refuses to run elsewhere, because it resets the clone to `origin/main` first). It refreshes the backup CSVs, makes the missing covers (skipping a video modified in the last 10 min: still exporting) and the ones the owner asks for in `Vignettes.txt` in the montage folder (`Name = seconds` or `Name = file @ seconds`; marked `main` in the JSON, applied once, never replaced by the nightly TikTok retry), commits « Vignettes : … », pushes (on rejection: fetch, rebase, push again) and shows a Windows notification. A recent place (video under 14 days) with no matching file gets one notification. Log: `journal-vignettes.txt` beside the clone; notified places: `etat-vignettes.json`. **Never put files the task needs under `AppData`:** the Claude desktop app is a packaged (MSIX) app, so what its tools write under `AppData` goes to the package's private copy, invisible to Task Scheduler (the first clone in `%LOCALAPPDATA%` gave 0x8007010B, then exit 2), and `imageio-ffmpeg` had been pip-installed there too (it was reinstalled for the real user from a one-off scheduled task). D: is not redirected. To test what the task sees, register a throwaway task running a diagnostic script, then unregister it. Tested against a local bare remote (missing cover, `Vignettes.txt`, a concurrent push). The clone updates its own script at each pass. To stop it: `Unregister-ScheduledTask "Vignettes carte Japon"`.
- `serveur/`: the multiplayer game server (Cloudflare Workers, see Multiplayer and friends). In that folder: `npm install` once, `npm run essai` runs it on this PC at `ws://127.0.0.1:8787` (the site served on localhost uses it automatically), `npm run envoyer` deploys it (`wrangler deploy`, after the owner's one-time `npx wrangler login`). Node.js LTS was installed with winget on 2026-10-09.
- Deploying means `git push` to `main`. `.github/workflows/mise-en-ligne.yml` publishes `site/` to GitHub Pages. The same workflow also runs nightly and on manual dispatch; in those runs it first commits refreshed `site/data/secours-*.csv`, new TikTok covers, new game photos and the outlines of cities the robot added (`fabriquer_villes.py --nouvelles`). Every run then builds the place pages before uploading `site/`.

On this Windows machine, the shell is PowerShell 5.1. After a winget install, refresh PATH before using `git` or `gh`:
`$env:Path = [System.Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable("Path","Machine") + ";" + [System.Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable("Path","User")`

### Visual verification

The Browser pane can be hidden, and then `requestAnimationFrame` pauses and the map never renders. In that case, take screenshots with Playwright (Python) and headless Edge:
- Launch with `channel="msedge"` and args `--use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader --ignore-gpu-blocklist`.
- Wait for `window.carte && document.getElementById('chargement').classList.contains('fini')`.
- `window.carte` is the MapLibre map, exposed for debugging.

The TikTok embedded player shows "Player error" in headless Edge (codec issue). That is expected.

### Measuring smoothness (phones)

The owner asked on 2026-10-02 for smoother zooming on phones. The method that found the causes:
- Headless Edge can use this PC's real GPU: args `--enable-gpu --use-angle=d3d11 --ignore-gpu-blocklist`. Use a phone context (390×844, `device_scale_factor=2`, `is_mobile`), and slow the main thread with CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` (rate 4 ≈ a mid-range Android).
- Script a series of `easeTo` zooms (far view → zoom 12.5 → back to 6). Count frame intervals with a `requestAnimationFrame` loop, and read `Performance.getMetrics` (`ScriptDuration`, `RecalcStyleDuration`) before and after. Do a warm-up pass and reload first, so tiles come from the HTTP cache.
- For JS hotspots, take a CDP `Profiler` profile with `maplibre-gl.mjs` routed to `maplibre-gl-dev.mjs` (readable names). For style recalcs, trace with `disabled-by-default-devtools.timeline.invalidationTracking`: `UpdateLayoutTree` gives the element count, and the invalidation events give the JS stack that caused them.
- SwiftShader is far too slow to judge a phone GPU, so it can't be used for that.
- Results: 6.5 → about 68 frames/s at rate 4 (desktop: about 174).
- For the dive, `plongeon_mesure`-style runs record per frame the zoom, `getCenterElevation()`, the exaggeration and `map.project(place)`. A visible jump shows as the place's screen speed changing sharply between two frames. Resource Timing entries for `tiles.mapterhorn.com` tell which tiles were fetched before the flight and which during it.
- **This PC's GPU hides GPU costs.** It is an AMD Radeon RX 7900 GRE (CPU i5-14600KF): 20 to 50 times a 500 € laptop's integrated chip (Intel UHD/Iris, AMD Radeon 610M…). A frame costs it about 0.7 ms of GPU, so headless runs are always CPU-bound. On 2026-10-19 the owner saw the map stutter on such a laptop. To see GPU costs, time each frame with `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` (available in headless Edge): wrap `map.painter.render` with `beginQuery(TIME_ELAPSED_EXT)`/`endQuery`, and wrap `painter.drawFunctions.terrainDepth`, `painter.renderToTexture.renderLayer` (terrain textures + mesh) and `drawFunctions.custom` (per custom layer) with sequential queries; read results a few frames later. Use a laptop context: viewport 1536 × 730, `device_scale_factor=1.25` (a 15.6" 1080p screen at 125 %), CPU rate 2–3. Compare variants by GPU ms per frame, and check each one on magnified screenshots.

## Architecture

### Data flow (`site/app.js`)

1. `chargerCSV()` fetches the Sheet's "Publish to web" CSV URLs (`CONFIG.tableau.lieux` / `.categories` in `site/config.js`, fetched with `no-store`). If that fails, or the answer is HTML, it falls back to `site/data/secours-*.csv`.
   - **Google sometimes hangs a request for 90 s or more** (measured 2026-10-09: 2 requests out of 16; the owner could no longer open the map, which waited without limit). `lireTableau` sends a second request if the first has not answered after `RELANCE_TABLEAU` (2.5 s) and takes whichever succeeds first (`Promise.any`); both are cut at `ATTENTE_TABLEAU` (8 s, `AbortSignal.timeout`), then the backup is used. Tested: normal 1.8 s, first request hung 3.7 s with live data, Google hung 8.4 s with the backup.
   - The loading screen also lifts at `ATTENTE_CARTE` (12 s) even if MapLibre's `load` has not fired (it waits for every tile of the first view).
   - `load` fires only once, often before the Sheet arrives. `demarrer` therefore checks the `carteChargee` flag (set by `load`), never `map.loaded()`: that is false whenever a source or filter has just changed (the seasonal details are painted just before), and the screen then waited for a `load` already gone, until the 12 s fallback (2026-10-12: the owner saw 15 s loads; 1.2–2.1 s after the fix). Benchmarks that serve the CSV instantly miss this: test with the real Sheet. And an inline classic script in `index.html` shows "The map is taking too long to load", a Reload button and the first error (failed file or JS error; GoatCounter ignored) if the screen is still there after 20 s, for example when a module or the CDN fails to load.
2. `lireCSV()` is a small custom CSV parser (there is no PapaParse). Header names are matched loosely by `champ(ligne, [aliases])`, after `normaliser()` has removed accents, spaces and punctuation from them. So `Nom (EN)` matches the alias `nomen`.
   - When you add a Sheet column, add its alias in `construireLieux` / `construireCategories`.
   - Never rename the Sheet headers (row 1) or the tab names `Lieux` / `Catégories`.
3. `construireLieux()` skips rows with no English name, unparseable GPS, or `Afficher ? = Non`. An optional `Début vidéo` column (seconds) sets where the guided tour starts that place's TikTok.
   - The id is the slug of the English name. It is also the URL hash deep link (`#udo-inari-shrine`).
   - `lireGPS` accepts `lat, lng`, decimal commas, and swapped order.
4. `construireCategories()` joins places to the Catégories tab by name, case-insensitively.
   - A category the tab doesn't know gets the `pin` icon and a `PALETTE` color.
   - Empty categories are hidden.
5. Each language has its own column (`nom.en/fr/ja`, `description.*`). `enLangue()` falls back to English. The FR/JA cells in the Sheet are prefilled with `GOOGLETRANSLATE` formulas, so `nettoyer()` treats `#ERROR…` and `Loading…` as empty.

### Map

- MapLibre GL JS **v6**. It is ESM-only, so import it with `import * as maplibregl from '…/maplibre-gl.mjs'` (there is no default export).
- There is no basemap, only these layers:
  - a `raster-dem` from **Mapterhorn** (terrarium, 512 px tiles; the sea is exactly 0 m, so it stays flat);
  - a `color-relief` hypsometric palette;
  - `hillshade`;
  - no neighbours: outside the Japan zone the relief is set to 0 m, so Japan floats alone (see The Japan zone).
- Terrain exaggeration and the pin scale change with zoom in `majSelonZoom`. The relief curve lives in `CONFIG.relief`: ×15 at zoom 4, 10 at 6, 6 at 8, 3.6 at 10, 2.2 from 12. On 2026-10-09 the owner chose this gentler curve (it was 34 → 1.5) from a filmed comparison: mountains change proportion less while zooming, and seen from afar and tilted they no longer turn into smooth spikes (the far DEM has one point per ~2 km, which a ×17 exaggeration stretched into cones).
  - The pin scale is the `--t` CSS var. It moves in 0.1 steps only, because changing it every frame re-lays out all the pins.
  - The exaggeration moves in 0.01 steps. With 0.1 steps, up close (×1.5 to ×2.5) the mountains shrank by 4 to 7 % at each step, and their summits jumped on screen while zooming (the owner saw it in the dive, 2026-10-04). Finer steps cost nothing measurable: `changerRelief` is cheap.
- **Do not call `map.setTerrain()` to change exaggeration.** It destroys and rebuilds the whole terrain, which made zooming stutter badly. Use `changerRelief()` instead. It mutates `map.terrain.exaggeration`, then calls `map._camera.applyTerrainChange()`. These are MapLibre 6.11.2 internals (the version is pinned in the CDN URLs), so re-check `changerRelief` if you upgrade MapLibre. It falls back to `setTerrain` if the internals are missing.
- **Terrain level of detail (`stabiliserRelief`, 2026-10-09).** The owner saw mountains grow or shrink when rotating the map a little. Cause: Mapterhorn's low-zoom DEM tiles are averaged, so peaks are lower there (Fuji: 2,709 m at z4, 3,377 at z5, 3,599 at z6), and MapLibre's terrain read its heights two levels below the view (`TerrainTileManager.deltaZoom` 1, mesh tiles of 1024 px), one level higher in the foreground and one or two lower in the distance: rotating moved a range between levels (measured: the Alps went from DEM z5 to z4 at a 90° turn, about −20 %). Below `ZOOM_RELIEF_UNIFORME` (9.5), where the levels differ most, it now:
  - picks the mesh tiles with `niveau()`: MapLibre's own rule (`setSourceTileLodParams(9.314, 3)` recreates it) but never lower than the centre's level within twice the centre's distance, then dropping 1.5 levels per doubling. It is given to the `relief` source (`calculateTileZoom`) and, since `TerrainTileManager.update` calls `coveringTiles` without it, slipped into that call by patching `allowVariableZoom` on the covering-tiles details provider's prototype (only for calls with `terrain`, the terrain's tile size and no `calculateTileZoom` key);
  - reads every mesh tile's heights at one shared level, `floor(zoom − 1)` (one more detailed than before, already downloaded): `deltaZoom` is 0 and `getSourceTile` is wrapped so finer foreground tiles read their parent at that level. `_sourceTileCache` is cleared once, and the elevation cache when the shared level changes;
  - uses a finer mesh (`meshSize` 240 instead of 128, `majMaillage`; ≤ ~250, or the vertices overflow MapLibre's 16-bit indices), so peaks are drawn alike everywhere.
  Measured: every peak reads the same DEM level at all 8 bearings (before: two levels), the same tile counts, the same frame rate at CPU ÷4 in a phone context. Above 9.5 levels differ by 1–3 %: MapLibre's rule stays (with the heights one level more detailed). The far view's mountains are a little taller than before (more detailed heights). Re-check these internals if MapLibre is upgraded.
- **The relief socle (`site/socle.js`, 2026-10-20).** The owner sent a screen recording: flinging the map fast, new land stayed sea-coloured for 0.2–0.5 s, then the relief appeared ("la carte a du mal à charger"). MapLibre requests a tile only when it enters the view; until then it draws a loaded ancestor, but for a new area none is loaded (its parent walk in `_updateRetainedTiles` requests the ancestors at the same time), so nothing is drawn under the terrain and the background (the sea) shows. `brancherSocle` wraps `_updateRetainedTiles` on the `relief` and `ombrage` tile managers and prepends the zoom-5 tiles under the view (`coveringTiles` at 5) and their 8 neighbours (minus known-empty ocean tiles) from zoom 7 up: they stay loaded, so a new area shows a blurry relief at once, then sharpens. They are mostly the start view's tiles (almost no extra download), and come first in the list so a missing one is queued before the view's tiles. Hillshade and color-relief draw overlapping tiles with stencil overlap, so the socle only fills gaps. Measured (`charge2.py`: `easeTo` flings with an ease-out, 2048 × 1024 at 1.25, empty cache, per frame the share of the view's `ombrage` tiles loaded / covered by another level / missing): flings 2.5× faster than a normal drag over Shikoku went from 44 % missing tiles on average (523 of 736 frames with over 10 % missing) to none; a zoom route from 0.41 screen·s of holes to none; same frame times. Tried and dropped: a socle 4 levels under the view (5–7) reloaded everything at each integer zoom (+15 % data); zoom 6 tiles sometimes arrived too late; prefetching the fine tiles where the camera heads (velocity × 0.4 s, into the HTTP cache): 2.3× the data for little. `avant_apres.py` films the same flings without and with it, side by side. Re-check `_updateRetainedTiles` on upgrade.
- Do not guard terrain updates with `map.isStyleLoaded()`. It stays false while tiles load, which left the ×30 exaggeration stuck after flying to a place.
- **The far view redrew itself non-stop at rest** (found 2026-10-19: 160 redraws per second in headless Edge, so 60 on a real screen, instead of the swell's 15). MapLibre redoes the symbol placement whenever the camera matrix differs, bit for bit, from the last placement's; in the far view its far plane comes out about 1e-7 different on every frame, so placement never settled and every frame asked for another. `corrigerPlacement` (`app.js`) replaces `map.style._placementInputsChanged` with MapLibre 6.11's own rule, but compares the matrices to a millionth. Re-check on upgrade. To spot such loops: wrap `map._render` and count calls per second at rest, with `map._placementDirty`, `_sourcesDirty`, `_styleDirty` and `painter.renderToTexture.needsFollowUpFrame` as reasons.
- **Weaker devices: the drawing adapts** (`site/qualite.js`, see below).
- Phone performance choices:
  - `pixelRatio` is capped at 2 (less on weak devices, `qualite.js`);
  - `.panneau` panels have no `backdrop-filter` (the blur was recomputed every frame);
  - place flights use a lower pitch.
  - **Off-screen HTML markers are taken off the map** (`site/reperes.js`, 2026-10-02). With terrain, `Marker._update` calls `map.project`, which calls `terrain.getElevationForLngLat`. For a point outside the rendered terrain tiles, that runs a full `coveringTiles()` per marker per frame: zoomed in, about 190 per frame, the biggest cost of all. `gererReperes(map)` keeps a marker on the map only when it is wanted and within the view bounds + 10%. It re-checks on every `move`. Every marker goes through it: `suivre(marker, { voulu, placer })`, then `montrer(marker, voulu, delai)`. Never call `addTo`/`remove` directly. `placer(el)` puts a re-added element back in its DOM place: places keep north-to-south order (south in front), names stay before `#papier`, legends and sea names right after it. Names and legends are added 0.35 zoom before their range and removed after their fade-out (`delai`); the sea creatures only while a scene plays.
  - **Never set a custom property or class on `#carte` per frame.** It restyles all ~2000 elements of the map. The pin lift therefore uses `setOffset` (see 3D models). `--t` (pin size) is changed in a dedicated CSSOM rule `.repere { --t }` (`regleTaille` in `app.js`). Selectors under `#carte.<class>` must not end in `> *`: a universal selector makes a class toggle restyle the whole subtree.
  - GeoJSON sources have a `maxzoom` (7 for the masks, 8 for the coast, 10 for lakes, rivers and borders): fewer tile levels to search every frame.
  - The loading screen gets `display: none` once faded, so its looping logo animation stops.
- Places are HTML `Marker`s with `opacityWhenCovered`. Hiding a category hides its markers (`appliquerFiltres` → `reperes.montrer`). Most of them are drawn on one canvas instead: dots in the far view, pins nearer (`points.js`, see Drawing quality per device).
- The start-up camera is different for desktop and phone (`CONFIG.camera`, with `estTelephone()` at ≤720px). A turntable rotation runs until the first user interaction.
  - **Its dots shimmered** (the owner's screen recording, 2026-10-22). The turntable calls `setBearing` every frame, so every frame ends with a `moveend`, and MapLibre 6.11's `Marker._update` rounds a marker's position to whole pixels except on `move` events (unless `subpixelPositioning`): each dot jumped a pixel in turn. `reperes.fluide(true)` (when the turntable starts) gives every followed marker `setSubpixelPositioning(true)`; `arreterRotation` calls `fluide(false)`, which rounds them again (`setLngLat` re-places them), so pins are sharp at rest. Measured (`rotation_points.py`, 2026-10-22 session, 141 dots over 3 s): 1-pixel jumps 6,500 → 0–2, every position whole again after a click. Re-check on upgrade.
- **Right-drag (2026-10-09).** The owner found it too fast and erratic. MapLibre's defaults turned 0.8° and tilted 0.5° per pixel (the whole tilt range in 140px), and with `aroundCenter` it reversed the turn when the mouse crossed into the top half of the screen and followed the angle around the centre beyond 100px. The map options now set `rotateSpeed: 0.3`, `pitchSpeed: -0.25` and `aroundCenter: false` (read by MapLibre 6.11's `generateMouseRotationHandler`; re-check on upgrade): 200px of drag = 60° everywhere, always the same way. The release inertia shrinks with the square of the speed.
- **The sources (attribution, 2026-10-21).** A compact `AttributionControl` (bottom-left): an « i » that opens « © OpenStreetMap | © Mapterhorn · Natural Earth ». MapLibre shows it open at the start and folds it on a drag; open, it covered the map on phones, and the owner asked for it closed. OSMF's attribution guidelines allow an attribution that folds by itself only if it was visible for a while first (on a map interaction or after 5 s), so `app.js` folds it 5 s after the loading screen lifts (`replierSources` in `demarrer`) or at the first touch on the map (`pointerdown` on the canvas container, a user `movestart`), and keeps it open during a tour or a dive (`body.en-visite`, watched by a `MutationObserver`: often filmed). `montrerSources(oui)` sets MapLibre's own state (`maplibregl-compact-show` + the `<details>` element's `open`), so its « i » still toggles it. Re-check on upgrade.

- **The Japan zone (2026-10-21).** The owner asked to keep only Japan and its islands: no foreign islands (the whole Kuril chain showed), and no foreign mountains on the horizon. Painting the neighbours in the sea colour left their relief standing in 3D, so the relief itself is flattened.
  - **The zone.** `data/zone-japon.png` (`fabriquer_houle.py`) covers `BORNES` in Mercator. It is 255 within 70 km of Japan's coast and nearer to Japan than to a neighbour, 0 elsewhere, with a soft 2-pixel edge (pixels of about 1.6 km).
  - **Japan's land** comes from `japonais()`: the owner chose to keep the Northern Territories and Takeshima (asked, since they are disputed). Straits are cut halfway (Sōya, Tsushima–Korea, Yonaguni–Taiwan, Etorofu–Urup).
  - **The relief protocol** (`tuiles-relief.js`) reads the zone through a module worker, `zone-calcul.js`. On the main thread it falls back to `zone-japon.js`, the shared code.
  - **Classifying tiles.** `classer` sorts each DEM tile by a grid of 4 × 4-pixel min/max cells.
    - `dehors`: not requested at all; the same 404 as the empty ocean tiles, also seen by `estVide`, so `precharge.js` and `socle.js` skip it. MapLibre keeps the parent.
    - `dedans`: passed through unchanged.
    - `mixte`: the worker decodes it (no colour conversion), sets every land pixel whose bilinear zone value is under 128 to sea (terrarium 128, 0, 0 = 0 m), and re-encodes it as PNG. If nothing changed (only sea was cut), the original is kept. The `relief` and `ombrage` sources ask for the same tiles, so the last 64 results are cached; each request gets a copy, because MapLibre takes ownership of the buffer.
  - **Measured** (laptop context, a turn of Japan at pitch 72): 36 tiles were `mixte` and 20 changed, about 20 ms each in the worker (at most 100). The zone is read about 0.3 s after the page starts, at the same time as `tuiles-vides.json`. The time until the loading screen lifts is unchanged (0.8–1.5 s, as before; the Sheet sets the pace).
  - `ressac-calcul.js` still reads raw tiles, but its surf only shows within about 14 km of Japan's coast.
- **The horizon (`site/horizon.js`, 2026-10-21).**
  - **The problem.** MapLibre draws the map only inside the world, and `renderWorldCopies` is off. Tilted, the owner saw its edge (180° east, 85° north) as "a square forming the horizon", with the page background beyond.
  - **The sea plane.** A custom layer, `mer-infinie`, is added before `fond` at `style.load` (before the first terrain textures). It draws one quad of the sea colour from −8 to 9 world widths, depth test off, so the terrain covers it wherever there is a map.
  - **Matching fog.** It reproduces the fog of MapLibre's terrain shader: the transform's `_fogMatrix` × world size, `z` and `w` interpolated separately, and the sky's `fog-color`, `horizon-color`, `fog-ground-blend`, `horizon-fog-blend` and `calculateFogBlendOpacity(pitch)`. Far away it fades to the horizon colour, so it meets the sky with no seam, at sunset and at night too.
  - **`maxPitch` went from 72 to 78.** At 72 the top of the screen is 0.4° above the horizontal: almost no sky. 78 shows a band of sky, with the same frame time as before.
  - **The sun.** At sunrise and sunset, `ambiance.js` puts `TEINTES.soleil = { hauteur, azimut, force, matin }` (the azimuth is now computed in `soleil()`). It is set only while the shown moment has `soir` > 0.02, so not when the day/night switch forces day or night.
    - `horizon.js` draws a soft disc and halo just before the sea plane, in the sun's real direction, at an angle of −0.75° + 0.4 × its height. MapLibre stops the ground at 89.25° (`maxMercatorHorizonAngle`), hence the −0.75°.
    - So the sun sinks through the visible sky and sets behind the sea plane as it really sets (Tokyo, 21 October: 16:50 above the sea, 17:00 touching it, 17:08 half set, 17:20 a glow).
    - It is golden in the evening and pinker in the morning. Its radius is 3 % of the screen height, the halo 5×.
  - These are MapLibre 6.11 internals: re-check on upgrade.

### Drawing quality per device (`site/qualite.js`, 2026-10-19)

The owner saw the map stutter when zooming and panning on an ordinary laptop (about 500 €, integrated graphics). Every earlier test ran on this PC's gaming GPU (see Measuring smoothness). Three levels; the lighter ones only lighten the frames of a movement, and the map is sharp again once it stops (see Sharp at rest):

| | fine | légère (`legere`) | très légère (`tres-legere`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pixelRatio` (pr = min(dpr, 2)) while moving | pr | max(1, pr × 0.8), at most pr | max(0.75, pr × 0.6), at most pr |
| `pixelRatio` at rest | pr | min(2, pr × 1.5), at least pr | pr |
| terrain textures (`rttSize`) while moving | 2048 (MapLibre's 1024 × `qualityFactor` 2) | 1024 (1536 when the pixel ratio is ≥ 1.4) | 512 during a gesture or flight, 1024 during the start-up turntable (since 2026-10-24) |
| terrain textures at rest | 2048 | 1024 to 2048, per tile's size on screen | 1024 (upgraded at rest like légère) |
| far-view mesh (`majMaillage`, zoom < 9.5) | 240 | 180 | 128 |
| antialias (only at map creation) | when dpr < 2 | off | off |
| swell redraws per second (`reglerMer`) | 15 | 10 | 6 |
| surf, ships | yes | yes | no |
| cranes, season particles, legends' idle loops (and Halloween's cobweb, night dew and yōkai halos) | yes | yes | no (`body.qualite-tres-legere`) |
| tiles downloaded at once while moving (`telechargements`) | 16 on a computer (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`), else 8 | 8 | 8 |

- **Measured** (laptop context 1536 × 730 at 1.25, this PC's GPU, the zoom and pan route of `mesure.py`): median GPU time per frame 0.48 ms (fine) → 0.16 (légère) → 0.13 (très légère); terrain textures + mesh 0.51 → 0.26 ms per frame. Separately: no antialias −30 %, pixel ratio 1 instead of 1.25 −25 %, 1024 textures −22 % on that part. An integrated chip is limited by memory bandwidth and fill much more than this card, so it gains more. On magnified screenshots légère is hard to tell from fine (the hillshade a little softer); très légère is slightly blurry; without antialias the 3D models' outlines show stair steps up close. The GPU timer costs nothing measurable.
- **Fewer terrain texture redraws** (`site/textures-relief.js`, 2026-10-24, every level). The owner filmed stutters on their ThinkBook (Intel Iris Xe) in the far view, already in très légère: the Fluidité panel read 18–24 fps and 19–23 ms of GPU per frame there (6–10 ms up close). Measured on the same route here (`scratchpad/saccades` of the 2026-10-24 session: `mesure_nuit.py`, `couches.py`, `essai_lots.py`, `essai_rendu.py`): almost all the GPU time went to the terrain textures, one texture costing more than a whole ordinary frame (≈ 12–20 ms on an Iris Xe), and 43 terrain tiles got 419 textures (up to 32 times the same, up to 9 in one frame). Causes and fixes, all MapLibre 6.11 internals (re-check on upgrade; a missing one leaves MapLibre's behaviour): MapLibre re-rendered a texture at each data tile arriving from any source (`_handleTerrainDataEvent` → `releaseRTT`, six sources one after another after a zoom): the terrain tiles concerned now wait `CALME_DONNEES` (150 ms) without new data (at most `ATTENTE_DONNEES`, 600 ms), then their turn, one per frame while moving, two at rest (the old texture stays shown); its own one-per-frame refresh for changed source tiles (`_textureDifference` 'sourceTiles') is spaced to once per `ENTRE_REFONTES` (400 ms) per tile while moving; a style change re-renders the textures only if a layer drawn in them (or the image list) changed (adding the 3D models' custom layer at the first zoom to 7 re-rendered all of them in one frame); a visible-layers change that only concerns layers outside the textures (the leaves' symbol layer at 10.5, i.e. on arriving at a place) re-renders nothing, and one caused by a layer's zoom range (the seasonal dabs at 6) re-renders one per frame at rest like a zoom change, not all in one frame; the terrain depth pass (`maybeDrawDepth`, every moving frame, about a third of an ordinary frame's GPU) is drawn only when a symbol or circle layer is visible (only those shaders read it). With très légère's 512 textures during gestures: texture work on the route −79 % (355 → 75 units of 1024²), worst frame 9 → about 2 small textures, frames with ≥ 2 units 55 → 1, ordinary frames −35 %; the map at rest is pixel-identical to before (desktop and phone, day/night switch, season change, city outline, zoom through 6).
- **Places drawn on one canvas** (`site/points.js`, 2026-10-24: the far-view dots, then the pins at every zoom the same evening; tried in developer mode first, then released to everyone that night, after the owner's laptop read 57–60 fps at mid-distance on battery instead of 17). After the texture fix the owner's panel read 22–28 fps for 5–11 ms of GPU: the processor had become the limit. Measured here (`cpu_nuit.py`, `trace_nuit.py`, `tour_variantes.py` in the session's `scratchpad/saccades`; the same night route at CPU ÷3, and at ÷1 the main thread's own CPU time per frame from the trace's `tdur`, which other programs on this PC do not skew): the ~135 place markers cost about 6 of ~19 ms per frame at ÷3 (MapLibre's `.maplibregl-marker` has `will-change: transform`, so each is a composited layer that the browser re-layerizes, restyles and hit-tests every frame, plus `Marker._update`'s projection). Now a place whose `suivre` has `point: true` (`creerEpingle`) and no particular state (`PARTICULIERS`: `actif`, `etape`, `carrousel`, `nouveau`, which keep their HTML marker with their name, number, tag or pulse) is not added to the map but drawn by `points.js` on one `canvas.points-lieux` (dots in the far view, `reperes.vueLointaine` from `majSelonZoom`; pins nearer, see The pins below), inserted before the first pin (above the paper and the legends, under the remaining markers and the cities), redrawn on `move`, `moveend`, `terrain`, `idle` and `resize` and after a change (`sale()`: a pin's class, seen by a `MutationObserver`; the night, an itinerary, through `body`'s classes). Each dot is a cached sprite reproducing the CSS dot (the category colour mixed in OKLab 78 % with `#4a3521` as `color-mix` does, the paper border, the ink ring and drop shadow, the night glow, the favourites' red ring; `hors-saison` greyed with the CSS `grayscale(.7)` matrix at 0.2, `toute-annee` 0.55, `body.avec-itineraire` 0.5), drawn at subpixel positions while moving and whole pixels at rest like MapLibre's markers, at 35 % behind the relief (MapLibre's own `_isCovered` test, at most 30 dots per moving frame, each every 100 ms). A click on the map canvas within 15.5 px of a dot (the HTML dot's `::after` zone) is caught in the capture phase on `#carte` (MapLibre never sees it) and clicks that place's detached button (`ouvrirLieu`); not during a tour (`body.en-visite`). With a mouse, the hovered dot becomes its HTML marker again (name label, pointer, its own click) until the pointer leaves it. The first Tab press turns every dot back into HTML markers for the visit (keyboard access); a dive (`body.plongeon`, whose recording composes the HTML pins) keeps them all HTML; `?points=non` disables the canvas (`tourner.py` and `halloween/rushes.py` use it: their films animate the pins one by one). Measured at ÷1 (main-thread CPU per frame, three alternating runs each): 3.84 → 3.11 ms (−19 %); at ÷3 the main thread stops being saturated (≈ 980 → 870 ms busy per second). Also: the canvas size is read only after a resize (reading `clientWidth` every frame forced a style recalculation), and the new places' far-view pulse is a disc behind the dot (`.repere.nouveau::before`) scaled with `transform` and `opacity` (it was an animated `box-shadow`, restyled and repainted on the main thread every frame, the map at rest included). Screenshots (`essai_points.py`: day, night, favourites, a season, phone; `visiteur.py`: without developer mode, against the last commit) match to the antialiasing, except that dots now sit **on** their place: `.repere` is `position: relative` (inline in the canvas container's line box), so every HTML pin is drawn ~14 px below its point; up close the crests and the 3D models were tuned with that, so only far-view dots are centred (`#carte.loin .repere { position: absolute }`). New places' (and the other particular) markers, being HTML, now stay in front of the drawn ones. The region and sea names, cities and the slow-path elevations were measured and are small. **The pins** (the same evening). The owner's panel then read 60 fps far away, but 17 fps at mid-distance (zoom ≈ 7.6 over Chūgoku and Shikoku at night, on battery: 55 ms of processor per frame, 20 of them the map's JS) and 36 up close. CPU ÷4 here reproduces it (19–20 fps, 48 ms; `moyen.py`, `profil_moyen.py` in the session's `scratchpad/saccades`); with the place pins hidden it went to 27–28 fps, and the compositor, viz and GPU-process threads did 40 % less work. So pins are drawn too, once their images are ready (`pret`; loaded ahead, `preparer`): one sprite per category colour, icon, favourite, night, Halloween, season state and `--t` (`reperes.taille`, from `majSelonZoom`), reproducing style.css: the 32 × `--t` px head (paper border, aged colour, ink ring, `0 3px 7px` shadow or the two night glows), the 10 × 7 px ink tip, the icon (the pin's own SVG as an image, `currentColor` → paper, 58 % of the inside; an emoji with `fillText`), the favourite's heart badge and the Halloween witch hat (its data URI read from `::after`, turned −17° with its turned drop-shadow), greyed pixel by pixel when `hors-saison`; the pin's offset (the 3D lift up close, `setOffset`) is applied. HTML pins sit ~14 px below their point (see above): `sonder()` measures that once on a hidden `button.repere` (again after a resize or a font load), so drawn pins land where HTML ones do (centroids within 0.4 device px). A click hits the head, the tip or the heart; one on an HTML element under the canvas (a legend, a name: `dessous`) still goes to the drawn pin above it. Checked: `essai_epingles.py` (day, night, favourites, a season, without Halloween, up close, phone; on a flat background, so only pins show), `gestes_epingles.py` (hover, click, a drag from a pin, zooms through dots, pins and models, Tab, resize, a phone tap), `essai_epingles2.py` (route, game, the panel, a visitor without developer mode). Measured at ÷4 (`moyen.py`, alternating runs): mid-distance 19–20 → 33 fps (processor 48 → 29 ms per frame), up close 22–29 → 32–33, far away as before (`passage.py`: zooming from dots to pins, the worst frames 211–261 → 167–195 ms). Tried and dropped the same day: keeping only the markers near the screen (a projected test instead of the lng/lat bounds: with a margin safe for fast flings, only ~10 % fewer markers, no measurable gain), and `will-change: auto` on markers (the GPU process then re-rasterised them, 10 → 37 ms of its CPU per frame, the main thread no faster).
- **The panel's processor time** (2026-10-24): while "Fluidité" is open, `qualite.js` wraps `map._render` and posts a message at its end; the message runs after the browser's style, layout and paint of that frame, so its delay is the main thread's work per moving frame (`qualiteCpu`: processor ms, and the map's own JS inside). It also says when ≥ 70 % of the frame gaps are 30–37 ms (`qualitePlafond`: the browser's 30 fps cap in energy saver).
- **Also for every device** (2026-10-19): the far view no longer redraws non-stop at rest (`corrigerPlacement`, see Map), and `allegerCorrespondances` (`app.js`) wraps MapLibre's `TerrainTileManager._getTerrainCoordsForRegularTile` so it only sees the terrain tiles related to the tile it looks for (MapLibre allocated a matrix for every pair first): 573 → 226 ms in a 16 s route at CPU ÷3, same result. Re-check on upgrade.
- **No layout reads while drawing** (2026-10-24). Up close at night, the 3D models' glow (`couche3d.js`) read `map.getCanvas().clientWidth` in its `render`, every frame: the markers had moved since the last frame, so it forced the browser's style recalculation and layout in the middle of the map's drawing, and the layout ran again later in the frame (at CPU ÷4: 2.3 of the map's ~15 ms of JS per frame; layout 3.6 → 1.4 ms per frame). `tailleCarte(map)` (`commun.js`) reads the size once, then again after each `resize`; the ships' lanterns (`mer.js`) use it too. Never read `clientWidth`, `getBoundingClientRect`… in a per-frame path. Seen in a CDP profile as self time in `get clientWidth` (`profil_moyen.py`, `appelants.py` in the session's `scratchpad/saccades`).
- **Start.** `niveauDeDepart()` (before the map is created: antialias and pixel ratio) reads `localStorage.qualite` = `{ niveau, impose, puce, echec }`. On a first visit it guesses from the WebGL renderer name (`WEBGL_debug_renderer_info`, read on a throwaway WebGL2 canvas, then `loseContext`), so an integrated chip gets no antialias from the first visit; `brancherQualite` (at the map's `load`) guesses again if the remembered `puce` differs. The guess: a software renderer (SwiftShader, llvmpipe, Microsoft Basic Render) → très légère; Intel (not Arc) or an AMD Radeon without "RX"/"Pro" (APUs) → légère; anything else (discrete cards, Apple, phones) → fine.
- **Measuring.** The interval between two `move` events is one frame of a movement (gestures, flights, the start-up turntable; a gap over 250 ms starts a new movement, or 1.5 s during a flight or drag). Every 90 intervals, `juger()`: a median over 25 ms (under 40 fps) or more than 12 % of frames over 50 ms, in two windows in a row (one if the median is over 50 ms), steps down one level (a toast says so in developer mode only). Regular 33 ms intervals (Chrome's energy saver caps at 30 fps) do not count unless the GPU is busy. Three windows in a row under 18 ms with under 2 % slow frames (and the GPU under 6 ms a frame when known) step up one level, once per visit and never after a step down; a level that failed right after a step up is not retried for 30 days (`echec`). Nothing is measured during the first 6 s (the first tiles), with the page hidden, or while a dive is filmed (`body.enregistre`).
- **GPU time.** When the browser has `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` (Chrome, Edge), `map.painter.render` is wrapped in a `TIME_ELAPSED` query (results read a few frames later, never `GPU_DISJOINT_EXT`, which can stall). It feeds the decisions above and the panel.
- **Sharp at rest** (asked the same day: "keep the quality and the smoothness"). `painter.render` is wrapped (`regler`, before every frame): while the map moves (`options.moving`, or a `move` event less than `REPOS` = 300 ms ago) it uses the level's moving settings; at rest, the rest ones. MapLibre 6.11 internals, re-check on upgrade:
  - **The pixel ratio** changes without `map.setPixelRatio`: that one goes through `resize()`, which fires `movestart`/`move`/`moveend` and, outside a camera animation, calls `stop()`, which ended a drag in progress. `finesse()` sets `map._overridePixelRatio` and calls `map._resizeCanvas` + `painter.resize` itself, at the start of the frame that is then drawn (no blank frame). In légère the rest ratio is 1.5 × the screen's (at most 2): the image scaled down to the screen has smooth edges, as fine's antialias gives (antialias can only be chosen at map creation).
  - **Terrain textures.** MapLibre keeps one texture per terrain tile, and its pool resizes a recycled texture to the requested size, so tiles can have different sizes. But MapLibre's `renderLayer` sets the drawing viewport to `renderToTexture.rttSize`, not to the texture's own size: so `painter.bindRTT` is wrapped to set `rttSize` to the bound texture's size (`regler` puts it back each frame). Without it (2026-10-19 → 21), a 2048 texture drawn with the 1536 or 1024 viewport of a movement kept an empty band and its relief shrunk into a corner. On a phone in légère at night, half the screen showed the page background, light against the night sea (the owner's screenshot). On a laptop up close, the relief was torn apart. `trous_nuit.py` (2026-10-21 session) counts these holes: the share of the screen in the page background's colour at night. `Tile.prototype.acquireRTT` is wrapped (`brancherTextures`, found from a terrain tile) so every texture gets `texturePour(tile)`: the moving size while the map moves; once it is still (`options.moving` false), `taillePour`, the tile's longest edge on screen (its corners projected at sea level with `transform._coordinateClipPoint`/`_pixelMatrix`) × the rest pixel ratio ÷ 1.25, rounded up to 1024/1536/2048, within the level's moving and rest sizes (a far tile shows small: 2048 there would be 4× the work for nothing). Textures drawn while moving are kept; once still, `affinerUneTuile` releases the nearest tile whose textures are smaller than wanted (MapLibre redraws it in that frame), at most one every `ENTRE_AFFINAGES` (40 ms). `painter.acquireRTT` is wrapped too so the pool hands out a texture already of the right size when it has one.
  - **All at once (2026-10-20).** The first version showed each upgraded texture as soon as it was redrawn: the owner saw the relief "load" square by square after every move (measured: blocks of the screen still changing 1.0 and 1.2 s after a drag in légère, none in fine). Drawing near tiles at their rest size while moving avoided it but made a zoom flight's textures 2048 (frames up to 24 ms on this GPU, like fine): rejected. Now, while the map is still, `Tile.prototype.releaseRTT` keeps the tile's old textures aside (`anciennes`) instead of returning them to the pool, and `RenderToTexture.getTexture` (read only by `drawTerrain`) shows them while the new ones are drawn; `apres()` (after each frame) marks them ready when a frame drew no texture, nothing is left to upgrade and MapLibre wants no follow-up frame, and `regler` swaps them all in the frame where the pixel ratio switches (`REPOS`), or right away if already at rest (`montrerTout`, which returns the old ones to the pool). Moving again swaps at once; after 4 s it swaps anyway. A `move` event in the last 100 ms counts as moving, like `options.moving`. The start-up turntable (`setBearing` every frame) does not set `options.moving`. Before 2026-10-21 its textures were therefore held until the 4 s limit, and the day/night switch changed the map seconds after the tap (the owner saw it). This also hides MapLibre's own one-tile-per-frame refreshes at rest (a DEM tile arriving, the zoom follow-up). Measured with `vague.py` (full screenshots every ~0.2 s after a drag or a wheel zoom, change per 48 px block): every block changes in a single capture, at the switch; `tampon_suivi.py` shows the textures drawn one by one and shown together, in the same frame as the pixel ratio.
  - Measured (laptop context, this PC's GPU, `net.py`): légère's moving frames cost the same as before (0.10–0.22 ms, fine 0.29–0.45), textures 1024 while moving; a frame at rest at ×1.875 costs what fine's does (0.13–0.30 ms); one texture redone at 2048 costs ~1.7 ms here (1024: ~0.3–0.6), maybe ~70 ms on an Iris Xe, hence the spacing. After a zoom to 12, about 14 textures go to 2048 and 10 to 1536, the far ones stay at 1024. Magnified, légère at rest is hard to tell from fine. A drag keeps going through the switch (same end point), and no `movestart`/`moveend` fires at rest.
- **Tile downloads while moving** (2026-10-20). MapLibre's image queue allows `config.MAX_PARALLEL_IMAGE_REQUESTS` (16) at rest but only `MAX_PARALLEL_IMAGE_REQUESTS_PER_FRAME` (8) while `map.isMoving()` (its throttle control). In a zoom route on the owner's screen, tiles waited up to 1.3 s in that queue (p90 1276 ms); with 16: p90 35 ms, sharp tiles 85 % → 92 % of the time, same frame times on this PC. In a phone context at CPU ÷4, 16 gave more frames over 25 ms (58 → 85), so phones and the lighter levels keep 8. Set through the `telechargements` tool (`app.js`, `maplibregl.config`).
- **Applying** (`appliquer`): the pixel ratio and textures follow at the next frame (`regler`; `map.setPixelRatio` only if the internals are missing); the mesh through `changerMaillage` (`app.js`); the download queue through `telechargements`; the sea through `reglerMer({ imagesParSeconde, ressac, bateaux })` (`mer.js`). `plein(true)` puts everything back to fine while the dive is filmed (`enregistrement.js`, which sets its own pixel ratio) and while the trip image is captured (`partage-voyage.js`), via the `pleineQualite` tool, and stops measuring.
- **Developer mode**: the gauge button above `</>` (`.btn-qualite`, created by `qualite.js`, red ring when a level is forced) opens "Fluidité": the GPU name, frames per second while moving, GPU ms per frame and processor ms per frame (refreshed every second; see The panel's processor time), and Auto / Fine / Légère / Très légère, plus the pixel ratio while moving and at rest when they differ (`qualiteRepos`). Forcing a level is remembered (`impose`); the antialias changes at the next load. Texts `qualite*`.
- **On the owner's devices** (2026-10-19, the panel's readings): a laptop with Intel Iris Xe: fine 26 fps (GPU 22.4 ms a frame), légère 56 fps (7.2 ms), très légère 60 fps (4.2 ms); a phone with Adreno 830: 119 fps at every level (no GPU timer in Android Chrome); this PC: 179 fps (0.9 ms). The first version judged during the first tiles and took the laptop down to très légère: hence the 6 s wait and the two windows. From très légère it climbs back to légère on a later visit (three smooth windows, GPU under 6 ms), and stays there (7.2 ms is not under 6).
- Tests: the 2026-10-19 session's `essai_qualite.py` (forced levels, a CPU-×10 run that steps down, a reload that drops antialias), `mesure.py q-fine q-legere q-tres-legere` (GPU ms per frame; it removes the wrapper of `painter.render`, so it no longer sees the rest switch) and `net.py fine legere` (per phase of a route: pixel ratios, texture sizes made, GPU ms, a mouse drag, the events at rest; it hides the timer extension from `qualite.js` with an init script, since two timer queries cannot nest). In Playwright, never resolve a promise with a MapLibre event (`once('moveend', r)`): Playwright serialises the event and its map, which froze the page for a minute and then crashed it.

### UI

- The categories menu is `construireMenu`. Each row has a checkbox (show/hide on the map) and a button that unfolds the list of its places. Unfolded state is kept on the category object (`c.deplie`), because the menu is rebuilt on every language change and filter change.
- Search results and sub-list items both go through `allerAuLieu()`.
- The place card (`ouvrirLieu` / `remplirFiche`) is a side panel on desktop and a bottom sheet on phones. Closed, it is `inert` (its buttons, off screen, are not reachable with Tab); `ouvrirLieu`, `villes.ouvrir` and `fermerFiche` toggle it.
  - On phones, its handle can be dragged (`brancherPoignee`). The sheet has three states: normal (`64dvh`), `.agrandie`, and closed. `paddingFiche()` keeps the place in view: it accounts for the card, and for the categories menu when that is open on desktop.
- The card's picture (`couverture(l)`, also the tour's) is the Sheet photo, else the TikTok cover copied into the site (`photos/tiktok/<video id>.jpg`, see `telecharger_couvertures.py`), else the TikTok oEmbed thumbnail (a place added since the last nightly run; empty since 2026-10-20, see `couvertures_montage.py`). The video is the TikTok `player/v1/{id}` iframe, loaded on demand.
  - **iPhone: the card's scroll length (2026-10-22).** A friend of the owner opened the map from Snapchat on an iPhone: once the video played, the description bounced back out of reach, as if the card ended there. The video (9:16, up to 70 dvh) is about 300 px taller than the photo (16:10) it replaces, and WebKit kept the old scrollable length. `lancerVideo` calls `recompterDefilement()` (`overflow-y: hidden` on `#fiche-defil` for two frames, then back, keeping `scrollTop`), again at the iframe's `load`. Checked in Edge (`fiche_video.py`, 2026-10-22 session: 807 → 1103 px, the end reachable); not testable on iOS from this PC.
- All UI strings are in `TEXTES` (en/fr/ja) in `config.js`. `config.js` is also the only settings file meant for hand editing.
  - The page's fixed texts say their key in `index.html`: `data-t` (the text), `data-t-titre` (the `title`), `data-t-aria` (the `aria-label`). `appliquerLangue` fills them with three loops; only texts that depend on a state are set in code. Give a new fixed text one of these attributes rather than a line in `appliquerLangue`.
- **Panels.** One open at a time: `PANNEAUX` (`app.js`) lists each panel with its button and its `ouvrirX(oui)` function. Opening one calls `fermerPanneaux(sauf)`; a tap elsewhere or Escape closes it; the tour, the game and a phone flight to a place close them all. A new panel goes in `PANNEAUX`.
  - **Every panel has a × at its top right** (asked 2026-10-21: on some, the owner did not know how to close them). `brancherBoutons` inserts `croixPanneau(t('fermer'))` (`commun.js`, class `.panneau-fermer`: floated right so the text flows beside it, `position: sticky` so it stays at the top while the panel scrolls) into each `PANNEAUX` panel except the phone menu (its own × is in its header). Several panels are refilled with `innerHTML`, so a `MutationObserver` puts the × back each time; never test a panel's emptiness with `firstChild` (`musique.js` checks `.musique-lecteur` instead). The developer panels (Fluidité in `qualite.js`, the date setting in `ambiance.js`) put it in their own template.
  - **Nothing drawn on the map goes over the interface**: `#carte` has `z-index: 0`, so map content (pins, popups, the parade…) stays under the buttons and panels whatever its own z-index.
- **The phone layout (2026-10-21).** The owner found the phone map cluttered (three rows of buttons, the clock mid-screen, about a third of the screen covered) and chose, from mockups, a full-screen layout with one central button (≤ 720 px, `style.css`; the desktop is unchanged):
  - **Top.** Top-left: the logo alone as a round medallion (`.entete`, still the TikTok link), then the clock as a small chip (`.heure-japon` taken out of `.controles` with `position: fixed`; the time only below 381 px).
  - **Top-right: two shortcuts.** `#btn-favoris` stays visible even with no favourite (`voirBoutonFavoris`, with a red count badge) and `#btn-halloween` shows while the event is on. Every other button of `.filtres-boutons` is hidden. The route, month and near-me pills (`.resume-itineraire`) are centred under the top row, stacked when several show.
  - **Bottom: `.barre-mobile`.** Menu, a big red « Au hasard » that rolls the dice in one tap (`lancerDe`, with the hasard panel's filters), and Chercher (opens the Categories menu with the search focused). It is hidden during a tour, the game, film mode and a tour video.
  - **The menu.** `#panneau-menu` (a `PANNEAUX` entry, `ouvrirMenuMobile`) holds tiles for every feature (Categories, Random by region, Tour, When to go, Legends, Favourites, Halloween while it runs, and the game and challenge with class `dev`), then Music and the language (its own `[data-langue]` buttons). A tile only clicks the real, hidden button (`data-ouvre`), so all their logic is reused; counts and the Halloween tile are copied from those buttons when the menu opens.
  - **Panels** become sheets rising from the bottom, full width, at most 82 dvh (`.filtres > .menu`, `.musique > .menu`). The Categories menu drops from the top instead, so the keyboard never hides its search field.
  - **Gone from the phone screen.** The language switch, the music button (it comes back, red, under the shortcuts while music plays) and the title scroll (with Halloween's cat).
  - **Moved.** The sources' « i » sits at the bottom-left corner beside the bottom bar; opened, the sources show in a bubble above the bar (on one line at the bottom during a tour, film or game). The hint, toasts, the received-trip banner and the far-from-Japan card sit above the bar too. At 350 px and less the bottom bar is tighter, clear of the right-hand buttons.
  - **Language.** It still follows the browser until a language is picked (`localStorage.langue`), now from the first supported entry of `navigator.languages`.
  - Mockups: the Design artifact « Carte mobile : 3 interfaces »; checks: the 2026-10-21 session's `essai_mobile.py` and `essai_panneaux.py` (390 and 320 px, day and night, developer mode, every panel, the desktop unchanged).
- **Shared helpers** (`site/commun.js`): `echapper` (text into HTML), `tactile()`, `partagerFichier(fichier, texte)` (the system share sheet on touch screens; false means download it with `telecharger`), `envoyer({ titre, texte, url }, { message, copie })` (share sheet, else copied to the clipboard), `retirerParametres(...noms)` (reads `?voyage=`, `?dev=`… and removes them from the address bar), `kmEntre(a, b)` and `cap(a, b)` (great-circle distance and initial bearing between `{ lng, lat }`), `jourDuDefi(debut)` and `debutDuJour(debut, jour)` (the daily challenge's day, counted in Japan time), `typoFr(s)` (French typography: the space before ! ? : ; and inside « » becomes non-breaking; `t()` and `enLangue()` in `app.js`, and the own text helpers of `halloween.js` and `enregistrement.js`, run every French text through it, so texts are written with a normal space). Use them instead of new copies.

### Visual design ("vieille carte", the antique relief map)

The owner chose this world on 2026-09-30, from three reference images. It replaced the "Estampe" print world of 2026-09-29. **`DESIGN.md` is the design system**: read it before any visual change. `PRODUCT.md` holds the product context. `.impeccable/brief-carte.md` holds the direction contract; it is dev-only and must never be copied into `site/`.

The redesigns were done with the Impeccable skill (`~/.claude/skills/impeccable`, installed without its binary launcher or hooks). The key mechanics:
- **Palette.** Map inks live in `app.js` and in `CONFIG.couleurs`: the sepia relief ramp, the umber hillshade, the sky, horizon and haze, and the sea. UI tokens are CSS vars in `style.css`: `--papier*`, `--encre*`, `--trait`, `--rouge`, `--sarcelle`.
- **Sea.** The relief's sea stops are **transparent** (the float16 margin rule below still applies). The sea colour is the `background` layer, and three sets of line layers show through from under the relief: `cote-ombre` (the islands' shadow) and `lignes-eau-1..3` (engraved water-lines, drawn with `line-gap-width`), both replaced by the swell layer of `mer.js` once it is ready (see Living sea), and `cote-encre` (the coast ink, over the relief).
  - Their source is `site/data/cote-japon.geojson`, built by `outils/fabriquer_cote.py` from Natural Earth. It covers Japan's coasts, with the Northern Territories and Takeshima (`japonais()`).
  - The Natural Earth coast is too coarse to match the relief up close, so these layers fade out between zoom 6.5 and 8.5.
- **Lakes and rivers.** The owner asked for them on 2026-10-01, in the sea colour. They come from `eaux-japon.geojson` (source `eaux`, see `fabriquer_eaux.py`), drawn over the relief and under `cote-encre`:
  - `rivieres-bord` (water-line #3d6b64) under `rivieres` (sea colour) make a turquoise line with a fine dark edge. The width grows with the zoom and with `km` (the river's extent). Rivers shorter than 100 km fade in between zoom 4.5 and 6.5, so the far view only shows the big ones.
  - `lacs` (sea-colour fill) and `lacs-bord` (water-line edge) are drawn after the rivers, so rivers end cleanly in the lakes.
  - The OSM shapes are accurate (simplified to about 60 m), so unlike the coast layers they stay at every zoom.
- **Regions and prefectures.** The owner asked on 2026-10-01 for every region to be outlined and named when zooming in. Borders come from `frontieres-japon.geojson` (source `frontieres`, see `fabriquer_frontieres.py`) and are drawn over the relief, under the rivers:
  - Between two big regions: `lavis-g` and `lavis-d`, a blurred watercolour band on each side of the line in that region's colour (`LAVIS_REGIONS` in `app.js`, placed with `line-offset`), under `frontieres-regions`, a dash-dot sepia line. They show at every zoom.
  - Between two prefectures: `frontieres-prefectures`, short sepia dashes over `frontieres-prefectures-fond`, a pale paper underlay that keeps them readable in the mountains' shade. They fade in between zoom 5.5 and 6.5. (Dots were too faint on the ridges.)
  - Hokkaidō, Shikoku, Kyūshū and Okinawa have no land border with another region: the sea outlines them.
  - Names are HTML markers in `site/noms-regions.js`, pitched with the map like the sea names. Region names (Zen Antique spaced capitals; Kyūshū and Okinawa are written separately) show between zoom 5.5 and 7.4, then prefecture names (IM Fell italic, Zen Antique in Japanese; French uses the English name, as the dice does) between 7.4 and 10.5.
  - The text is in an inner `<span>`, because MapLibre sets the marker element's own opacity. The markers are inserted just before `#papier`, so the aged paper lies over them and the legends and pins stay in front.
- **No masks since 2026-10-21.** Two fill layers in the sea colour used to hide the neighbours: `voisins` (their land) and `large` (everything more than 70 km from Japan). Their mountains still stood in 3D under the paint, and the whole Kuril chain showed. The relief itself is now flattened outside the Japan zone (see Map).
  - The second mask exists because Natural Earth misses thousands of tiny foreign islets, mostly off Korea. From above they showed as sub-pixel sand specks that twinkled whenever the map moved.
- **Aged paper.** `#papier` (grain, foxing and vignette: one static background with SVG turbulence, normal alpha) is moved by `app.js` into the map's canvas container, right after the canvas. It therefore sits over the relief and under the markers.
  - Do not use `mix-blend-mode` or `backdrop-filter` there. Both are recomputed every frame.
- **Ornaments.**
  - Sea names are HTML markers in `MERS` (`app.js`), with `pitchAlignment: 'map'` and `rotationAlignment: 'viewport'`. They hide below the `.loin` threshold. Their positions were picked so they fit on the phone start view.
  - The compass rose `#btn-rose` rotates with `-bearing` on every `rotate` event; a tap eases north up. The owner asked on 2026-10-09 to turn the map with it: `brancherRose` lets a mouse or finger grab it (pointer capture, 4px threshold) and the map's bearing follows the pointer's angle around the rose's centre, so the N stays under the pointer (`setBearing` with the `originalEvent`, so a tour pauses as for any gesture). Released within 7° of north it snaps north; the click after a drag is swallowed. ← → turn it by 15° from the keyboard.
  - The title scroll `.bandeau` (Japan · 日本 · Japon, inline SVG) sits bottom-left. The attribution control is shifted to its right, and wraps on phones.
- **Category colours.** They come from the Sheet and are aged toward sepia in CSS with `color-mix(in oklab, var(--c) 78%, #4a3521)`, both for markers and for `.pastille`.
- **Far view.** Below zoom `ZOOM_POINTS` (6.2), `majSelonZoom` adds `.loin` on the map container. Markers then turn into 11px dots so the relief shows, and the sea names show.
- **Place card.**
  - The Japanese name is a vertical `.cartouche` over the photo. It is appended to `#fiche-media`, so it disappears when the video plays.
  - Category · prefecture sits under the title. The prefecture fills in asynchronously via `preparerPrefectures()`.
  - Share is an icon button on the photo (`#fiche-partager`), and "More info" is a link after the description (`#fiche-plus`).
- **First-visit hint.** `#aide` shows `aideTel` on phones and `aide` on desktop. It is shown once: a `localStorage` flag (`aideVue`) is set when it hides.
- **Phone camera.** The start-up camera is rotated (`orientation: 38`) so Japan stands upright on the tall screen.
- **Fonts.**
  - Zen Antique sets names and titles.
  - IM Fell English (italic) sets map lettering only.
  - Noto Sans sets everything else.
  - The Google Fonts stylesheet (119 KB compressed, mostly Noto Sans JP's 372 `@font-face` slices) is loaded with `media="print"` switched to `all` on load: a blocking stylesheet also held back the module scripts, so the map started later.

### 3D place models (`site/couche3d.js`, `site/modeles3d.js`)

When the map is zoomed in, every place shows a small low-poly model of its category's **icon**, standing straight on the relief. The HTML marker floats just above its model.
- **The look (2026-10-05).** The owner found the first models (on a round base in the category colour) poor on video, and asked for no base, more detail and the site's art direction. They approved a pilot of 4 models, then all 30 were redone the same way:
  - **No base.** The ground is y = 0. Each model brings its own patch of ground (`parcelle`: grass, sand, gravel, water, with an irregular `bosses` outline) and its foundations go down to about −0.2, so it never floats on a slope: the relief hides what is below.
  - **Ink outline**, like the legend vignettes: an inverted hull. `matiereContour(THREE)` returns a sepia `BackSide` material whose shader pushes each vertex along its smoothed normal (the `contour` attribute, computed per piece in `fin()`). `couche3d.js` draws it as a second `InstancedMesh` per model that shares the model's `instanceMatrix`, and sets `epaisseur` every frame for about `TRAIT` (1.4) px on screen. Flat pieces laid on another surface (water lines, foam) are built with `trait: false`.
  - **Watercolour painting.** Colours come from the palette `C` (faded washes from the vignettes). `fin()` gives each triangle a small random wash (`nuance`) and darkens the bottom of the model (`0.74 + 0.26 × lisse(−0.06, 0.32, y)`), as if it stood in its own shadow. Water gets fine light lines that follow the shore (`lignesEau`, `ruban`), like the map's engraved water-lines; a per-triangle stripe pattern looked like a mosaic.
  - **The workshop.** `ateliers(THREE)` returns `atelier()`, a chainable builder: primitives (`boite`, `cylindre`, `cone`, `boule`, `toit`…), `piece(geo, c, { deformer, teinte, couleurs, trait, nuance })`, and Japanese parts reused across models: `toitJapon` (curved roof, upturned corners, tile rows; `avant` colours the edge band between the top and the underside, `bord` by default; since 2026-10-25 its flat top is closed underneath too, as seen from below at the walk's Diamond Gate the sky showed through it), `ishigaki` (castle stone base), `cedre`, `pinJapon`, `erable`, `lanterne`, `shimenawa`, `chochin`, `baton`, `tube`.
- **Model set.** `modeles3d.js` builds every model from three.js primitives (no asset files), one per icon name in `ICONES`.
  - `ALIAS` maps `camera` to `viewpoint`, and `star` and `pin` to `stele`.
  - Emojis and unknown icons get `stele`.
  - Each model is about 1 unit tall, fits in a radius-0.5 disc, and is merged into one vertex-coloured geometry. The 30 models make about 77,000 triangles.
  - When you add an icon, add its model too (otherwise it shows the stele). `site/modeles.html` is the owner-facing gallery and the quickest visual check.
  - **Building them without freezing the map.** `fabriquerPeuAPeu(THREE)` is a generator that yields after each model; `sansFiger` (`couche3d.js`) runs it in slices of about 6 ms between frames. Built in one go they took about 80 ms on desktop, a third of a second on a mid-range phone. `fabriquerModeles` builds them all at once, for the gallery.
- **Loading.** three.js (pinned `0.186.1`, jsDelivr ESM, `URL_THREE` exported by `couche3d.js`) is dynamically imported, so start-up is unchanged. `mer.js` loads it about 1.5 s after the map is up, for the ships. Otherwise it loads the first time zoom reaches 7, or when `modeles3d.preparer()` is called (before a dive, which also compiles the shaders). If it fails to load, the map just has no models or ships.
- **Rendering.** One MapLibre custom layer, `modeles-3d` (`renderingMode: '3d'`), shares MapLibre's GL context and depth buffer, so terrain hides models behind mountains.
  - There are two `InstancedMesh`es per model in use, the model and its ink outline (`nouveauType`): about 45 draw calls.
  - Instance matrices are rebuilt every frame, only for places inside the view bounds, relative to the map centre (relative-to-centre, so there is no float32 jitter at zoom 16). The projection is `defaultProjectionData.mainMatrix × translate(centre)`.
  - The ground elevation is `map.queryTerrainElevation()` (exaggeration included). It is cached per place until the exaggeration changes or a `relief` tile arrives.
- **Pitfalls.**
  - The model-to-map basis is deliberately a **mirror**: (x, y, z) → (x, z, y). With a proper rotation, the faces rendered inside-out, showing back faces only (dark models).
  - The models' directional light follows the camera, coming from the viewer's upper left. A fixed north-west light (like the hillshade) left every model backlit, because the camera usually looks north.
- **Size.** Models appear between zoom 8.6 and 9.6 (they grow out of the ground). Their on-screen height is `62px × 2^((z − 10.5) / 2)`, capped at 170px.
- **Marker lift.** `majLevee` (`couche3d.js`) computes the screen height of a size-1 model × sin(pitch), in 4px steps. When it changes, each place marker gets `setOffset([0, −lift × (model height + 0.08)])`. MapLibre repositions markers every frame anyway, so this costs no restyle. The earlier `--leve` CSS variable on `#carte` restyled the whole map on every step and was the main cause of phone stutter.
- **Clicks.** A tap on a model opens its place (`lieuSous()`, a screen-box test from the drawn places).
- **Antialiasing.** It is enabled only when `devicePixelRatio < 2` (`canvasContextAttributes`). Phones don't need it and it costs GPU time.

### Living sea (`site/mer.js`)

In the far view (below `ZOOM_CALME` = 7.2) the sea comes alive. The owner settled the parts on 2026-10-01: the coastal swell, a few 3D Edo ships far out at sea, a whale and a sea serpent. On 2026-10-08 they added a typhoon every 15 minutes.
- **What was dropped.** The owner first chose engraved wavelets and ships on fixed coastal routes. After seeing them live, they found it too busy: the map was harder to read. Keep the sea sparse and keep ships away from the coasts.
- **Hiding when zoomed in.** At 7.2 and above, the container gets `.mer-calme`: every `.vie-marine` element gets `display: none` and the ship layer draws nothing. The clock keeps running (since 2026-10-09) for the swell and the surf, but moves no ship and wakes no creature or typhoon.
- **Swell.** The fixed `cote-ombre` and `lignes-eau-1..3` layers are hidden once the swell is ready (`DESSINS_FIXES`), and a custom layer, `houle`, redraws them: lines rolling in toward the coasts, over the islands' shadow.
  - **The islands' shadow (2026-10-12).** The owner disliked the triangles around the islands. `cote-ombre` is a blurred 10–20px line along the Natural Earth coast; around islets narrower than the line, and in tight bends, MapLibre's join extrusions cross over and stick out as spikes (round joins do not help: the inner side of each turn still overshoots). The shader now draws the shadow from the distance image: the distance at a point `OMBRE_ILES.decalage` ([6, 7] map px, south-east: the light comes from the north-west) back, `1 − smoothstep(0, rayon)` (7 px at zoom 4 to 14 at 7), opacity 0.52, fading out with the swell (6.5 → 8.5), only where the image says sea (low coastal plains are not hidden by the relief). Its colour is `TEINTES.ombre` (`ombreIles` of the palette). `cote-ombre` stays as the fallback.
  - The custom layer is raw WebGL2: one quad over `BORNES`, plus a fragment shader that reads `data/distance-cote.png`. That image encodes the distance as `d = (v/255)² × 160` screen px at zoom 5, in Mercator, from Japan's coast only, so the neighbours' former coasts get no swell.
  - Its GL resources are created in the first `render()`, which MapLibre wraps with `setDirty()`. The layer turns off `POLYGON_OFFSET_FILL` itself, because MapLibre does not track it.
  - It sits slightly above sea level with a polygon offset, so the relief hides it on land and behind mountains.
  - If WebGL2 or the image is missing, the fixed lines and shadow simply stay.
- **Why not animate the line layers?** With terrain, MapLibre bakes every line, fill, hillshade and color-relief layer into per-tile textures. Their cache key ignores paint values, and `setPaintProperty` fires a style event that re-bakes every tile. Changing paint every frame would re-render the whole relief every frame. A custom layer only needs `triggerRepaint()`, which redraws the cached textures.
- **The clock.** One `requestAnimationFrame` loop runs at 15 fps. It moves the ships, schedules the creatures (both only in the far view) and calls `triggerRepaint()` while the swell, a ship or the surf is on screen (`ressac.visible()`: the last frame drew surf). It does nothing while the page is hidden.
- **Surf (`site/ressac.js`, `site/ressac-calcul.js`, 2026-10-09).** The owner noticed that up close the waves stopped (the clock stopped at 7.2) and then disappeared, and asked for waves breaking on the shore at every zoom. The swell's Natural Earth distance image is far too coarse up close, so the surf takes the coast from the relief itself: Mapterhorn's sea is exactly 0 m (lossless WebP).
  - **The distance field.** A worker (`ressac-calcul.js`) takes a DEM tile and its 8 neighbours (fetched again: they come from the HTTP cache), decodes them (`createImageBitmap` + `OffscreenCanvas`, no colour conversion), marks land as the map's palette does (≥ 0.6 m or ≤ −1.2 m), runs an exact Euclidean distance transform (Felzenszwalb) over the tile plus a `PORTEE` (64 px) band of its neighbours, and returns the tile as an R8 field (0 = land, 255 = 64 px or more). The neighbours avoid broken waves along tile edges. All-land or all-far tiles come back `vide`. About 100 ms per tile the first time; masks are cached (48).
  - **Which tiles.** `map.coveringTiles({ tileSize: 1024, minzoom: 7, maxzoom: 12 })`: one level below the view, so a field pixel is 2 to 4 screen pixels (12 is the DEM's maxzoom: the map enlarges those beyond). Only tiles within `PRES_DU_JAPON` (7 px at zoom 5, ≈ 14 km) of Japan's coast per `distance-cote.png`; the shader fades the rest out too, because the neighbours' coasts are in the DEM but hidden by the masks. While a tile computes, a ready parent (up to 3 levels) is drawn on its part. At most 3 calculations at once, 2 texture uploads per frame, 48 textures kept.
  - **Drawing.** Custom layer `ressac` (WebGL2), added just after `houle`, one quad per tile, with the tile's matrix computed in double precision from `defaultProjectionData.mainMatrix` (no jitter at zoom 16) and the same height and polygon offset as the swell.
