Only the code's SHA-256 is in the site (`CONFIG.devCondense`); the link is written nowhere in the repo. The page removes `?dev=` from the address bar. The browser then remembers it (`localStorage.modeDev`: `oui` = developer map, `non` = normal map), and `body.dev-permis` shows the `</>` button above the compass (`#btn-dev`, red when active). One click switches (`basculerDev`). Leaving developer mode stops a running game or daily challenge and goes back to the Tour tab.
- It is hidden, not locked: the site's code is public, and anyone could set the localStorage key. Nothing private is behind it.
- **A new link.** Make a new random code (for example `python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(12))"`), put its SHA-256 hex in `devCondense`, and give the link to the owner in chat, never in a repo file. Browsers that already have it keep it.
- The owner must open the link once per browser: phone browser, computer, and not TikTok's in-app browser (its storage is separate).

### Guess-the-place game (`site/jeu.js`, not public yet)

The owner asked on 2026-10-02 for a "Guess the place" game, to be coded now but **released next month** with a TikTok. Until then the `#btn-jeu` button exists only in developer mode (class `dev`; it used to need `?jeu` in the address). `jeu.js` is loaded only on the first click (`lancerJeu`). To release it, remove the `dev` class from `#btn-jeu`.
- **A game.** It has 5 rounds (`choisirLieux`: random places at least 80 km apart, each with a photo). The next round's photo is downloaded and decoded ahead.
- **Photos (2026-10-04).** The first version blurred the name band of the TikTok cover; the owner found you could see nothing. Each round now shows a clean photo of the place: the Sheet photo if any (61 places, no text on them), otherwise a free photo from Wikimedia Commons. The owner suggested Google Images; those photos belong to their authors, so Commons it is, with credit.
  - `outils/photos_jeu.py` copies them into `site/photos/jeu/<video id>.jpg` (960 px max) and writes `site/data/photos-jeu.json` (`{video id: {fichier, auteur, licence, lien}}`). `CHOIX` holds 58 photos picked by hand from contact sheets of candidates (Wikidata P18, Commons geosearch around the GPS point, Commons search by name): the right place, recognisable, no readable place name (the Ōnaruto view with a "Magosaki Cape" sign was swapped). For a place not in `CHOIX` it takes the Wikidata item's main image (P18), found by the Japanese then English name, only within 3 km (20 km for mountains, lakes, islands, coasts, caves, viewpoints). It runs nightly after the covers. It only fetches what is missing, or a `CHOIX` entry that changed.
  - 10 places have no usable free photo (listed in a comment above `CHOIX`): they are left out of the game. A Sheet photo puts a place back in.
  - The photo is shown whole (`contain`) over a blurred, darkened copy of itself (`.jeu-fond`), so portrait photos get no black bars. When its shape is within about 22% of the frame's, it fills the frame instead (`photo-pleine`, `ajusterPhoto` on `load`).
  - The credit sits on the photo. While guessing it shows only "Photo Wikimedia Commons · licence"; the author and the link to the file page appear once the place is revealed, because an author name or a file name can give the place away (one author is 松山市役所, another 大分帰省中).
- **Timer.** Each round lasts `DUREE_MANCHE` (30 s, the owner's choice), counted from when the photo shows (at most `ATTENTE_IMAGE`, 5 s, later). `.jeu-chrono` in the header and a bar on top of the photo turn red in the last 10 s (`presse`), and the number beats in the last 5 (`urgent`). At zero, a placed pin counts; with no pin the round scores 0 ("Time's up!") and the camera flies to the place. It runs on `setInterval` (200 ms) against `performance.now()`, and keeps running if the page is hidden. `tic()` checks the end; `majChrono()` only draws, so `afficher()` can call it without revealing from inside itself.
- **Hints.** "Hint" shows the region, then "Another hint" the prefecture (`regionDe`, `PREFECTURES`; the place's prefecture comes from the `prefecture(l)` tool, `preparerPrefectures` in `app.js`). Each zone is tinted red on the map (`jeu-zone` fill + `jeu-zone-bord` line on a `data/prefectures.geojson` source, filtered by `code`, added once and refiltered) and framed by `cadrer`. The framing keeps the zone's big pieces (≥ 10% of its largest polygon) plus the place's own piece, so Tokyo's or Kagoshima's far islands do not shrink the view. A round keeps `PART_INDICES` of its points: 100%, 75% after one hint, 50% after two. Hokkaidō is both a region and a prefecture, so it gets only one hint.
- **Best scores.** `localStorage.jeuRecords` = `{ parties, meilleurs: [{ score, date }] }`, the 10 best. The welcome and end screens list the 5 best with their dates and the number of games. The game just played is highlighted, with "New record!" (when there was an earlier game) or "Your nth best score". The owner would like a shared leaderboard one day, but it needs accounts or a server: not started.
- **Camera.** `cadrer(bornes, m)` frames bounds in the space the card leaves and flies back to zero padding (the overview of all places, the hints). Always give `cameraForBounds` a real `maxZoom`: an explicit `undefined` overrides MapLibre's default and makes the camera `NaN` (an uncaught "Invalid LngLat"). A reveal without a pin uses `flyTo`, not `fitBounds` on a zero-size box.
- **Guess and reveal.** A tap on the map places a red drop pin (a marker through `reperes.suivre`, removed with `reperes.oublier`). Confirming re-shows the true place's marker, with `.actif` so its name label shows even as a far-view dot, and its 3D model. A dashed red line (source/layer `jeu-trait`) joins the two. `fitBounds` frames both.
- **Scoring.** Points are `round(1000 × e^(−km/250) × the part kept after hints)`, with verdicts at 10, 50 and 150 km. The final titles depend on the share of 5000 (≥90%, 70%, 40%). Sharing uses `navigator.share` on touch devices and the clipboard elsewhere. The text links to `https://<adresse>/?jeu`.
- **During a game** (`body.en-jeu`), every place marker is hidden through `reperes.montrer`, and models through `modeles3d.filtrer(fn)` (a new `couche3d.js` hook). The filter buttons, title scroll and hint hide; on phones the header hides too.
- **Placement.** The card sits top-left on desktop and at the top on phones. On phones the main button is fixed at the bottom, just above the attribution's line (at the very bottom during the game) and left of the rose. On 2026-10-22 the owner found too little map left under the card during a round, the friends' part of the welcome hidden below the fold, and the button too high: on phones the photo is 16 dvh during a round and its reveal (the card ends at about a third of the screen), the welcome card may grow to the button and shows « Play with friends » before the best scores (flex `order`), and the button sits at `--bas` + 30 px (it was + 86). Check: the 2026-10-22 session's `jeu_mobile.py` (390 × 844 and 360 × 740: welcome, round, pin, reveal; the boxes of the card, the form and the button). Each round's overview fits all places with `cameraForBounds`, corrected for the current padding as in `visite.js`.

### Multiplayer and friends (`site/jeu.js`, `site/amis.js`, `site/reseau.js`, `serveur/`)

On 2026-10-09 the owner asked for multiplayer with a code everyone enters and that is easy to share, and for adding friends without an account. From a mockup they chose a **Cloudflare** server (free plan, no card; the other options were Firebase, capped at 100 connections, and serverless WebRTC, which fails on some 4G networks), and that **anyone with an invitation link can join** even though the game button stays developer-only.
- **The server** (`serveur/src/index.js`, deployed as the Worker `carte-jeu`): WebSockets in hibernation (an idle connection costs nothing; `ping` gets an automatic `pong`). It accepts only the site's origins and localhost:8123 (`ORIGINES`). `/salon/<CODE>?joueur=<id>` goes to a **`Salon`** Durable Object per code, `/annuaire?joueur=<id>` to the single **`Annuaire`**. Both are SQLite-backed (the free plan's only kind). Since 2026-10-19 a third one, the single **`Defis`** (migration `v2`), answers plain HTTP for the daily challenge: `GET`/`POST /defi/YYYY-MM-DD` (the date in Japan; only yesterday, today and tomorrow are accepted), table `resultats (jour, joueur, essais, trouve, quand)` with the first result per player kept (`INSERT OR IGNORE`), 60 days kept; called through RPC (`stats`, `noter`), with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` set to the allowed origin. Tests: `node` scripts with two WebSocket clients (see the 2026-10-09 session), and two Playwright contexts (desktop + phone) for the whole flow.
- **A room (`Salon`) is the referee.** It keeps the players (join order, colour from `COULEURS`, total), the host (the creator; while disconnected, the first connected player acts as host), the phase (`salon`, `devine`, `revele`, `fin`), the 5 places sent by the host (`{id, lng, lat}`: places live in the Sheet, the server only gets their slug and coordinates), and each round's start and end times. A round starts `DECOMPTE` (3.5 s, the client shows 3·2·1 while photos load) or `ENTRE_MANCHES` (1.2 s) after the order, and lasts 30 s; an alarm reveals at the end (+0.6 s grace), or as soon as every connected player has confirmed. Pins count when not confirmed (like solo). The server scores with the solo formula (×0.75 / ×0.5 with hints, sent by the client). Each change is broadcast as a personal `etat` message: other players' pins are hidden until the reveal. 10 players at most; a room idle for 2 h is deleted. A reconnecting player (same id) gets their seat, score and current pin back; a second connection with the same id closes the first (`remplace`). Players who left are dropped when a new game (rematch) starts.
- **Codes.** A Japanese word from `MOTS` + two digits (`FUJI42`, shown `FUJI-42`). The client picks one at random and the server answers `occupe` if that room is alive (the client retries). Typing accepts any case, spaces, dashes, or a pasted link. The invitation link is `https://<adresse>/?partie=FUJI42`: `app.js` reads it at start-up (`lireLiensJeu`, then removes it from the address bar) and opens the game in that room (asking for a name first if the browser has none). The room shows the code big, "Send the link" (`partagerTexte`: share sheet on touch devices, else copied) and a QR code (`qrcode-generator@1.4.4` from jsDelivr, loaded on tap, drawn as an ink-on-parchment SVG).
- **The client (`jeu.js`).** The welcome screen has "Play solo" plus a "Play with friends" form (name, Create a game, a code + Join, My friends). `etat.multi` holds the connection (`reseau.js`: reconnects with backoff, and at once when the page becomes visible or the network returns), the last server state and the server clock offset (the least delayed `maintenant − Date.now()`). Server times are turned into `performance.now()` times for the countdown and the timer. At the reveal every player's pin is drawn in their colour with their name, a dashed line in that colour to the place, and the camera frames them with `cadrer` (constant pitch: `cameraForBounds` computes at the current pitch, and the solo reveal's pitch-40 `fitBounds` left pins off screen). The players list shows who is waiting (lobby), who has placed/confirmed (chips during a round), the round's km and points (reveal), and the ranking (end; the total also goes to the local best scores). ← leaves the room. A place the browser does not have (Sheet changed minutes ago) stops with "reload the page".
- **Pitfall:** never give a MapLibre marker element `position: relative` (`.maplibregl-marker` is absolute): the element became full-width in the flow and `translate(-50%)` threw the pins 640px to the left.
- **Friends without an account (`amis.js`).** On first use the browser gets an identity in `localStorage.joueur`: `{ id, secret, pseudo }`. The id (8 characters without 0/O/1/I/L) is the public friend code (`K7M2-QX8P`, link `/?ami=K7M2QX8P`); the server keeps only the SHA-256 of the secret, and refuses a known id with another secret. The `Annuaire` stores players, friendships (both directions) and pending requests (30 at most per sender, 200 friends). Adding someone (by code or link, or the "+ Friend" button next to a player in a room) sends a request; the other side accepts or declines from an alert at the top of the screen (`.amis-alerte`, even outside the game), or from the list. A request crossing one already received makes them friends. Online = an open `/annuaire` connection: a browser that has played with friends connects 2.5 s after start-up (`chargerAmis` in `app.js`), so friends see it online and can invite it. In a room, "Invite your friends who are online" sends an invitation (`inviter`) that pops up with Join / Not now. Limits, told to the owner: the identity lives in one browser (another phone, cleared data or TikTok's in-app browser means a new one).
- **Release.** `CONFIG.serveurJeu` is the server address: `wss://carte-jeu.carte-jeu.workers.dev` (the owner's Cloudflare account, created and logged in with `wrangler login` on 2026-10-09; the first deploy registered the account's workers.dev subdomain `carte-jeu`). Empty, the live site shows no multiplayer and ignores `?partie`/`?ami`; on localhost the local server is always used. Live checks: the protocol test and a timer-only round passed against the deployed server. The game itself is still released by removing `dev` from `#btn-jeu`.
- Texts: `jeu*` (multiplayer ones after `jeuRang`) and `amis*` in `TEXTES`.

### Daily challenge (`site/defi.js`, not public yet)

From a list of ideas the owner chose "the daily challenge", and from an examples page (2026-10-13) the formula "6 tries, hot or cold" (the other one was a single try per day, like the game). It belongs to the game: `#btn-defi` has class `dev` like `#btn-jeu`; to release it, remove the class. A shared link `…/?defi` opens it for anyone (`lienDefi` in `demarrer`), like a multiplayer invitation. `defi.js` is loaded on the first click (`lancerDefi`).
- **The day.** `CONFIG.debutDefi` (`'2026-10-13'`) is challenge #1; the number goes up at midnight in Japan (`jourDuDefi`, UTC+9). Set it to the release day for a #1 then.
- **The place of the day** (`lieuDuJour`): the places with a game photo (the Sheet photo, else the Commons one, `imageDe` as in `jeu.js`), minus those whose video is younger than `NOUVEAU_LIEU` (3) days (their Commons photo arrives in a nightly run, and the place must not change during the day). They are sorted by a hash of `<round>:<id>` (FNV-1a + murmur mix), one per day, a new order each round; the last of a round and the first of the next are swapped if equal. Same list everywhere ⇒ same place for everyone.
- **Playing.** The card reuses the game's styles (`.jeu.defi`): welcome (number, date in Japan, rules, Play), then the photo blurred by `FLOU` (11 px → 0.8 px, `--flou` on the card, set once per try) and the tries list. A tap places a pin; "Confirm try n" turns it into a numbered round on the map (`.defi-pion`, coloured by `TEMPERATURES`: cold > 400 km, warm > 150, hot > 60, burning, found ≤ `TROUVE_KM` 20 km) and a list row: distance, an arrow toward the place (rotated by `cap − bearing`, updated on `rotate`, so it points on screen) and the word. Pins and models are hidden (`body.en-jeu en-defi`, `reperes.montrer`, `modeles3d.filtrer`), as in the game; the game and the challenge stop each other.
- **The end.** The place shows (pin, name, model), dashed lines in each try's colour join the tries to it, then: photo + name + category · prefecture + the photo's credit, the squares (🟦🟨🟧🟥🎯), the verdict, statistics (played, % found, streak, best), the distribution of tries (bars, ✗ for lost), "Next challenge in 5 h 09 min" (every 20 s; at midnight a button opens the new one), Share and "Its video" (opens the place's card).
- **Sharing** (`texteAPartager`): `Daily challenge #12 🗾 3/6`, one line per try (square, km, a compass arrow emoji), `🔥 n days in a row` from 2, and `https://<adresse>/?defi`; never the place's name. Share sheet on touch devices, else copied (`defiCopie`).
- **Memory** (this browser): `localStorage.defi` = `{ jour, essais: [{ lng, lat }], fini }` (a reload resumes the day's game, or shows its end) and `defiStats` = `{ joues, trouves, serie, record, dernierTrouve, repartition[7] }`. The streak counts days found in a row and holds while yesterday was found. `majPointDefi` (`app.js`) shows a red dot on the button until today's challenge is finished.
- **Comparing with the other players** (asked on 2026-10-19). At the end, `comparer` sends the result once to the game server (`POST /defi/<Japan date>`, a plain-text JSON body `{ joueur, essais, trouve }` so the browser needs no CORS preflight; `joueur` is the browser's identity from `amis.js`, `identite().id`; `envoye: true` in `localStorage.defi` afterwards, so a reopening only `GET`s). The answer `{ joueurs, repartition[7] }` fills `.defi-monde`, a teal-ruled box under the verdict: "Today, 78% of the 9 players found it, in 3.1 tries on average." and, if found, "You did better than 38% of the other players" (those who needed more tries or did not find it, among the others) or "…than all the other players!"; alone: "Nobody else has played today yet". Without a server (`CONFIG.serveurJeu` empty) or offline the box stays hidden. The server address is `adresseServeur()` (`reseau.js`) with `ws` → `http`; on localhost it is the local `npm run essai` server. Never test against the live server: its numbers are the real players'.
- Texts: `defi*`, `direction_*` in `TEXTES`. Tests: read the photo's `src` to find the place in `secours-lieux.csv`, then click `map.project(place)` (the 2026-10-13 session's `essai_defi.py`).

### 3D walk (`site/balade/`, not public yet)

On 2026-10-21 the owner asked to visit important places in 3D with a third-person character, like a video game in the art direction of the Pokémon games: a bounded area per place, ZQSD/WASD + mouse, or a joystick and an A button on phones. From examples they chose Itsukushima and Aogashima (Gokayama came third on 2026-10-23, Himeji Castle fourth on 2026-10-25: see Gokayama, Himeji), a boy or girl picked at the start with Kon (a white Inari fox, our own creature) as companion (since 2026-10-23, one of three companions chosen at each visit: see Companions), and three activities: signs and passers-by to talk to, a stamp book (5 stamps per place), and the owner's own TikTok of the place played inside the game. **No Pokémon IP** (names, creatures, logos, music); homage phrases like "Un daim sauvage apparaît !" are fine.
- **Where.** `site/balade/index.html` (`?lieu=itsukushima|aogashima|gokayama|himeji`, `?perso=garcon|fille`, `?compagnon=kon|pon|chapu` (imposes it and skips the choice: for tests), `?langue=en|fr|ja`; for tests `?photo=heros|perso` a still frame, `?demo` a game driven frame by frame through `window.__pas`, `?flou=0`, `?aa=0`, `?ciel=parchemin`). On the map, the card of a place listed in `BALADES` (`app.js`, map id → walk id) shows "Visit in 3D" (`a.dev`, so developer mode only, asked on 2026-10-22), opening the walk in the map's language; the walk's ← goes back to `../#<place>`. To release it, remove the `dev` class. Stamps, the chosen traveller, the last companion and the language are in `localStorage` (`baladeTampons`, `baladePerso`, `baladeCompagnon`, `langue`); the secrets and the shop in `baladeSecrets`, `baladeAchats`, `baladeKobanDev`, `baladeMarchandVu`, `baladeSaison` (see Secrets).
- **Files.** `balade.js` (the scene, camera, controls, talking, stamps, night), `styles.js` (palette, the colour → material table, three.js materials, final pass), `textures.js` (painted textures), `monde.js` (where one can walk: ground, floors, stairs, circles, walls, blocks for the camera, roofs), `personnages.js` (travellers, the three companions, deer, passers-by), `mouettes.js` (Itsukushima's gulls), `betes.js` (Aogashima's, Gokayama's and Himeji's animals), `lueurs.js` (the lanterns' halos at night, and which lamps light the scene), `tenues.js` (the outfit pieces won with the stamps or bought), `tresors.js` (secrets, koban, the shop's items), `corbeaux.js` (Itsukushima's crows), `feux.js` (fireworks), `calendrier.js` (the falling petals, leaves and snow of the magic calendar), `photo.js` (the camera), `decor.js` (sign, tripod), `etiquettes.js` (the painted texts and pictures: see Details), `dialogue.js`, `carnet.js` (stamp book), `son.js` (the UI notes and the shared output), `bruitage.js` + `bruitage-calcul.js` (the sound design), `musique.js` (the tunes), `effets.js` (drops, rings in the water, footprints), `textes.js`, and one module per place (`itsukushima.js`, `aogashima.js`, `gokayama.js`, `himeji.js`): `infos` (id, map id `carte`, TikTok `video`, names, stamps, texts en/fr/ja) and `construire(ctx)` → objects (built with `ateliers()` of `modeles3d.js`, which takes a repaint table and a material table for the walk), `monde`, passers-by, interactions (an interaction may have `pres(x, z)`: a point that follows the player, as along the steam vents' rope at Aogashima, where at first only one spot near the guard worked and the owner could not find it), `terrain(x, z)`, `aspectSol(x, z, h)` → `[colour, MATIERES id]`, extents, water, haze, sun, start; optionally `etiquettes` (painted texts), `trouSol(x0, z0, x1, z1)` (fine-ground cells left out: the tunnel mouth), `musiciens` (a sound source: the Noh stage), `bancs` (see Night, benches and animals), `lumieres`, `betes` and `actionBete`, `animes`, `secret`, `marchand`, `sons`, `feux` (see Secrets), `ruisseaux` and `mares` (Gokayama: the stream's polylines and the paddies' centres, for their sounds), `eau.son` (the water loop: `vagues` or `riviere`), `musiciens.son` (`no` or `kokiriko`); and the module may export `sortesNuit(P)` (its colours that light up at night).
- **The look.** First in big DS-era pixels; on 2026-10-22 the owner found it far too pixelated and asked for a smoother look inspired by **Pokémon X and Y**, with as much detail as possible as long as it stays smooth. Now:
  - the scene renders into a 4× MSAA target with a depth texture (`passeFinale`); a quarter-size copy holds only the far pixels (weighted by distance, so the foreground does not bleed into it) and blurs the distance from 90 to 260 m; the sky (depth 1) stays sharp for the stars; then saturation ×1.08 and a light vignette;
  - pixel ratio at most 2 (1.6 on touch screens); if the median frame over 2 s is above 22 ms (from 4 s after load), it drops by ×0.8, three times at most (`adapter`);
  - toon lighting: a soft ramp for the scenery, three.js's crisp two-tone default for characters; normals smoothed where a shape is round (the `contour` normal when within 42° of the face's, `lisser`), boxes keep sharp edges; PCF shadows (`radius` 2: three r186 removed PCFSoftShadowMap) whose camera follows the player **snapped to shadow-map texels**, or shadow edges shimmer while walking;
  - outlines: the inverted hull of the models, about 2 CSS px for scenery and 2.6 for characters on an 1100 px screen, thinner far away, a darker shade of each colour;
  - painted textures drawn by code at load (`textures.js`, about 150 ms): one `DataArrayTexture` of 16 layers of 256², tileable, each texel a multiplier of the object's colour (0.5 = ×1) or, where alpha is 0, its own colour (flowers in the grass); ground layers (sand, grass with flowers, wet sand, slabs, dirt with pebbles, rock strata, asphalt, jungle floor) and object layers (wood, roof tiles laid along the slope, stone, plaster, foliage, lacquer, corrugated iron), projected on the face's dominant plane;
  - the ground map (`carteDuSol`): colour and material every 0.5 m over the fine extent, computed at the 1 m points and only at the 0.5 m points whose neighbours differ; in the shader the four neighbours vote, at a position wobbled by noise, so grass/path edges are crisp organic curves; outside it the coarse terrain takes one material per triangle (`flat`); `aspectSol` uses `bruitDoux` (smooth noise) for patches, never one random value per cell (squares);
  - 3D grass tufts (`creerTouffes`): seven blades in the ground's colour on grass and jungle floor away from obstacles, one instanced mesh per 24 m square, swaying, shrinking away between 22 and 32 m;
  - steam as soft alpha puffs, round additive stars, a Milky Way of 3D-noise clouds with a dust lane; the UI font is M PLUS Rounded 1c (it has ō, ū).
- **Sound, wind and water (2026-10-22).** The owner asked for footsteps that change with the ground, clothes that stay wet a few seconds after the water, and wind that moves the trees with its sound.
  - **Every sound is synthesized, nothing recorded or downloaded** (`bruitage-calcul.js`: filtered noise, grains, resonant modes, gliding tones). A module worker makes them once after the first gesture (about 0.33 s on this PC; on the main thread, one per 30 ms, if the worker fails); `bruitage.js` plays them. Footsteps: 10 grounds (`SOLS`: grass, sand, wet sand, wood, stone, asphalt, dirt, volcanic rock, forest floor, water), 5 variants each, never the same twice in a row, a little higher or lower each time. The ground is the ground map's material (`SON_DU_SOL` in `balade.js`); on a floor or stairs, the surface's `sol` (`monde.solSous`, wood by default; the stone stairs say `pierre`); water deeper than 3 cm splashes. `animer` (`personnages.js`) returns the foot that lands (at each end of the swing); Kon is lighter and quicker, and only every other pair of paws sounds. Loops: soft wind, gusts, leaves (by the foliage around, `densiteFeuillage`: foliage triangles per 4 m cell), and per place (`lieu.ambiances`) waves, steam vents (`lieu.vapeurs`), night insects, the Noh stage (`no`: see Details); birds (`lieu.oiseaux`): the black kite and the uguisu, every 30–70 s by day. Levels are set against the UI bips, master gain 2.4 in `son.js`. The same evening the owner found the ambience too present under the music, the wind above all: wind −6 dB, the other loops −3 dB, footsteps −2 dB (`V` in `bruitage.js`); on 2026-10-23 the trees rustling in the wind were still too loud: leaves −12 dB, and `densiteFeuillage` counts every foliage kind that sways (1–4, bushes 11, evergreen 12, crowns 21–23: since the vegetation kinds, Aogashima's forest had dropped out of it). Measured (`mesure_ambiance.py`): the ambience alone, standing, −32.7 LUFS at Itsukushima and −36.1 at Aogashima, against the music's −29.2; walking, −27 / −30. Silent during the TikTok video; a hidden page suspends the audio context, and so does sound off.
  - **Wind** (`rafale` and `VENT_GLSL` in `styles.js`, the same formula in JS and GLSL): a breeze of 0.25 plus gusts up to 1 from three slow sines, crossing the place at 7 m/s along `lieu.vent.dir`, × `lieu.vent.force`. Foliage vertices (the `saison` attribute of the models: deciduous, maple, cherry; conifers stiffer) move in the decor's body, its outline and its shadow (`customDepthMaterial`), continuously in space so triangles never part; grass tufts bend with the same gust. The wind sound reads the gust at the player's position, so a gust is heard as the trees bend.
  - **Water** (`effets.js`): splashes and a ring at each step in the water, rings around the legs while wading; clothes darken under the water line (`persoMouille`, skin less), stay wet 2 s, dry in 8 s, and drip; Kon shakes himself once out of the water (`secouer`, a "brrr" and a spray); footprints in sand and wet sand (30 s) and wet prints elsewhere after the water (7 s, also on wood). Prints multiply the ground (custom blending) and are double-sided: their slope-aligned basis flips the triangles.
  - **The TikTok video** sometimes shows TikTok's "Player error" (their servers refusing for a while: the owner saw it in the app's browser pane). On `onPlayerError` (not 3002, autoplay refused) `video()` reloads the player once, then shows the cover (`photos/tiktok/<id>.jpg`) with "Watch it on TikTok" and "Try again"; a small link under the frame always leads to TikTok.
  - Tests (2026-10-22 session scratchpad): `bruitage_test.py [lieu]` (records the page's sound with MediaRecorder on `son.sortie()`, logs each step's ground), `gros_plan_eau.py`, `vent_test.py` (two frames during a gust and their difference), `video_secours.py` (a fake failing player routed in Playwright), `film_son.py` (a real-time demo with sound: CDP screencast + MediaRecorder), `sons_test.mjs` (every sound made in Node: timings, levels, WAVs, spectrograms). GPU time unchanged (0.38 ms in a laptop context); alternate the places when measuring: between runs the GPU clocks down and some medians double.
- **Music (2026-10-22, asked by the owner: "in the style of the old Pokémon games, like X and Y, a bit nostalgic").** `musique.js`: two original tunes composed for the walk (no existing music, nothing downloaded, no Pokémon IP), played note by note with Web Audio through `son.sortie()`, so the sound switch mutes them too. Itsukushima: D major, melody on the yo pentatonic scale, shakuhachi-style flute (a pitch glide and breath noise), koto arpeggios (a `PeriodicWave` through a closing low-pass), pizzicato bass, a string pad, taiko, shaker and wood block, 104 BPM. Aogashima: F major, 126 BPM, flute, marimba off-beat chords, a bouncy bass, light drums. Each: a 2-bar intro, then a 24-bar loop A, A' (a second voice a diatonic third below, a last bar rising into B), B (`AIRS`: melodies as [MIDI note, sixteenths], chords per bar; `partition(lieu, moment)` builds the events). At night (`M.nuit.v > 0.5`) the same tune, slower (76 / 84 BPM), as a music box with soft arpeggios and no drums, after a fade. A scheduler (`setInterval` 60 ms, 0.35 s ahead on the audio clock) creates the notes; a late note (page stalled) is skipped, not bunched. One bus per instrument (pan, a send to a generated 2.2 s reverb). Levels: about −29 LUFS by day, −31 at night (`VOLUME`), about the walking sound's level (−27 to −30 since the ambience was lowered), peaks −16 dBFS; script cost about 5 ms/s. The menu has a « Musique » line (`localStorage.baladeMusique`); it is silent during the TikTok video and resumes at the loop start. Test: the 2026-10-22 session's `musique_ecoute.py <place>` (records the music alone by day and night and with the sound effects, measures script time with and without), `musique_test.mjs` (bar lengths), and a pitch track of the recordings (the melodies come out as written).
- **Gulls (`mouettes.js`, 2026-10-22, asked by the owner: "des mouettes ou des oiseaux qui apparaissent à côté de la mer").** Black-tailed gulls (umineko, the "sea cats" of Japan's coasts, whose cry is a meow), a little bigger than life (×1.4, a 2.2 m span) like the deer next to the travellers. `lieu.mouettes`: `vols` (three circling over the bay at 9–17 m) and `poses` (three on the wet sand near the start, two floating on the water, one on top of the Ō-torii's upper lintel). A resting gull takes off when the player comes within `fuite` (4.2 m; 7 for the torii one) or Kon within three quarters of that; its neighbours within 6 m follow 0.15–0.6 s later (the whole group flies off). It flees away from the player and toward the open sea (`mer`), circles 16–38 s over the water, comes back by the sea side (an approach point 9 m out, 2.5 m up) and glides down to its spot (back to circling if the player is still there). Flight is a steering model (the velocity turns toward a moving target: a point ahead on the circle), heading from the velocity, roll from the turn rate, pitch from the climb; wings in two pieces per side (shoulder and wrist pivots) flap at 2.8 Hz in bursts between glides (4.2 Hz taking off and landing; gliding, the "M" shape). Two body meshes swap: resting (folded wings, legs) and flying (no legs), so a resting gull is one draw. Built with the characters' material (`ctxPerso`). Cries (`bruitage-calcul.js` `mouette`: three nasal meows, a harmonic tone through a 2.3 kHz formant, slightly rough) at take-off and every 8–22 s in flight, 22–62 s at rest, never at night; louder near the camera, panned (`bruitage.oiseau`). Measured in a phone context at CPU ÷4: 180 fps, as before. Test: the session's `mouettes_test.py` (rest, take-off, the bay, the torii, the return: all six back on their spots within 40 s).
- **Stairs and the owner's other fixes (2026-10-22, from their screenshots).**
  - **Aogashima's stairs**: the flattened band followed the stairs' slope while each tread is flat, so the ground cut through every tread (from one mesh point to the next, 1 m apart, it even covered the step ends: "a piece of the mountain comes over the stairs"), and the thin treads showed gaps. Now each step is a solid block down to 0.75 m under its front edge, the ground sinks 0.65 m under the steps (only within 1.1 m of the line, where `monde.chemin` holds the walker: wider, one walked under the steps) with a dirt shoulder 0.35 m lower at their ends, then rejoins the slope. One walks on `monde.chemin(MARCHES, 1.15, { sol: 'pierre' })`, a surface along a polyline (`surLaLigne`, moved to `monde.js`, also gives the distance along the line, `s`). The handrail posts reach the shoulder.
  - **Itsukushima's pagoda stairs** ran slantwise up the round hill: buried on one side, floating on the other, beside a paved slope, with a lantern in the way. Now `ESCALIER`: straight up the hill's axis toward the pagoda (x 46, z 19.5 → 27.6), 21 solid steps between two stone walls (sheared boxes, so their ends stay plumb) with a coping, a landing at the top, a lantern at each corner, `monde.rampe(…, { sol: 'pierre' })`. `terrain` = `solNaturel` carved: sunk under the steps (mesh points |x − 46| ≤ 1), no higher than the walls' top under and behind them, then back to the hill by 4.6 m. The sign and the male deer moved; the hill is grass only (since 2026-10-24 all of it: the street's paving stops at the stairs' foot, and the paved forecourt around the pagoda is gone, at the owner's request).
  - **The shopkeeper floated on the shop's roof**: `monde.hauteur(x, z)` (y = ∞) picked the top of the `plein` block she stood in. Now `hauteur` never stands anyone on a `plein` block, and the momiji shop's counter moved out so she stands between it and the façade (the block starts at the façade).
  - **The red block under the parasol** (an unexplained red box, floating above the slope) is now a tea-house bench (`BANC`): wooden legs, red felt (hi-mōsen) hanging over its edges, a tray with green tea and two maple-leaf cakes, and a nodate-gasa parasol (a flattened 24-sided cone, ribs, coloured threads under it), on the ground.
  - **The bronze lantern** at the end of the platform (`lanterneBronze`): a six-sided tōrō: two-tier base, pillar with rings, console, a cage of six posts with lattice windows over the paper (lit at night: `P.fenetre`), a hollowed roof with curled corners, a jewel on a lotus.
  - **Right-click while walking**: the context menu took the key release, so the player kept walking. The game canvas has no context menu any more (right-drag turns the camera), and held keys are forgotten when a menu opens elsewhere or the page is hidden. Test: the session's `clic_droit.py`.
  - `monde.libre` tells a surface from the ground (`poserPied`): the slope limit and the deep-water rule apply to the natural ground only (at the top of Aogashima's stairs, the path lay flush with the steep ground and blocked the walker). Checks: `regions.mjs` (everything reachable in both places), `marche_escaliers.py` (both stairs climbed with the W key: stone footsteps), `coplanaires.mjs`.
- **Details (2026-10-23, from the owner's screenshots: "rajouter un maximum de détails").**
  - **Aogashima's stairs, again.** The mountain still covered the top steps: the belvédère's blend (its bowl reaches 10 m around) was applied after the stairs' carving and raised the ground 1–2 m over the last flight. `terrain` = `relief` (the old one) with the stairs carved last; in the top corner the flattening takes the flight's height, not the crest step's; a stone landing continues the flight by 1.3 m (a slab put across the turn made a low wall over the last steps), then the step onto the crest, whose barrier opening follows (`A_HAUT`). `marches_sol.mjs` samples the drawn ground (the 1 m grid, its two triangles per cell, +3 cm) over every tread and beside it: 0 points above a tread (2,246 before). The handrail stood 1.11–1.3 m from the line, pulled straight toward the crater's centre (its bottom posts stood on the steps) with a 12 cm wall: the walker's big head went into it. Now its posts follow the line's side at `RAMPE` (1.25 m, mitred at the bends), with a 38 cm wall, and an invisible wall on the slope side: one stays within 0.88 m of the line. **Sliding** (`balade.js`): a blocked move is retried turned by ±0.32, ±0.64… ±1.28 rad (slower by the cosine) before the two axes: along a slanted rail neither axis passed and the walker stopped. `rampe_glisse.py`: down the whole flight pressed against either side, no stop.
  - **The tunnel** (`TUBE`, `FACADE`, `versTunnel`/`duTunnel` in `aogashima.js`): a stepped concrete headwall (an `ExtrudeGeometry` with the arch as a hole, 5.5 m deep: its inner faces are the tunnel's first metres), a raised arch band, a plaque « 青宝トンネル SEIHŌ TUNNEL », then a tube to 18 m with inward faces darkening with depth (vertex colours by slice), sidewalks, white edge lines, orange lamps (`sorte: 'lampe'`), a black end. The ground is lowered under the mouth and raised behind the façade up to its top (the wall covers it); the 1 m cells between, where the ground would cross the tube, are left out of the fine mesh (`trouSol`, only cells behind the façade, which covers them from above). Outside: the road now starts at the mouth, white delineators with orange reflectors, a 30 km/h sign facing the cars leaving the tunnel, an orange curve mirror at the path junction.
  - **The sauna** (`S`, `versSauna`): one now walks in: the door (blue « ゆ » noren, the « ふれあいサウナ » board, a step), the hall (shoe lockers, a bench, the 300-yen box and its notice, a lamp), the partition's passage, the steam room (two tiers of slatted benches, duckboards, two floor grates whose steam rises, a bucket and ladle, the twelve-minute timer and a thermometer drawn as `etiquettes`, « 高温注意 », a caged lamp). Walls are `mur`s with gaps; floors are surfaces; each wall is a camera `bloc` and the roof a `toit`, so the camera stays inside. **Sitting** (the « S'asseoir » action on the lower tier): `jeu.asseoir(siège, vue)` (the player's seated pose, `asseoir()` in `personnages.js`; Kon comes in front; the player gets up where they stood: the seat is inside the benches' wall), `jeu.vapeur(oui)` (`.vapeur-ecran`: white veils rising over the screen, CSS transforms only), `jeu.transpirer()` (clothes soaked: they dry in about ten seconds; Kon shakes himself off). `sauna_test.py [--tel]` walks in by the door and the passage, sits, gets up, buys a drink.
  - **Also at Aogashima**: the camp kitchen (炊事場: concrete sink with two steel basins and four taps, a hearth with glowing embers, a pot and a kettle, a hanging bulb, the board, firewood, the « もえる / もえない » sorting bins), a vending machine by the sauna door (its lit window drawn as an `etiquette`) and its can bin, a white kei truck with a yellow « 品川 » plate and crates of sweet potatoes, a field of Phoenix roebelenii palms (the island's crop: arched fronds, `PALMERAIE`), the geothermal pot's basket of eggs and sweet potatoes and its « 地熱釜 » sign; every reading board has its Japanese title (`panneau(…, { ecrit: true })`, `PAPIER_PANNEAU`). New actions: look at the tunnel, sit in the sauna, buy a drink.
  - **Itsukushima's Noh stage**, rebuilt facing east toward the big platform (where the player stands, as the audience): cypress floor and pillars, a curved gable roof (custom geometry) with barge boards, the gable's wooden triangle and its gold ornament; the painted pine on the back wall and bamboo on the side wall (`pinPeint`, `bambous`: canvas paintings), the small kirido door; the hashigakari from the back corner to the west gallery (its own roof, railings, the five-coloured agemaku curtain) and three small pines on rocks. On stage (passers-by standing on the stage's surface, unreachable): the dancer of « Hagoromo » (`pose: 'danse'`, `masque`, `coiffure: 'kazura'`, `chapeau: 'tengan'`, a gold fan) gliding slowly along a round; four musicians (`pose: 'jouer'`, `instrument`: fue, kotsuzumi, otsuzumi, taiko, striking now and then) and three chorus members kneeling (`pose: 'seiza'`). **Its music** (`bruitage-calcul.js`, loop `no`, 16 s): the nōkan flute gliding between notes with its shrill hishigi, the kotsuzumi « pon », the ōtsuzumi « kan », the taiko « ten »; heard within 9–32 m (`lieu.musiciens`), and the walk's music dips by up to 80 % there (`musique.maj(nuit, baisse)`). Measured at the platform's edge (`mesure_no.py`): −28.3 LUFS, peaks −10 dBFS (the walk's music: −29.2). The old stage faced north and its bridge's roof stuck through the back wall in front of a pine drawn as one green square.
  - **Also at Itsukushima**: by the miko, an omikuji counter (white cloth, the hexagonal omikuji box with its stick, omamori on a slanted stand, the coin tray, « おみくじ 一回 100円 »); on the platform's east edge the rack where bad-luck slips are tied (« おみくじ結び所 ») and an ema rack; a stack of decorated sake barrels (kazaridaru) in the east gallery; the shops' signboards (« もみじ饅頭 », « しゃもじ », « あなごめし », « お土産 »). Drawing a bad-luck omikuji now ends with tying it to the rack. New actions: watch the play, the ema, the rack, the barrels.
  - **Painted texts** (`etiquettes.js`): every label of a place is drawn (M PLUS Rounded 1c, once the font's slices for those characters have loaded, then redrawn) into one 2048-px atlas, and the labels make one mesh (Lambert; a second, unlit, for the glowing ones) with alpha-test cut-outs (the round sign, the timer). `?etiquettes=0` leaves them out, for measurements. The first version (one canvas, texture and mesh per label) showed, with the GPU timer of `gpu.py`, Itsukushima's frames at 2.2–2.9 ms instead of 0.4 in 8 runs out of 10, while frame rate and CPU time did not move (178 fps; 1.1 instead of 1.0 ms of CPU per frame): that timer is unreliable on this PC (it flips between two modes); the atlas did not change the reading, `fluidite.py` (CPU ÷4 phone) stays at 172–180 fps with no slow frame, also facing the stage and in the sauna.
  - **Lights**: the decor shader adds the colour as emission for `sorte: 'lumiere'` (lanterns, windows, the kitchen bulb, the embers: at night, `mats.lueur`) and `sorte: 'lampe'` (the tunnel, the sauna: always) (`styles.js`, `SORTES.lampe` in `modeles3d.js`).
  - Checks (the 2026-10-23 session's scratchpad): `vues.py` (new spots), `coplanaires.mjs` on both places (the truck, the barrels, the racks, the stage's beam ends and planks were fixed), `regions.mjs` (now also lists the new actions: all reachable), `fuites.py` (no way down the crater wall), `partie.py [--tel]`, `nuit.py`, `marche_escaliers.py`, `clic_droit.py`.
- **Companions and characters (2026-10-23, evening; the owner: "changer le compagnon à chaque début de visite, comme dans Pokémon, le choix entre 3", and "refais le renard, le garçon et la fille mieux faits").**
  - **Three companions** (`COMPAGNONS`, `creerCompagnon` in `personnages.js`, original creatures from Japanese folklore): **Kon** the white Inari fox (redone: chest ruff, red bib and bell, big ears red inside, red flicks from the eyes toward the ears like the shrine statues, a forehead mark, a plume tail with a red tip), **Pon** the tanuki (round, dark mask and shoulders, cream muzzle, big belly, the leaf on its head that tanuki use to transform; it sits upright like the Shigaraki statues and now and then drums on its belly: « pon, poko, pon »), **Chapu** the river otter (kawauso, also a shape-shifter in tales: long supple body, whiskers, webbed paws, a blue kerchief with white dots; it bounds, stands up on its hind legs to wait, and swims where the water rises over its legs: `renard.nage`, floating at the surface, paddling, no footsteps). Each `FORMES[espece]` gives the body, head, eyes, four legs, tail (a curved `fuseau`: a cylinder bent along a Bézier curve with a radius profile) and the `bascule` point the body tilts around (sitting, standing). All blink (`cligneur`), hop (`sauter`), shake off water (`secouer`).
  - **The choice, at the start of every visit** (`depart()` in `balade.js`): the first time, « Bienvenue… garçon ou fille ? » (a narrator now, no name: `bienvenue`), else « Te revoilà à … » (`revoila`); then `cadrerTrio()`: the three sitting in a row in front of the traveller, the camera placed low in front of them (`M.cadre`; a little to the left on a wide screen, the choice menu is on the right; further back on a tall one), and a choice with the last companion marked first (`dialogue.choisir(…, { depart, survol, obligatoire })`: the marked option's description replaces the text, the creature hops and cries; Escape chooses nothing). The two others vanish in a little puff (`effets.nuage`, the « pouf » sound), like foxes and tanuki in tales; the chosen one cries and introduces itself (`salut`), then the camera goes behind the traveller. The menu has a « Compagnon » line (the new one comes in a puff). Texts: `compagnons` in `textes.js` (per companion: `nom`, `choix`, `salut(n)`, `indice`, `tout`; each its own voice: Kon polite, Pon greedy and jolly, Chapu bubbly); a place's line for the companion is an object per companion (`saunaCompagnon`, `abriCompagnon`) said with `jeu.dire(…[jeu.compagnon], 'compagnon')`. Sounds (`bruitage-calcul.js`): `cri/kon` (two yaps), `cri/pon` (belly drum), `cri/chapu` (three chirps and a splash), `tambour`, `pouf`; made first, `bruitage.preparer()` starts the making at the choice.
  - **The travellers** (`creerHeros`, same skeleton as the passers-by, who keep `creerPersonnage`): a real face on the head sphere (`surVisage` places flat pieces facing out): big eyes as flat discs (iris, its light lower reflection, pupil, two highlights, the lash line; the girl's flick), in their own meshes so they blink; eyebrows, nose, smile, blush; hair in locks (cones). The boy: two-tone cap (teal, a white badge with a red Fuji), spiky fringe and nape, open red jacket over a white T-shirt, belt, cuffed shorts, sneakers with red toe caps, a backpack with flap, pocket, buckles and a rolled mat. The girl: straw sun hat (a turned profile with straw rings, red ribbon, bow, a flower), fringe, side locks, pigtails tied with red bows, pink blouse with sailor collar and red bow, pleated navy skirt, knee socks, red shoes, a pink backpack with an omamori. The hat and the cap are a separate mesh tilted as a whole (`coiffe`); a lathe profile must go bottom to top, or its faces point inward.
  - `jeu.cadrer({ de, vers, champ })` / `cadrer(null)`: the camera placed elsewhere for a moment (the azumaya view, the binoculars; `champ` widens on tall screens to keep the same width); `jeu.jumelles(oui)`: two circles over the screen (an SVG mask, `.jumelles-ecran`).
  - Checks (the session scratchpad): `choix_test.py [lieu] [--tel] [--premiere]` (the whole departure), `compagnons_vue.py` (each companion from four sides), `marche_compagnons.py` (trotting, bounding, the otter swimming), `copl_persos.mjs` (coplanar faces inside the characters: the tails' last ring was NaN, a cosine a hair below 0 raised to a power), `fluidite_port.py <place> --port 8124` (the same measure on the previous version, served from a `git archive` on another port: identical, 148 or 90 fps depending on the GPU's state, never between versions).
- **Aogashima, the same evening.** The sauna's door jambs flickered: the posts' faces lay in the plane of the walls' ends (and the step was buried under the ground). The crest barrier east of the stairs' opening dipped into the stairs' cut and shot straight up the slope: `rayonBarriere(t)` now pushes it onto the plateau, along the top of the cut (the first point where the ground is no longer dug, then smoothed), and over the belvédère along its stone ring's lip (`ANNEAU`); `dansLeCadre` follows it. The belvédère's outer wall goes 5 m down (it floated over the cut). West of the stairs top, the empty crest got a corner (`AZUMAYA`, `PIN_VENT`, `JUMELLES`, `STELE`): a wooden azumaya facing the caldera (sit on the bench: the view over the shoulder; the 思い出ノート travellers' notebook on the table), a wind-bent pine, coin binoculars toward Hachijō-jima (`HACHIJO`, seen through two circles), the « 外輪山 » stone (the 1785 eruption and the islanders' return fifty years later), sakuyuri lilies, ashitaba and susuki; the belvédère's sign moved there. `crete_test.py [--tel] [compagnon]` goes through it all.
- **Night, benches and animals (2026-10-23, late; the owner's screenshot of the girl sunk into the belvédère, then: "s'asseoir sur n'importe quel banc", "quelques animaux sur Aogashima", a passer-by who switches to night and back, the night music, lamps lit, interactions that change at night, "la vendeuse ne va pas vendre la nuit").**
  - **The belvédère**: the walker followed the natural ground, a smooth bowl under the stone tiers, and sank to the knees; each tier is now a round surface (`monde.anneau(cx, cz, r0, r1, h)`, from `GRADINS`, which also builds the lathe profile).
  - **Night on demand.** Aogashima's camper and Itsukushima's new boatman (`batelier`, on the beach in front of the start, by his rowing boat `BARQUE` moored in the shallow water: an extruded U hull tapered and raised to the bow, two thwarts, the sculling oar, a red chōchin on a bamboo pole, the stake and its rope) make night fall when one says yes, and bring the day back when one talks to them again (`jeu.nuit(oui)` no longer moves the camera; the camper's scene uses `jeu.leverLesYeux(oui)`, the boatman's a `jeu.cadrer` on the torii from the beach). Each visit starts by day. `jeu.estNuit`; `jeu.txn(cle)`: the place's text `cleNuit` at night when there is one. At night: the stars, the night tunes (already `M.nuit.v > 0.5`), the insects (Itsukushima too now), the deer lie down (`creerDaim` `animer(…, couche)`: the body sinks 0.57 m, legs in the ground, head turned; each at its own pace), the lanterns and windows lit with a warm light that keeps a vivid colour (a red lantern stays red), Itsukushima's vermillion floodlit orange (`sortesNuit(P)` of the place → the 4th argument of `ateliers()` in `modeles3d.js`, colour → kind; `SORTES.illumine` = 10, read by the decor shader in `styles.js`: `P.vermillon`/`vermillonFonce` illuminated, `P.fenetre` lit: the corridors' hanging lanterns now have a band of paper windows, they were a closed bronze can), and around each lamp a halo and its light on what surrounds it (`lueurs.js`; `lieu.lumieres` [{ x, y, z, halo, sol, r, c }]: Itsukushima's corridor, shore, hill and bronze lanterns and the boat's lantern on the water; Aogashima's kitchen bulb, a new lamp by the sauna door, the vending machine's cold white light, a new camping lantern on the picnic table).
  - **What changes at night**: Itsukushima: the shopkeeper's shop is closed (no momiji manjū, no stamp), the omikuji counter too, the priest, schoolgirl, tourist and old man say other things, the deer doze ("Chut… il dort"), the torii's wood "has kept a little of the sun's warmth". Aogashima: the sauna keeper is closed (no eggs: the stamp waits for the day), the steam room too, too dark for the binoculars, the vending machine still works ("au Japon, ils marchent jour et nuit"), the guard, hiker, boy and old man, the azumaya and the companions' lines there change. Texts `…Nuit` in each place's `TEXTES` (en/fr/ja).
  - **Benches**: every bench can be sat on (`lieu.bancs` [{ de, a (the line one can sit along), y (the seat's top), cap (facing), vue (the camera's turn, 0.35 by default) }]; `pointDuBanc` and `interactionsBancs` in `monde.js`, `jeu.asseoirSurBanc` in `balade.js`): one sits where one stands along it and stays seated until walking (or A, « Se lever », `seLever`: the camera stays where it is); the companion hops next to the traveller when there is room (`renard.banc`, on arrival or after 2.5 s) and hops down when one gets up. The azumaya's back bench keeps its scene, then one stays seated. Benches: Aogashima's belvédère (2), the azumaya's sides (2), the sauna hall, the picnic table (2); Itsukushima's tea-house bench (the camera from the side: behind, the parasol pole hid everything) and two new wooden beach benches facing the torii (`BANCS_PLAGE`; the old man now sits on the second: passers-by may have `assis`, the seat's top, in `creerPassant`: they stay put).
  - **Aogashima's animals** (`betes.js`, `lieu.betes`, original drawings, bigger than life like the gulls): three Izu thrushes (akakokko: black head, rusty belly, yellow bill; they hop, peck, sing a synthesized fluty « kyoron kyoron tsîî » now and then, and fly to the farthest of their spots when one comes within 2.2 m), four Okada's skinks with blue tails (they bask, do push-ups, dash away if one runs at them or comes within 1 m, then walk home), a ginger cat asleep curled by the geothermal pot and a black-and-white cat sitting by the vending machine (pet them: a synthesized purr; awake at night, their eyes glow: `sorte: 'lumiere'`; a meow when one comes near), chestnut tiger butterflies (asagimadara) fluttering and settling, two black kites circling high over the caldera. At night the birds, skinks and butterflies are gone (asleep). Look at a thrush or a skink, pet a cat (`lieu.actionBete`; the companion comments on the cats). Sounds `oiseau/akakokko`, `bete/miaou`, `bete/ronron` (`bruitage.bete(nom)`). Animals beyond 40–60 m are hidden and frozen.
  - Checks (the session scratchpad): `nuit2_test.py itsukushima|aogashima [--tel] [compagnon]` (night by the boatman/camper, the lit places, the night lines, sitting on benches with the companion, day again), `betes_vue.py [nuit]` (each animal close up), `bancs.mjs` (every bench reachable, the belvédère's heights), `barque_vue.py`, plus `nuit.py` (now: the camper's night stays, the keeper refuses eggs, the camper brings back the day: all 5 stamps), `partie.py [--tel]`, `crete_test.py`, `sauna_test.py`, `regions.mjs`, `fuites.py`, `marche_escaliers.py`, `clic_droit.py`, `choix_test.py`, `coplanaires.mjs` (the beach bench's back slats and the boat's rope were fixed), `fluidite_port.py` (same frame times as the previous version, `site-avant` served on port 8124).
- **Lamplight and outfits (2026-10-23, night; the owner's screenshot of the pagoda stairs' lanterns, whose pools of light "form arcs of circles above the ground", then: win a cosmetic piece with the 5 stamps of a place, and a menu to change one's outfit).**
  - **The lamps' light falls on the scene.** The pools were flat discs at the height of the ground under each lantern: on a slope, by the walls of a stairway, they floated above the ground in arcs. Now the light is computed in the shaders (`LAMPES_GLSL`, `eclairerParLampes` in `styles.js`): each fragment of the decor, the ground, the grass tufts, the characters (half as strong: a pale hat under a lantern turned white) and the water adds its own colour × each lamp's colour × (1 − d²/R²)² × how much it faces the lamp, as emission. R reaches from the flame to `r` on the ground. `lueurs.js` picks every frame the `NB_LAMPES` (12) lamps nearest a point 8 m ahead of the camera, sorted, the rest off (`w` = 0 ends the loop), and fades a lamp to 0 at the distance of the first one left out (no pop when the set changes), and 70–160 m from the camera; `lampesZone` is a ball around them all, so fragments far from every lamp skip the loop; by day the loop ends at once. The halos (one `Points`) stay. With fewer than 13 lamps (Aogashima has 4) the fade must not use the missing 13th: an `Infinity` there gave NaN and black discs. Checked: `lampes_test.py <place>` (night views: the pagoda stairs, the corridors, the shore, the boat, the camping, the sauna door, the kitchen).
  - **Outfits.** All the stamps of a place win a piece (`tenues.js`, `PIECES`: Itsukushima → the deer antlers, a headband with deer ears and three-tined antlers; Aogashima → the star glasses, gold star frames with violet lenses; plus the cap or straw hat and « no hat », « nothing » on the eyes, always there). One piece per place (`tete`, `yeux`; Gokayama → the mino, in the `dos` slot: see Gokayama; Himeji → the white heron headdress, `tete`: see Himeji); won = all the place's stamps are in the book (`lireTampons`, nothing else stored); each traveller keeps their outfit (`localStorage.baladeTenue` { garcon, fille }), and a piece no longer won (book cleared) falls back to the default. The 3D pieces are built in `creerHeros` (`porter(place, id)`, in the head's frame, the first time they are worn); a hat-slot piece hides the default hat, and the boy then shows spiky hair on top (`dessus`).
  - **The reward.** At the 5th stamp, after the companion's line (and at arrival for a piece won before, `localStorage.baladeTenuesVues`: `offrirRecompenses`): the piece on a card under a « Wardrobe » ribbon, turning in on golden rays, a jingle (`son.cadeau`), « Every stamp of …! Here is your reward… / You get: … », then « Put it on now? »: yes, the camera faces the traveller, a puff (`effets.nuage`, `pouf`), the piece is on, « It suits you so well! / Menu → Wardrobe ».
  - **The wardrobe** (« Garde-robe » / « Wardrobe » / « きせかえ »): the hanger button at the top (hidden at 360 px and less) or the menu (with n/2 won). The camera faces the traveller (`cadrerDeFace`: from where it was, or the nearest direction, in 0.4 rad steps, where nothing hides them: rays from the camera to their feet, waist and head, in the middle and on each side, against the decor's real shapes (`M.decor`), and back from them to the camera (a camera inside the pagoda's stone base saw nothing of it), the ground and the passers-by); the panel on the right (at the bottom on a tall screen, the camera further back so the antlers fit above it); the companion stands beside, a little forward. Each place's pieces, the worn one framed in red; a piece not won yet is greyed with a padlock and says what to do (« Find the 5 stamps of Itsukushima to win it (3/5) »). A tap puts it on at once (a little bow). Dragging turns the traveller. × or Escape closes. The stamp book shows each place's reward under its stamps (to win, or won). Texts: `tenue` in `textes.js`.
  - Checks (the session scratchpad): `tenue_test.py [--tel] [--garcon]` (no stamps: the wardrobe and a locked piece; 4 stamps then the pagoda's: the card, « yes », the antlers; the wardrobe: each head piece, dragging, Escape; Aogashima's stamps: the glasses announced at arrival; the book, the menu; walking), `lampes_test.py`, plus `nuit.py`, `partie.py [--tel]`, `nuit2_test.py`, `choix_test.py`, `crete_test.py`, `clic_droit.py`, `sauna_test.py`, `fluidite_port.py` (night included).
- **Secrets, koban and the merchant (2026-10-23, evening).** The owner liked the place-themed outfit pieces and asked to keep doing it: **every new place gets its own cosmetic piece, which may be clothing.** Then they asked for an easter egg per place, hard to find, for attentive players; from my proposals they chose Itsukushima's sacred crows and Aogashima's treasure map, rewarded with something other than stamps: a token to buy precious things, and « Ok pour toutes les propositions, fabrique tout ».
  - **The economy** (`tresors.js`): a secret found gives one **koban** (an Edo gold coin). Nothing is stored but the secrets found, the clues seen and the purchases (`baladeSecrets` { trouves, indices }, `baladeAchats`); the koban count is derived: secrets + `baladeKobanDev` (only counted in developer mode, `localStorage.modeDev === 'oui'`, the map's own key) − prices. `trouverSecret(id)` (via `jeu`) shows the spinning koban card (`.koban-gagne`), a jingle, and the hint where the merchant is; the stamp book ends with a golden « Secret found » line and the koban count; the menu shows the koban and the actions bought.
  - **Itsukushima, the sacred crows** (`corbeaux.js`, from the otoguishiki rite): a flat stone hidden behind the pagoda (`PIERRE`) with crumbs on it (« Regarder »: a hint, the companion suggests a sweet). The momiji shopkeeper now also gives a manjū to carry (`jeu.donner`/`aDansSac`/`prendre`, a session bag). Put on the stone (« Poser »), two jungle crows (×1.3, built like the gulls) come down from the sky (the camera from the side: `jeu.cadrerDeCote`, which picks the side where nothing hides the traveller or the spot), peck, one takes the cake, then they fly from perch to perch (a stone lantern, a maple's top, a tall bamboo by the grove) and wait each time until the player is within 7 m. At the end they land on a small faded torii inside a ring of bamboo (`BOSQUET`); its door of bamboo stalks swings open like two gates (a scene from `animes`, once the crows have arrived and the player is within 5.5 m); inside: a moss-covered hokora, two stone crows, a stone lantern, a light shaft (a shader quad fading by height and angle) and the koban on a sanbō. The grove is walls for the walker, 16 camera blocks around the ring (the camera stays inside the clearing), and no two stalks touch (their nodes would flicker).
  - **Aogashima, the treasure map**: the last page of the travellers' notebook in the azumaya (`cahier`) is a children's map (`carteTresor()`, SVG on a paper sheet: `jeu.image`) with a red cross east of Maruyama, by a red camellia (`TSUBAKI`) and three stones, in a small hollow (`TRESOR`, dug into `relief`). The boy in the village hints at it. Only once the map has been seen (`indiceVu('carte-tresor')`) does « Creuser » appear there: the camera from the side, the dig gesture, three shovel sounds and dirt, a rusty tin box rises, a 1985 class photo of the island's school and a letter (`photoClasse()`, `lettre()`: the 1785 eruption, the return in 1835 led by Sasaki Jirōdayū, « on revient toujours chez soi »), then the koban.
  - **Bunbuku** (`creerMarchand`, `personnages.js`): an old travelling tanuki (Pon's uncle; Pon reacts), straw hat, a big wooden oi box with drawers on his back, a little flag; under the pines between the shrine and the hill at Itsukushima (`MARCHAND` 27.2, 25.2), in the middle of the eastern forest at Aogashima (`MARCHAND` 45, −3; behind the palm field until 2026-10-23, when the owner asked to move him into the forest): a clearing 4.5 m wide in two rings of trees (small in front, tall behind, so the shop camera fits), open toward the crater centre, with ferns, mossy stones and his red chōchin on a bamboo pole, lit at night (`LAMPION`, a `lumieres` entry); the grove draws from `hasard()` and skips spots within 1.8 m of a crater tree (their crowns met face to face). He introduces himself the first time (`baladeMarchandVu`), then opens his shop (`.boutique`: on the right, at the bottom on a tall screen; the chosen item's details scroll into view; the camera from the side where nothing hides either of them, `cadrerMarchand` with `gene(de, cible)`). Items by price (`ARTICLES`): 1 koban the bon-odori dance, magic footsteps (petals, stars or maple leaves behind you), companion accessories (bandana, small straw hat, bell); 2 fireworks, fox mask, festival happi; 3 yukata, camera; 5 the three secret companions, the magic calendar, the golden kimono, the samurai armour with its kabuto. In developer mode the shop has « +1 koban » and « Tout recommencer » (`remettreDev`: purchases, extra koban, secrets and clues, the merchant's first meeting; then the walk reloads, to replay the secrets).
  - **What they do.** The outfit pieces go to the wardrobe, which now has five sections (`PLACES`: head, eyes, outfit, footsteps, companion); a bought outfit (`HABITS` in `creerHeros`) replaces the meshes it covers, a long robe walks with small steps. The dance (`commencerDanse`): the camera in front, an original festival tune (`musique.js` `odori`: fue, shamisen, kane, taiko, 112 BPM), the traveller and the companion dance in time (`musique.fete(true)` gives the beat offset); a key or a tap stops it. Fireworks (`lancerFeux`, `feux.js`): at night (it offers to wait for night), over the sea behind the torii or over Maruyama; a show too close is moved away to 1.6 × its mean height, and the camera looks up at it (`M.regardHaut`) with the traveller at the bottom of the frame; each burst lights the scene (`M.flash` on the hemisphere light) and booms with the sound's delay. The camera (`photo.js`): orbit around the traveller, four frames (postcard, ukiyo-e, snapshot, notebook page with the place's stamps), six poses, the picture composed on a canvas, shared or saved. The magic calendar (`changerSaison`, `baladeSaison`): summer (as always), spring, autumn, winter; the shaders repaint the scenery (`saisons` uniform: cherry blossom, red maples, bare trees, snow on what faces up and on the ground, `SAISONS_GLSL`, `SOL_SAISON_GLSL`), grass tufts go in winter, footsteps crunch in snow, and petals, leaves or snow fall around the camera (`calendrier.js`, on the GPU).
  - **The secret companions** (`COMPAGNONS_SECRETS`): **Gao**, an Okinawan shisa (a lion-dog with a curly mane; it sits upright like a roof guardian), **Fuwa**, a baby sea dragon (a small Ryūjin: pearly rings that undulate, it floats, a pearl in its paw), **Saya**, a kodama (a little tree spirit: veined wood, moss cap and sprout, glowing eyes, it walks on two root legs). Once bought they join the departure choice (more than three: two rows like a class photo, the three starters in front, the camera a little higher; on a tall screen the names in a grid, `choisir(…, { grille: true })`) and the menu; they have their lines in each place and their cries.
  - **For a new place**: give it a secret (`SECRETS` in `tresors.js`, its scenes and texts in the place module), a spot for Bunbuku (`marchand`), its fireworks spot (`feux`), and its outfit piece (`PIECES`, `lieu`).
  - Checks (the session scratchpad): `secret_it_test.py [--tel] [--compagnon pon]` (the whole crows' secret, the book, Bunbuku's first meeting, buying the dance, dancing), `secret_ao_test.py [--tel]` (the map, digging, the koban; developer koban, buying everything; the wardrobe with each outfit; the four seasons; a photo saved; fireworks; the six-companion choice), `atteint_secret.mjs` (the koban, the hidden shrine and the merchant reachable with the door open), `copl_tresors.mjs` (outfits, new companions, accessories, merchant: no overlapping faces left but two folded quads on Gao's tail and Saya's moss cap), `regions.mjs`, `coplanaires.mjs`.
- **Seasons, poses and accessories (2026-10-23, late; the owner: the photo poses' arms went into the head, the seasons could be better — leaves on the ground, footprints that stay in the snow like in the sand, better trees and bushes —, the companions' accessories fitted badly, and Aogashima's ground around the camp looked pixelated).**
  - **The ground no longer shows its triangles.** The ground shader painted each triangle with the projection (top, front, side) that its own face normal chose, and laid the snow by that face normal: on the crater wall's 4 m triangles, a patchwork of facets. It now uses the terrain's smoothed normal (`vMondeN`) and blends the three projections (`peindreSol`, triplanar), and the snow's edge is waved by noise.
  - **Vegetation kinds** (`SORTES` in `modeles3d.js`): `buisson` 11 (azaleas: in flower in spring — round flower dots drawn in the shader, `fleurs()`, magenta, pink or white per bush —, bright red in autumn for one in two, dark green under the snow in winter), `persistant` 12 (Aogashima's slope forest, palms and ferns: wild cherries here and there in spring, a few golden patches in autumn, green under the snow), and the crowns of deciduous trees, `houppier` 21–23 (20 + the colour's kind): they fall in winter (`tombee`, `CHUTE_GLSL`: the triangle collapses to a point in the body, the outline and the shadow, each at its own `tirage`, so a season change sheds them gradually). Until 2026-10-25 one triangle in six also fell in autumn: the crowns are hollow clumps, and the owner saw holes in them at Himeji; the fallen leaves are on the ground instead (below). Autumn cherries are red, orange or golden, one tint per tree, a few redder leaves (they were all one light brown). `arbre(…, { caduc: true })` and `ramure()` add branches and twigs inside the crowns, hidden until they fall (Itsukushima's maples, Aogashima's crater trees). `buisson()` builds a bush from five tufts. Each tree or bush has one `graine` (stored in `saison.y` for the walk only: the map keeps its paint factor there), so a tree blossoms or turns red all at once (it used to be split by a 3.2 m grid). Snow on foliage follows the smoothed `contour` normal with a waved edge (`neigeFeuillage`): on the faceted balls it looked like rocks. Maples are fresh green in spring.
  - **Leaves and petals on the ground** (`carteCanopee` in `balade.js`, read by `solSaison`): a 1 m map of the deciduous crowns seen from above (density; share of crowns that blossom in spring; autumn hue), blurred over 2 m. The ground shader scatters drawn leaves there (ovals, maple stars under red trees, `feuilleSol`) in autumn, and pink petals under the cherries in spring. Since 2026-10-25 (the owner: « au printemps des feuilles de cerisier au sol, en automne des feuilles d'automne par terre ») three layers of bigger leaves (cells of 21, 15 and 11 cm: leaves about 11 cm long) and of petals (13, 9.5, 7 cm), denser, and under the crowns the ground takes a little of their tint (a leaf litter in autumn, `litiere`; a pink haze in spring), seen from afar.
  - **Footprints in the snow** (`effets.js`): a negative `teinte` is a bluish hollow, 1.6× the sole; they stay 7 minutes (`duree` 420; 640 prints in all). The traveller, the companion and the walking passers-by leave them (`pasDansLaNeige`, from their `animer` footfall). Prints are now laid on the ground as it is drawn (`solDessine`: the 1 m mesh's two triangles, 3 cm up), not on the terrain's curve: on uneven ground they sank under it. Changing season clears them (`effacerEmpreintes`).
  - **Poses.** The travellers' heads are big (radius 0.3 m for 0.29 m arms): an arm raised straight up always went through it. Raised arms now open in a V beside the head (the photo's « Ouais ! », jump and wave, and the dance's raised beats), and the heart is made with both hands in front of the chest. The 2026-10-23 session's `bras_tete.mjs` measures each arm's clearance from the head.
  - **Accessories per companion** (`accessoires` in `FORMES`: `bandana { r, tube }`, `triangle`, `triangleR`, `collier`, `grelot`, `grelotR`): Kon's bandana covers his red collar and hangs in front of his bib, his big golden bell replaces his own; Chapu's yellow bandana covers its blue one and the bell hangs from it; Gao's hat sits on the mane; Fuwa's bandana is at the neck (it covered the mouth).
  - Checks (the session scratchpad): `vues_saisons.py [prefix] [seasons] [--port]` (five views in four seasons, before/after on port 8124), `sous_arbre.py` (close-ups under the densest crown), `neige_pas.py` (walking in the snow), `poses_test.py` (every photo pose and the dance, girl and boy, with outfits), `acc_vues.py [companion]` (each accessory, front and profile).
- **No snow indoors (2026-10-25; the owner: « corrige la neige dans les intérieurs »).** With the magic calendar in winter, snow covered every floor and whatever faced up indoors (the keep's three floors, Gokayama's houses, the minshuku, the shop, the sauna's benches, Itsukushima's galleries), the calendar's flakes fell indoors, and footsteps crunched on the keep's basement floor: the snow only looked at the normal.
  - **The shelter map** (`carteAbri`, `balade.js`, once at load). Snow falls a little slanted: the GPU draws the decor (only the objects of `lieu.objets`, put on the layer `CALQUE_ABRI` for it; foliage, grass, petals and water discarded by their `saison` kind) along four directions tilted 30° (`DIRECTIONS_NEIGE`, `PENTE_NEIGE` in `styles.js`: from the east, west, north and south) with an oblique parallel projection: the image is (x − y·tx, z − y·tz), the depth keeps the highest piece on each slanted line, and its world height is written into a half-float red target, one quadrant of a 2048² texture per direction (`QUART_ABRI`, about 0.2–0.27 m per point over the fine extent), then a 3 × 3 maximum within each quadrant (`fente`: a few millimetres' crack between two roof pieces let streaks of snow into Itsukushima's gallery). Without a renderable half-float format (`EXT_color_buffer_float` or `EXT_color_buffer_half_float`) nothing is sheltered.
  - **Reading it** (`abrite(p)`, `ABRI_GLSL`): the four heights on the four lines through p (`textureLod`: no derivatives in the loop); p gets snow if it sees the sky in at least one direction. So roofs keep their snow, the keep's lower roofs too (a first version, a single map straight from above, left them bare: they lie under the eaves of the tier above, 5 m higher), and so does the ground at the foot of a wall; indoors, roof and walls hide every direction. Used by the decor's snow (`neigeFeuillage` takes `abri`), the ground (`solSaison`: no snow, fallen leaves or petals under a roof, the grass there dry instead of summer green; read only where needed) and the calendar's flakes, petals and leaves (`calendrier.js`, per flake in the vertex shader: through a window one sees them fall outside). Wind-blown snow still reaches about half a metre inside an open door and the open sides of Itsukushima's galleries.
  - **Footsteps** no longer crunch under a camera roof (`sousLesPieds`: `monde.abri` is Infinity).
- **Measured** (alternating with the previous version): building the map costs about 5 ms once its two small shaders are compiled (25–70 ms the first time, most of it the decor's geometry sent to the GPU, which the first frame did anyway); 8 MB of GPU memory. GPU per frame at 3200 × 1800 (`gpu_saison.py`): in winter +0 to +0.08 ms (Himeji 1.30–1.55 ms, Gokayama 1.85–1.95, Itsukushima's gallery 1.13 → 1.19); in summer the same (the map is read only in winter, and under trees in spring and autumn).
  - Checks (the 2026-10-25 session's scratchpad `himeji/`): `neige_dedans.py <prefix> [--port] [--seul place] [--saison s]` (interiors and outside views in winter, the player placed), `neige_dessus.py <prefix> [--pas]` (views from above: do roofs keep their snow; with `--pas`, the ground each footstep says, indoors and out), `planche_neige.py a b` (before/after sheets), `abri_bascule.py` (the same frame without and with the map: `mats.abri.abriFin` set empty), `gpu_saison.py [season]`.
- **Gokayama (2026-10-23, night; the owner: "Ajoute le prochain lieu pour la 3ème balade avec toutes les spécifications mises en place sur les 2 autres lieux", and picked Gokayama).** `gokayama.js`, map id `gokayama` (added to `BALADES`), TikTok `7645659293513108739`. A village of gasshō-zukuri houses in a meander of the Shō river (the river 14 m below in its gorge: `eau.niveau` −14, loop `riviere`), rice paddies, a stream, the shrine under the cedars, the mountain all around.
  - **Houses** (`MAISONS`, `gassho()`): 9 gasshō houses and 2 kura. Stone base; blackened plank walls with posts and shōji (1.5 cm in front of their frames: at 5 mm, the far houses' windows flickered seen from the belvedere); the door, closed or open; the gable's triangle of planks with three tiers of shōji under little plank eaves; two thatch slabs at 60° (patches of new golden and old grey thatch; `MATIERES.chaume` 16, the 17th texture layer: straw along the slope, rows, moss); the ridge with its poles and crossed sticks. At night the shōji paper glows (`sortesNuit`: `P.shoji` → `lumiere`); in winter the thatch (`sorte` 13, `chaume` in `SORTES`) keeps a thick snow cover even on its 60° slopes.
  - **Houses one enters, and the others (2026-10-24, the owner: « on peut tout le temps aller dans les maisons… qu'on puisse rentrer dans les maisons, mais pas dans toutes… il faut qu'il y ait quelque chose à l'intérieur, des décors, des personnages »).** A closed house is a `plein` block up to its ridge (it stopped at the stone base, 0.4 m: less than a step, `PAS`, so one walked in through the walls). Three are open (`ouverte`, a real door; `murH` 3.3, the museum 3.5): the museum, and those in `DEDANS` (each a builder in the house's frame), which get `dedansCommun` (the shōji seen from inside, `sorte` `lampe`: the daylight comes through them; the ceiling of smoke-blackened slats, its beams) and `mursOuverts` (walls with the door gap, the camera's blocks and the ceiling `toit`, the threshold stone). Their people carry `dedans` (the house's rectangle): `balade.js` hides them, and stops animating them, while the camera is outside farther than 14 m (six more passers-by had doubled the start view's frame time at CPU ÷4; back to 5.6 ms).
  - **The museum house** (民俗館, `musee`): earth floor (doma), plank floor (`plancherTroue`: cut around the irori; the planks stop 7 cm short of the doma step, whose top they shared and flickered), tatami room, the butsudan (`butsudan()`: the lacquered altar, its gold inside, the Buddha, candles, rin, offerings, doors open; it was a black box with a yellow square), fusuma, the irori (`foyer()`: frame, ash, logs burning at the tip, embers; the ash no longer in the frame's plane), jizai-kagi and kettle (steam), andon, display cases, the 塩硝の里 panel; sit at the irori. **The attic** (asked: « qu'on puisse monter à l'échelle et qu'il y ait un étage ») : the ladder goes through a trapdoor (`ECH`, `TR`) and sticks out above the attic floor; « Monter » / « Descendre » (`jeu.grimper` along `ECHELLE`, the camera placed above or below, `jeu.cadrer`, then `jeu.placer`); up there, under the thatch, the A-frame of the gasshō beams crossed at the top, bamboo purlins, tie beams, a slatted upper floor, the gables from inside with their lit shōji, six silkworm racks (trays, mulberry leaves, cocoons, worms), the silk reel (`DEVIDOIR`), rice-straw bundles, the 養蚕 panel, an andon; « Regarder » the racks (the companion's line) and the reel. The attic floor is a surface with `etage` (`monde.js`: `hauteur(x, z)` with no `y`, as for a passer-by or a start, ignores it: the guide stood on it) and `passage` (one walks under it: the tatami room is 2.59 m below, `TETE` is 2.6); the camera has its own `toit` up there and blocks under both thatch slopes and the gables.
  - **The minshuku** (`construireMinshuku`, door to the west, the okami still outside): the doma (shoe cabinet, slippers on the step, the water jar and its ladle, firewood, umbrellas), the plank floor and its irori with salt-grilled iwana stuck in the ash around the fire (white salted tails), the host (`aruji`, seiza, headband: he offers one, `iwana` + the companion's line) and a traveller from Tokyo (`invitee`) on cushions; the painted fusuma (`fusumaPeint`: ink mountains in golden mist, a moon, a pine), open onto the tatami room: two futons (« Dormir »: a nap until evening, or the night until morning, `jeu.nuit`), the tokonoma and its 一期一会 scroll, a vase, the tea table, an andon. Night lines.
