Measured: 294 k triangles of decor (262 k before); per frame +4 % from the bridge and the lawn, +6 % at the keep's foot and the top floor, +9 % at the stall; draw calls 252 → 217 (bridge), 241 → 200 (lawn), 220 → 177 (gate), 156 → 186 (at the stall); GPU (`gpu_charge.py`, alternating) 1.44–1.47 against 1.45–1.51 ms, keep and stall views 1.16–1.18 both; phone CPU ÷4: main-thread time per frame (`gk_cpu.py`, the CDP `TaskDuration` per frame) lawn 4.35 → 4.18 ms, top floor 5.25 → 5.14, keep's foot 3.91 → 4.07, medians 5.6 ms everywhere. Checks: `gk_test.mjs himeji`, `hm_bords.mjs` (none), `hm_marche.py` (16 routes), `hm_partie.py` (computer, phone with the boy and Pon), `menu_tel.py` (the menu at 390 × 844 and 360 × 640: a finger drag scrolls it to its end, the × stays on top and closes it), `copl_mm.mjs` on the gate, the keep and the stall (only hidden or same-colour overlaps: the hip tubes fold a little on themselves), `scintille.py` at the gate (edges only, no flat patch), night and seasons.
  - **A second floor to visit (2026-10-25; the owner: « rajoute la possibilité de monter un étage de plus : devant l'échelle tu choisis deuxième étage ou le dernier… tu peux te baser sur cette vidéo », What's Inside Himeji Castle, Anywhere But Home).** YouTube gave the Browser pane a Google captcha and its embed refused (error 153), so only the video's thumbnails were seen (`i.ytimg.com/vi/<id>/maxresdefault.jpg`, `maxres1..3.jpg`): the cover is the real keep's musha-bashiri (weapon racks on plank walls, lattice windows, curved beams, sloping rafters, a polished floor); the rest follows the real keep.
    - **The stairs** (cuts, `arriver(jeu, ou, texte)` and `ARRIVEES`): the basement's « Monter » asks the second floor or the top (`monterOu`, `etagesHaut`); the top's « Descendre » the second floor or the basement (`etagesBas`); on the second floor, « Monter » goes to the top (`monter2`) and the hatch's « Descendre » to the basement (`descendre2`).
    - **The tier** (`ETAGES[1]`, `ETAGE2`) was a solid box; now `etageOuvert`: four walls in two layers (plaster outside, `ENDUIT` inside, which does not light up at night), each one `ExtrudeGeometry` with real window holes at `fenetres()`'s positions (`centresFenetres`; `nuance: 0`: the holes' long triangles made patches), the windows a little taller there (`H_FEN2`, `Y_FEN2`) and framed outside without the black panel (`fenetres(…, { ouvert })`); its two bands in four strips (`ceinture`: the full boxes crossed the room); a plank floor, a ceiling. The first roof's gables now stop inside its wall (they reached into the room).
    - **Inside** (`adM`, drawn only with the camera on that floor): the moya (`MOYA`, 11.2 × 7.8 m): 20 posts, lintels with black iron nail covers, plaster above, plank partitions in some bays (two racks each on the corridor side), sills in the others; around it the musha-bashiri (1.9 m wide): racks between the windows (a board, six pairs of pegs), window frames and wooden lattices, gun-port shutters under the windows (square, round or triangular), the lintel, the wall plate, a curved ebi-kōryō beam from every post to the wall, the sloping plank ceiling and its rafters, hip beams at the corners; spears and guns on two partitions and the « 武具掛け » plaque, as in the video; the two great pillars; the stair up (to a ceiling hatch) with the « 二階 » and « 順路 → » plaques; the hatch of the stair from below; a dais of old roof tiles (an oni-gawara, two round crest tiles, « 姫路城の瓦 »); four paper lamps (the moya was black at night). The window frames and lattices, the lintel and the sloping ceiling are in the always-drawn part: from outside, without them, this tier's windows looked lit.
    - **People**: a volunteer guide (`benevole`), a little girl at a window too high for her (`enfant`), an Australian photographer (`photographe`), with night lines. **Things to do**: the racks, the east pillar, the tiles, a gun port, the view through a south window (`fenetre2`, the camera between the bars).
    - **Engine**: a `dedans` (an object of `lieu.objets` or a passer-by) may end with a height range, `[x0, x1, z0, z1, y0, y1]`; a passer-by's `etage` is the floor it stands on (`monde.hauteur(x, z, etage)`; without it an `etage` surface is ignored and it stood in the basement). The keep's indoor decor is three objects, each with its height range: the basement (`ad`), the second floor (`adM`), the top (`adH`), so from the second floor neither the basement nor the top is drawn, and the basement guide hides from the other floors. The keep's camera block is cut around the second floor (it kept the camera out). And an object drawn with the camera inside its bounding box is drawn first (`renderOrder` −1, set when it changes): what it hides, the walls, the town, is no longer shaded for nothing; `o.avant` gives this to an always-drawn object (`am`, the second floor's shell). On the second floor this took 1.1–1.3 ms off the GPU time (3200 × 1800).
- **Measured** (`hm_mesures.py`, alternating with the previous version): elsewhere in the castle, the same: draw calls +2 or 3 from the bridge, the keep's foot and the basement (the floor's shell), −3 on the top floor (the basement's decor is no longer drawn there); triangles +0.8–0.9 % (the top floor −0.1 %); GPU (3200 × 1800) 1.4–2.2 ms against 1.3–1.9; phone CPU ÷4 (`gk_cpu.py himeji`, four alternating runs) 5.4–8.1 ms per frame against 5.6–8.0, medians 5.6 ms everywhere. On the second floor: 160–192 draw calls, 0.98–1.03 M triangles per frame, CPU ÷4 like the other views, GPU 2.0 ms in the corridors and 2.8–3.6 ms looking across the moya (Gokayama's village: 2.3–2.9).
    - Checks (session scratchpad `himeji/`): `gk_test.mjs himeji` (everything in reach), `hm_bords.mjs`, `hm_etage.py [--nuit] [--tel]` (the choice both ways, every person and thing of the floor, a walk round the corridor, up, down to the second floor, down to the basement), `hm_partie.py` (now answers the choice), `hm_libre.py` (free-camera views, outside and inside), `hm_mesures.py` (before/after), `copl_mm.mjs himeji 10` and `8` (left: faces turned into the walls or against the ceiling).
    - **Seen then, fixed the same day:** with the magic calendar in winter, snow covered every indoor floor and the flakes fell indoors (see No snow indoors).
- **Walking into things (2026-10-22).** The owner walked into the Itsukushima shop houses (only their counter had a wall: one could enter from the sides and back) and into big bushes on Aogashima's plateau. The four shops are now `plein` blocks (from the front wall; see above for the shopkeeper). On Aogashima, the flank forest drew `t` from 0 to 2π while `largeurCrete` and `PLATEAU` expect −π to π: on the west half of the plateau the crest counted as 4 m wide and 2–3.6 m foliage balls grew on the walkable plateau; `ta` normalises the angle (this shifts the later random scenery: plateau and Maruyama bushes, clouds). Every bush (crater floor, plateau, Maruyama) and the wall-foot foliage balls collect into `buissons` and get a circle (radius + 0.15 m) once the passers-by are known, except within radius + 1.4 m of the road, a path, the stairs or a round. Checks (session scratchpad `balade/`): `obstacles.mjs` (before/after grid of free points, rounds, interactions as close as before), `atteignable.mjs <place>` (a breadth-first walk with the game's rules from the start, listing objects with geometry 0.4–2 m above reachable ground: railings, roofs and trunks show up there too), `regions.mjs` (every interaction and passer-by still reachable), `obstacles_jeu.py` (the player walks into a shop side and a bush and stops).
- **Never leave two faces in the same plane facing the same way**: they flicker (z-fighting). The owner saw it on the shop fronts at Itsukushima (the dark window was flush with the white wall); gallery corners (two floors, two ceilings), crossing rails and overlapping steps did it too. Fixes: an offset of a few millimetres to centimetres, one gallery owning each corner floor (`sol`), alternate step widths, each side of a railing a few mm higher (`lisses`). To find them: a Node script loading a place's `construire` with a local copy of three.js, grouping all triangles (world space) by plane and testing same-plane overlaps with separating axes (the 2026-10-22 session's `coplanaires.mjs`). Faces turned down under floors and tiny overlaps in the `toitJapon` roofs were left.
- **The camera** stays behind the player, comes closer and lower under a roof (`monde.toit`), stops in front of blocks (`monde.visee`), never under the ground or the water; scenery hiding the player fades in a screen-door pattern (`COUPE_GLSL`), characters never.
- **Measured** (2026-10-22): 180 fps in a phone context at CPU ÷4; about 0.4 ms of GPU per frame in a laptop context on this PC (3.5× the pixel version), hence the adaptive pixel ratio for integrated chips. Tests in the 2026-10-21/22 session scratchpad: `partie.py [--tel]` (a whole game: choice, talking, a stamp, the book, the menu), `visite.py <place> [--tel] [--seul a,b]` (screenshots at set spots), `nuit.py` (Aogashima's night and all 5 stamps), `fuites.py` (a breadth-first walk from the top of Aogashima's stairs with the game's own `libre`/`hauteur`, listing where one lands on the crater wall: the owner slid down it on 2026-10-22, through the 13 m gap of the crest fence; the fence now opens only on the path's width, the handrail turns to meet it, and the crest edge outside the fence is out of bounds except at the stairs), `gpu.py`, `fluidite.py`, `bouton3d.py` (the card button), `film_balade.py` + `realisateur.js` (the 51 s demo, slowed with `outils/video/ralenti.js`). Two "no supported source" errors come from the TikTok iframe in headless Edge: expected.

### Big cities (`site/villes.js`, 2026-10-22)

On 2026-10-22 the owner asked for the main cities: a different pin, the city outlined on a click, and their TikTok photo carousels ("8 places you HAVE to visit in Fukuoka city") on the map. They chose ~20 big cities (a pin even without a carousel) and the carousel's places as numbered points; from an examples page (the Design artifact « Villes sur la carte : exemples »), pin C (a little drawn town), smaller and **without its name** (it shows only up close), outline 2 (old town plan), and the card as drawn. After a first look in developer mode they asked for the music muted, a short description and history of each city, a box explaining each place, the list driving the carousel, the Sheet tab and their other carousels; then released it to everyone the same day.
- **Public.** `chargerVilles` (`app.js`, section « Grandes villes ») loads the module 1.2 s after the map is ready (at once for a `#ville-<id>` link, the card's share link). If it fails, the map just has no cities.
- **The pins.** `VILLES` (id, name, kanji, prefecture, city-hall point, `loin`): the 20 big cities plus Takayama (added for its carousel; `fabriquer_villes.py` has it too). A 32 × 26 cartouche with the town drawing (`DESSIN`), 24 × 20 in the far view, where only the cities marked `loin` show (14: Kansai's four cities would overlap); the name under the pin only from `ZOOM_NOM` 9.5 (`#carte.noms-villes`, toggled at the threshold), on hover or when open. Placed **in front of** the place pins (MapLibre appends): behind, Nakatsukasa shrine's pin covered Fukuoka's and took the click. Hidden in the game, a dive, a recorded dive and film mode.
- **A click** (`ouvrir`): the place card (`#fiche`) in `mode-ville` (CSS hides the place-only parts; `#fiche-ville` holds the city's), the outline (fetched once, prefetched 4 s after the module loads) drawn as an ochre wash, a thick sepia line and a thin one offset inside (`line-offset`), and **hatching as real lines on the ground** (`hachures`: 45° every 400 m, cut by the outline even-odd, fading out from zoom 11.5 to 13.5): a `fill-pattern` keeps its pixel size per tile level and jumps while zooming (as the season patterns did). Layers before `cote-encre`; if the style is not loaded yet (a `#ville-…` link), it waits for `style.load` (else "Style is not done loading"; `map.style._loaded`, re-check on upgrade). The camera framing is computed in `cadrerVille` (not `cameraForBounds`, which depends on the current padding and pitch: the same click gave zoom 9.6 or 8.9, 7.1 on a phone), on the pieces ≥ 10 % of the largest (Fukuoka reaches the islet of Oronoshima, 40 km out). `ouvrirLieu` and `fermerFiche` call `villes.quitter()`.
- **The card.** A red seal with the kanji (not in Japanese, the title is already kanji), « City · Fukuoka Prefecture · region » (`ville`: City / Ville / 都市), the city's description (`.ville-texte`, from the Sheet), the carousel, « 8 places to see in Fukuoka » (numbered buttons), the Sheet places inside the outline (point in polygon), « See the carousel on TikTok » and share.
- **The carousel** is TikTok's own player (`player/v1/<id>?autoplay=0&muted=1&music_info=1&description=0&rel=0`; it plays photo posts; `embed/v2` and `embed.js` answered "ratelimit triggered"). The photos are web pictures, so they are never copied into the site. Its frame is wider than tall (`height: min(46vh, 400px)`, 34 dvh on phones): the player then fills the width with the photo, and its arrows do nothing, so a line under it says to swipe the photos (a mouse drag works too). Its postMessage API (`{ type, value, 'x-tiktok-player': true }`):
  - **Sound off by default** (asked by the owner): `muted=1`, plus `mute` at `onPlayerReady` and at the first `onStateChange` 1 (`ouverte.sonCoupe`); the visitor turns it back on with the player's speaker icon.
  - **Photo → map**: the player posts `onImageChange` with the photo's index (0 = the cover); `choisir(n, { voler: true })` flies to place n (zoom ≥ `ZOOM_LIEU` 14, pitch 56, 50 on phones) and highlights it, 0 frames the city.
  - **List or point → photo**: `navigateTo` with n turns the carousel to that photo (found in the player's own code, it calls its `slideTo`; `seekTo` only restarts the music). A tap on a list number or a numbered point calls `choisir(n, { lecteur: true })`; the player then confirms with `onImageChange`, which does nothing more.
  - Tested with the real player in the Browser pane (EU cookie banner inside the frame; « Decline optional cookies » once).
- **The place's box** (`bulle(l)`): its number, name, Japanese name and the text from the owner's caption. On desktop a MapLibre `Popup` (`.bulle-legende.bulle-ville`) anchored `left`, beside the point; the flight pads the right by 300 px so it fits (and the top by 180 px for a merged pin, see below, so it stays clear of the top buttons). On phones it would sit under the card, so it goes inside the card, under the carousel (`.ville-encadre` in `#fiche-media`), and the card scrolls back to the top.
- **A place already on the map** (the owner's choice): a carousel place within `MEME_LIEU_KM` (300 m) of a Sheet place gets no numbered point. That place's own pin gets class `carrousel` and a red number badge (`.repere-carrousel`, a span in every pin's markup from `creerEpingle`), its box a « Watch its video » button (`.bulle-video`, `voirSaVideo`) that opens the place's own card. `quitter()` removes the badges.
- **The data**: two Sheet tabs, made on 2026-10-22 and published with the rest of the Sheet (new tabs are published automatically). `CONFIG.tableau.villes` (« Villes », gid 1022274676): `Ville` (English name, matched to `VILLES`), `Description (EN)`, `Description (FR)`, `Description (日本語)`, `Lien TikTok`, `Légende`, `Afficher ?`. `CONFIG.tableau.lieuxVilles` (« Lieux des villes », gid 2041476488): `Ville`, `N°` (the photo's index), `Nom`, `Nom (日本語)`, `Coordonnées GPS`, `Texte (EN)`, `Texte (FR)`, `Texte (日本語)`. Backups `data/secours-villes.csv` and `data/secours-lieux-villes.csv`, saved nightly by `sauvegarder_tableau.py`. `lireVilles` reads both when the module loads; `ouvrir` waits for them. Five carousels so far: Fukuoka (8 places), Hiroshima (7), Kanazawa, Nagasaki, Takayama (8 each). The places were read from the carousels' slides and captions (in the player, never by fetching TikTok pages), placed with OpenStreetMap (Nominatim), and their texts written from the captions; the names of Kanazawa's, Nagasaki's and Takayama's places were deduced from the captions, the owner may correct them in the Sheet. Shirakawa-go (Takayama no. 8) lies outside the city's outline, 50 min away. The descriptions (two sentences: what the city is and its history) were written by me. Since the same day, the Sheet robot fills new carousels (see The Sheet robot); the robot adds three columns to « Villes »: `Nom (日本語)`, `Coordonnées GPS` (for a city it adds) and `Robot`.
- **Cities added by the robot.** A « Villes » row whose `Ville` is not in `VILLES` (matched by `idVille`, the English name without accents or punctuation, the same rule as the robot's `cle_` and `fabriquer_villes.py`'s `id_ville`) and that has a `Coordonnées GPS` becomes a city (`nouvelleVille`, once the Sheet is read): its pin at that point (the city hall), `ja` from `Nom (日本語)` without 市/町/村, the prefecture from the point (`o.prefectures()`, the dice's outlines), visible in the far view when no far-view city is within `ECART_LOIN_KM` (60 km). Until the nightly run draws its outline, a click frames the city and its places (`bornesAutour`). `ouvrir(id)` waits for the Sheet when the id is unknown, so `#ville-<id>` works for them.
- **A place without a position** (the robot found none): kept in the list and the carousel, no point and no flight; its box goes in the card on desktop too (`.ville-encadre`).
- **Writing into the Sheet** (how the tabs were filled, in Claude in Chrome): a synthetic `ClipboardEvent('paste', { clipboardData })` with a `DataTransfer` holding tab-separated text, dispatched on `document.activeElement` (the `.cell-input`) after clicking the first cell; `navigator.clipboard.writeText` hung.
- Texts: `ville*`, `prefectureDe`, `voirCarrousel`, `voirSaVideo` in `TEXTES`. Test: the 2026-10-22 session's `essai_villes2.py` (public without developer mode, a fake TikTok player recording the commands it gets and answering `navigateTo` with `onImageChange`, Fukuoka's description and 8 points, list 5 → `navigateTo` 5 + flight + box, a swipe to 2, point 8, Japanese, the merged-pin case by routing the Sheet CSV with Ohori Park moved onto Nakatsukasa shrine, Takayama, a phone); `essai_villes3.py` (an added city, Kurashiki, routed into the Sheet CSVs with a place without a position: its pin, card, prefecture, list, `#ville-kurashiki`, phone).

### Near me (`site/pres.js`, 2026-10-13)

From the same list and examples page the owner chose: the round button near the compass (A), circles of distance rather than a list (B), and the route drawn on the map before Google Maps (B).
- **The button** `#btn-pres` (in `.controles`, before `#btn-recentrer`). The tap asks `navigator.geolocation` at once (`demanderPosition` in `app.js`: Safari wants the request inside the gesture) while `pres.js` loads (`chargerPres`), then `localiser(promesse)`. Pulses while searching (`.cherche`), red when active (`.actif`); a new tap re-locates. Refused or not found: a toast. Hidden during the game, the challenge and a tour.
- **In Japan** (`trouver.ecart(lng, lat)` ≤ `AU_JAPON` 25 km: the distance to the prefecture outlines, a new property of `chargerPrefectures`' function in `regions.js`): the 現在地 "You are here" marker (a red dot with a pulsing halo and a red-and-paper plaque, like the boards in Japanese parks; through `reperes`, in front of everything); three dashed ink circles (`pres-anneaux`, the smallest set of `ANNEAUX` whose outer circle holds the 3 nearest visible places: 0.5/1/2 km up to 100/200/400 km) with their radius in IM Fell under each; the distance under the 12 nearest places within 1.5 × the outer circle (`.pres-distance`, hidden in the far view); a pill in the button row (`.resume-pres`: "3 places within 10 km", a tap reframes, × clears). The camera frames the outer circle, north up, pitch 20 (`cameraForBounds` fits flat; a stronger pitch let the south spill under the bottom).
- **"Go there".** While a position is known, the place card's second button is "Go there" (`remplirBoutons` in `app.js`, `data-aller`) instead of the Google Maps link. `allerVers(lieu)` closes the card, clears the favourites' route, and draws the route from the visitor (`brancherItineraire` with `id: 'trajet'`, the same OSRM server and rules; on foot under `A_PIED` 3 km, else by car) with its time label. The `.pres-trajet` card (bottom-left on desktop, a bottom sheet on phones, where `body.avec-trajet` hides the clock) shows "To <place>", the time and distance, car / bike / foot, and "Open in Google Maps" (`/maps/dir/?api=1&destination=…&travelmode=…`, no origin: Google uses the phone's position). Drawing the favourites' route clears it (`tracerItineraire` → `effacerTrajet`).
- **Far from Japan**: the `.pres-loin` card: "You are 9,032 km from Japan" (the great-circle distance to the nearest place: `ecart` is a flat approximation, wrong at that range), the nearest place and its prefecture, "See this place" and "Plan my trip" (opens When to go). The camera goes to the start view, then a dashed red great circle (`pres-chez-toi`) leaves the nearest place toward the visitor, cut where it leaves the screen, with "✈ You" beside its middle (no city name: that would need a geocoding service).
- **Privacy**: the position stays in the browser; only "Go there" sends it to the routing server (as the favourites' route sends the places).
- Texts: `pres*`, `yAller`, `trajetVers`, `ouvrirGoogleMaps`. Tests: Playwright `geolocation` + `permissions=['geolocation']` (Kyoto, Paris, New York, Sydney), and a context without the permission for the refusal (the 2026-10-13 session's `essai_pres.py`).

### Place pages (`outils/fabriquer_pages.py`)

On 2026-10-02 the owner approved idea 6 (one page per place, so Google can find the places) from a mockup, as a step toward a shop on their own domain. The map itself is one JS page that Google can't index place by place.
- **What it builds.** For each place and language: `site/<lang>/<id>/index.html` (the id is the map's slug, so `#id` on the map and `<lang>/<id>/` match). For each language, `site/<lang>/index.html` lists every place by region and prefecture. Then `sitemap.xml` (with `hreflang` alternates), `robots.txt`, and `pages/japon.svg`, the locator map's background. 127 places gave 381 pages.
- **Source.** The backup CSVs (`site/data/secours-*.csv`), read with the same rules as `app.js`: loose headers, `nettoyer`, the slug with `-2` for duplicates, `Afficher ? = Non` skipped, unknown categories coloured from `PALETTE`. Region and prefecture names are parsed from `site/regions.js`; the prefecture comes from `trouver()` in `fabriquer_prefectures.py`. If `regions.js` changes shape, the script's assert fails.
- **A page.** Breadcrumb (Japan › region › prefecture, linking to the list's anchors). The TikTok cover (`photos/tiktok/<video id>.jpg`) as a 3:4 button; a click swaps in the TikTok player (`pages/pages.js`), so the page stays light. Name, Japanese name, category · prefecture, buttons (3D map `../../#id`, TikTok, Google Maps directions, the Sheet's other link), description and video date. "Where is it?": an inline SVG locator, the whole of Japan (Mercator, `japon.svg` as an `<image>`), the prefecture's outline in pale red and a pulsing red dot. "Nearby": the 3 nearest places.
  - Each page has its title, meta description (the description cut at 155 characters), canonical, `hreflang` en/fr/ja + `x-default` (en), Open Graph (the Sheet photo if local, else the cover), and JSON-LD: `TouristAttraction`, `VideoObject` (`uploadDate` from the video id, `embedUrl` = the player) and `BreadcrumbList`. Pages load GoatCounter too, so their visits appear in the dashboard by path.
  - Style: `site/pages/pages.css` repeats the map's tokens (parchment sheet with the double rule on the sea colour, Zen Antique, IM Fell, red buttons). Category icons come from `icons.js`, inserted by `pages.js`.
- **Covers.** TikTok's thumbnail URLs expire, so the covers are copied into the site (`telecharger_couvertures.py`). A place without a cover gets a page without the picture.
- **From the map.** The card links to its page (`#fiche-plus`, "Page of this place"), and the bottom of the Categories menu links to the list (`#lien-liste`); both follow the UI language.
- **The address.** `ADRESSE` at the top of the script is the public address used for canonical, `hreflang`, Open Graph and the sitemap. Change it with the domain (as well as `og:url`/`og:image` in `index.html`). Since 2026-10-02 the site is at a domain's root, so `robots.txt` counts; the sitemap is also submitted in Google Search Console (a Domain property for randomjapanplace.com, verified and submitted by the owner on 2026-10-02).
- **Checks.** No test suite: rebuild, then check every internal `href`/`src` resolves to a file and that each JSON-LD block parses (a throwaway script did this on 384 pages).

### Visit counter (`site/compteur.js`)

On 2026-10-01 the owner asked for a visitor count on the desktop map. They chose **GoatCounter** (free, cookie-free analytics with a dashboard) over an Apps Script counter or a keyless public counter, and chose to count **every visit**.
- `CONFIG.goatcounter` in `config.js` is the account code (`code` for `code.goatcounter.com`). While it is empty, nothing is loaded or shown. The owner's account is `randomjapan` (randomjapan.goatcounter.com, switched on 2026-10-01; only the owner can log in).
- `brancherCompteur` (called first thing in `demarrer`) injects `gc.zgo.at/count.js` with `data-goatcounter`, so every page load counts, phones included. count.js skips localhost, so local tests never count.
- On desktop only, it fetches `https://<code>.goatcounter.com/counter/TOTAL.json`. That needs "Allow adding visitor counts on your website" ticked in the GoatCounter site settings; without it the request fails and the counter stays hidden.
  - `count` is a formatted string ("1 094 100"), so the code keeps the digits only. It counts GoatCounter "visitors", which are sessions: a reload soon after does not count again, a return visit later does.
  - GoatCounter caches that public total for up to 4 hours. So the counter rolls up from 0 to the total in 1.6 s, then adds the current visit (+1, with a small jump). It re-reads the total every 15 minutes while the tab is visible.
- The `#visites` box sits in the header, right of the title, behind an ink rule. It is hidden on phones. (Do not reuse `.compteur`/`#compteur`: they are the Categories count badge.)
- Texts: `visites(n)` and `visitesInfo` (the tooltip) in `TEXTES`.
- Tests: route `config.js` to set a fake code and fulfil `TOTAL.json` and `count.js` with Playwright (`ctx.route`).

### Random place (the dice)

The "Au hasard" button opens `#panneau-hasard`: a region `<select>` (all Japan, 8 regions with `optgroup`s, or one prefecture), a type `<select>` (categories) and a roll button.
- **Prefectures are not in the Sheet.** Each place's prefecture is computed in the browser from its GPS coordinates.
  - The outlines are in `site/data/prefectures.geojson`, generated by `outils/fabriquer_prefectures.py` from Natural Earth.
  - That script also prints the prefecture found for every place, which is how the results were checked.
- Coastal and small-island places can fall just outside the simplified outlines. For those, the code picks the prefecture whose nearest **edge** is closest. The nearest vertex is not enough: Uradome Coast came out in Hyōgo instead of Tottori.
- Region and prefecture names and the region grouping live in `site/regions.js`. The outlines are prefetched 3 s after start-up.
- A place opened by the dice sets `tirageActif`, which shows the `#fiche-autre` "Un autre !" re-roll button on the card.

### Icons

`site/icons.js` exports `ICONES` (hand-made 24×24 SVG paths) and `iconeHTML(nom)`. `iconeHTML` falls back to rendering the text as an emoji, so a Sheet can use 🍜 directly.

The same icon names are listed in the Sheet's `Icônes` tab, which feeds the Sheet's dropdown. Keep both in sync when you add an icon, and give the new icon a 3D model in `site/modeles3d.js`. `site/icones.html` is a gallery page for the owner.

### The Sheet robot (`outils/robot-tableau.gs`)

The robot is a Google Apps Script. The owner pastes a TikTok link into an empty row of `Lieux` (in `Nom (EN)`, or in `Lien TikTok` with an empty name), and the robot fills the whole row.
- **Where it runs.** It is a *standalone* Apps Script project, "Robot carte" (`https://script.google.com/home/projects/1MXTOIbdJ85kVtWstryW6DnIrialyZOHZDgt5x0LnENphuMCl20Jit97q/edit`). It opens the Sheet by `ID_TABLEAU`. The repo file is only a reference copy.
- **Updating it.** Push the file, then load it into the editor:
  1. Get the raw GitHub URL at the commit sha. The pinned sha avoids raw's cache.
  2. In the editor tab, run `monaco.editor.getModels()[0].setValue(code)`.
  3. Click the "Enregistrer le projet dans Drive" button. Ctrl+S does not save.
  - If the Chrome window is minimized, dispatch mouse events on the aria-labelled buttons from JS.
- **Triggers.** `installerRobot()` creates two: `robotCarte` every 10 min, and an installable onEdit (`quandModifie`) for the Sheet.
  - `robotCarte` takes a script lock and handles at most 3 rows per pass.
  - Its status and doubts go in a `Robot` column: ⏳ = retry, ❌ = the robot waits until the owner erases the message.
- **Pipeline** (`preparerFiche_`):
  1. The caption: TikTok oEmbed's `title`. **Since 2026-10-20 oEmbed returns only the player `html`** (no `title`, `author_name`, `thumbnail_url`), so the robot read an empty caption, Gemini answered `pas_un_lieu` and the row was moved to `À trier` (the owner thought new places could no longer be added). Now: a name the owner wrote in `Nom (EN)` next to the link (`nomDonne`: a row with a link, a name, no GPS, no category, Robot empty or ⏳) is used as the caption; otherwise `lireIntegree_` reads `videoData.itemInfos.text` from the embed page `tiktok.com/embed/v2/<id>` (`__FRONTITY_CONNECT_STATE__`), but TikTok answers 429 "ratelimit triggered" to Google's servers (tested on 2026-10-20, first request); with no caption the robot retries (⏳) then asks for the name (`SANS_LEGENDE`), and never moves the row. `LIENS_TEST` holds `[link, name]` pairs. `telecharger_couvertures.py` takes the cover from the same embed page (`itemInfos.covers[0]`) when oEmbed has none; the site's own fallback (`miniatureTiktok`) just finds no image. So new places' covers are made from the owner's own video files (`couvertures_montage.py`, see Commands).
  2. **Before any AI**, it geocodes the caption's own place name with `Maps.newGeocoder()` (`nomDansLegende_`: "Ibuki Tree Art Sculpture | Kagawa 📍 #…" becomes "Ibuki Tree Art Sculpture, Kagawa").
     - The owner's names usually come from Google Maps, so this gives an exact POI (types `establishment`/`tourist_attraction`).
     - The resulting address is fed to call 1.
     - Without it, Gemini read "Ibuki" as the juniper tree and placed Ibuki Island's artwork 37 km away, in Manno.
  3. Gemini call 1 (`identifierLieu_`) returns the place name plus search queries. Compilations are moved to the `À trier` tab.
  4. It searches Wikipedia (en + ja) and runs a second geocode with call 1's Japanese query. All of these are free and keyless.
  5. Gemini call 2 (`redigerFiche_`) writes `nom_fr`, the category, the 3 descriptions and the seasons (`saisons`, see When to go), and picks a GPS source (`google_maps_legende`, `google_maps` or `wikipedia_*`).
  6. `choisirGPS_` prefers a precise Google Maps hit within 3 km. It flags `approx` when Maps only found an area, or when Wikipedia and precise Maps disagree.
  - An `approx` row gets `Afficher ? = Non`, so no wrong pin goes public.
  - **The EN/JA names always come from call 1, in code.** With the name left to call 2, Gemini (especially flash-lite) renamed Udo Inari Shrine to its famous neighbour Udo Jingū, because Wikipedia only covers the latter.
  - Because the names are locked to call 1, a kanji typo from it would stick (it wrote 七宝隆寺 for 七宝瀧寺). `corrigerNomJa_` fixes that case: it takes the title of a Wikipedia article located within 1 km of the chosen GPS when that title is the same length and differs by exactly one character. The 1 km check keeps 東大寺 and 西大寺 apart.
  - The free sources don't describe obscure places (for example the artwork 伊吹の樹 on Ibuki Island). The prompts forbid inventing details and made-up Japanese names, so expect short, general descriptions there.
- **AI.** It uses the free Gemini API tier (`CLE_GEMINI` in Script properties; the owner created that key and it must never pass through us). The Anthropic API was rejected because it is paid.
  - The free tier has no Google Search grounding on 3.x models.
  - `demanderGemini_` falls through the model list on 429, 404 or 5xx. `gemini-3.8-flash` often returns 503 "high demand", so flash-lite does much of the work.
  - `verifierCle()` lists which configured models exist.
- **Tests.** `testerSansIA()` and `testerAvecIA()` run on `LIENS_TEST` without writing to the Sheet. There are three test cases:
  - Udo Inari: a famous-neighbour trap;
  - Ibuki Tree Art Sculpture: its exact spot is 34.1302, 133.5345;
  - Shipporyu-ji: its correct Japanese name is 七宝瀧寺, and call 1 gets that kanji wrong.
  - A full run takes about 5 to 55 s per video. It is slower when `gemini-3.8-flash` answers 503.
  - To choose the function to run, use real clicks on the dropdown. Synthetic JS events do not select an option.
- Rows the robot fills get `Afficher ? = Oui`, `À vérifier = Oui` and a yellow background, so they are live on the map before the owner reviews them.
- **Carousels (2026-10-22, asked by the owner).** The owner pastes a carousel's link (`/photo/<id>`, or a short link: `lireLien_` follows it, shared with `lireVideo_`) into an empty « Villes » row (`Lien TikTok`, or `Ville`) and its caption into `Légende`: TikTok gives the robot no caption (it tries the embed page once). `robotCarte` then calls `robotVilles_` (one carousel per pass, if under 2 min were used); `quandModifie` also fires on a « Villes » edit with a link or in `Légende`. A row is done when its `Robot` cell starts with 🤖 or ❌; a row with a link, an empty `Robot` and places already in « Lieux des villes » (the five hand-made carousels) is left alone. `traiterVille_`:
  - the captions do not name the places ("📍1- Every night, around 100 open-air food stalls…"): Gemini call 1 (`analyserCarrousel_`, `CONSIGNES_CARROUSEL`, the known city names given) returns the city (EN, official JA, prefecture EN/JA) and per number the place's names, a Japanese Maps query, FR/JA translations and a certainty. The English text is the caption itself, cut in code (`textesDeLegende_`: split on 📍n, trailing hashtags dropped); numbers Gemini skipped are reported (`oublies`);
  - `placerCarrousel_`: the city hall (`<prefecture><city>役所`, 役場 for 町/村); each place by Google Maps (a precise result within `RAYON_VILLE_KM`, 80 km, of it: Shirakawa-go is 33 km from Takayama), else the Japanese Wikipedia article's coordinates, else a district result (`sublocality`/`neighborhood`, within 15 km) marked approximate, else no position;
  - the city's existing row (all big cities have one) gets the carousel and the pasted row is deleted; a city whose row already has another carousel, or whose places are already in « Lieux des villes », gets ❌ (one carousel per city; to redo, delete the places and clear `Robot`). A row with no description is a new city: Gemini call 2 (`decrireVille_`, `CONSIGNES_VILLE`, with the city's English Wikipedia intro) writes two sentences in 3 languages, and the robot writes its `Nom (日本語)` and `Coordonnées GPS` (the city hall);
  - the places are appended to « Lieux des villes » in yellow, the « Villes » row too, and `Robot` lists doubts (certainty below `haute`), forgotten numbers, approximate and missing positions, a new city.
  - Tests: `testerCarrousels()` (Nagasaki and Takayama captions in `CARROUSELS_TEST`, nothing written; about 30 s each). First run (2026-10-22): Takayama's 8 places as found by hand; Nagasaki 7 of 8 (n° 7, "a bright red Chinese temple built in the 1600s", came out Kofuku-ji, by hand Sofuku-ji: both fit), all placed. The Sheet logic (merge into the city's row, new city, missing caption, refused second carousel, far place) is tested in Node against a fake Sheet with canned Gemini/Maps answers: the 2026-10-22 session's `robot_test.js` (it loads the `.gs` in a `vm` context and replaces `demanderGemini_`, `chercherCarte_`, `chercherWikipedia_`, `lireIntegree_`).

### Promo film (`outils/video/`, 2026-10-09)

The owner asked for a motion design video of the map: 9:16, 30 s at most, in the site's style, showing the main features, with a call to action at the end. It is shot in the real site, not drawn: `tourner.py` opens the local site in headless Edge with the real GPU, at a phone size (432 × 768 CSS px, device scale 2.5, `map.setPixelRatio(2.5)`, so 1080 × 1920 and the interface at a real phone's size).
- **Slow motion (`ralenti.js`, an init script).** After `__ralentir()`, the page's time runs 10× slower (`performance.now`, `Date.now`, `requestAnimationFrame`, timers, CSS animations via `playbackRate`). Flights are smooth and tiles arrive in time. A CDP `Page.captureScreenshot` (JPEG, `clip.scale` 2.5, about 70 ms) is taken each time the film clock passes 1/30 s. A draft runs at ×4 and 15 frames/s.
- **The direction (`realisation.js`, injected after load).** A film clock that stops during pauses: under a paper wipe, the map jumps to the next view and waits for its tiles (`areTilesLoaded`, at most 5 s), and those moments are not filmed. Cues at film times trigger real actions: `flyTo`, the `#hash` deep link (card), clicks on the dice, favorites, "Draw the route", a legend, the music button; `jouerTyphon()`, `jouerScene('baleine')`, `faireVoler()`. The overlay (`#film`, classes prefixed `fm-`: the site already has `.cartouche`, `.logo`…) draws titles as parchment cartouches with the double rule (Zen Antique, red words), ink taps, torn-paper wipes with a red seal (旅, 伝, 海), and the end card. It is laid out for a 540 px wide screen and scaled with `zoom`.
- **Scenes** (8 × 3.75 s = 2 bars of "Michikusa" by PeriTune, 128 BPM, its first beat at 0.31 s): the whole map with the hook title; a dive onto Himeji Castle in 3D; its card and the heart; the dice (Kyoto, Temples, `Math.random` pinned so it lands on Kinkaku-ji); favorites and their route; the Orochi legend found, with cranes; the living sea (the typhoon is panned to the middle of the screen, the whale put in open sea) and the music panel; the call to action (logo, "Explore Japan in 3D, it's free", the address, "Link in bio", EN · FR · 日本語, the music credit). Titles stay out of TikTok's caption zone and right-hand buttons. The game and developer mode are never shown.
- **Output.** `promo-carte.mp4` (H.264 CRF 16, music at −14 LUFS, fading out over the last 1.3 s) and `promo-carte-sans-musique.mp4`, for a TikTok sound. The music is CC BY: credit "Michikusa · PeriTune (CC BY 4.0)" is on the end card; put it in the caption too.
- In English, the language of the account's videos. A French version means translating the titles in `realisation.js` and setting `langue` to `fr` in `tourner.py`.

**Three more films (2026-10-10).** The owner asked for a different motion design; from three concepts they said "make all 3". `tourner.py --film <name>` picks one in `FILMS`: its scripts, music (a CC BY track of `site/musique/`, credited on its end card), where the track starts, output name (`videos/film-<name>.mp4` and `-sans-musique.mp4`) and the visitor's favorites. They share `commun.js` (`window.__creerFilm({ duree, css })`: the film clock with its pauses, `quand`, `sansFilmer` (pause the clock while the map changes: a cut), `chaqueImage`, `toucher` (ink drop or a phone screen-recording dot), `flash`, `exposer({ preparer })`); `realisation.js` keeps its own copy. The music was picked by measuring tempo and loudness (`numpy` on the decoded track): 118 bpm for Avenue Cafe, Awayuki's big entrance at exactly 20.0 s.
- **The places' own videos (2026-10-10).** The owner asked to see each place's video in the voyage and POV films, as the guided tour shows it. TikTok's player does not play in an automated browser, and we must not get around that, so the films use the owner's own video files: `VIDEOS_LIEUX` in `tourner.py` (default `D:\Montage\Vidéo lieux random japon`, one finished TikTok edit per place, named after it; env var `VIDEOS_LIEUX` to change it). `EXTRAITS` maps a place's TikTok video id to (file, start in s), picked on contact sheets (a strong shot, after the dive and the title overlay). `preparer_extraits` cuts 4 s into `videos/extraits/<id>-<start>.mp4` (720 × 1280, no sound, a keyframe every 0.5 s), only the missing ones. While shooting, Playwright routes serve them at `/__extrait/<id>.mp4`, and `https://www.tiktok.com/player/**` gets `faux_lecteur`: a page that plays the clip slowed like the page (`playbackRate` = the slow-motion factor) and speaks the player's postMessage API (`onPlayerReady`, `onStateChange`, `onCurrentTime`; `play`, `pause`, `seekTo`: a seek restarts the clip and offsets the reported time, so `visite.js` sees its start reached). The file named "Daibutsu-den NARA" shows the Echizen Daibutsu in Fukui (its own title says so), so Tōdai-ji was left out.
- **`voyage.js`, "The great journey"** (Ametsuchi, PeriTune): one take, no interface. The camera flies north to south, high between stops and low and slow over each: Hirosaki Castle, Fuji Shibazakura (the Sheet place at the foot of Mount Fuji; the camera passes between the two), Kinkaku-ji, Itsukushima, Sakurajima, Shuri Castle. At each stop a red ink dot pulses on the place and a photo-print card (`.fm-carte`) opens beside it, left or right (`cote`; on the right it sits higher, above TikTok's buttons), with the place's clip and its name in IM Fell plus its Japanese name, tied to the dot by an ink thread (an SVG line from the card's nearest edge). The clips are loaded as blobs in `preparer()` and start just before their card opens, at the slow-motion rate (`window.__facteur`). "From Hokkaidō…", "…to Okinawa", then it pulls back and turns to the whole of Japan (the site's phone start view) under a map-title end card with the address on a red ribbon. The camera is `jumpTo` every frame along a precomputed track: keys `[t, lng, lat, zoom, pitch]` interpolated by monotone cubics in Mercator, the bearing following the direction of flight (smoothed, then turning to 38°). **The centre's height is frozen** (`map.setCenterClampedToGround(false)` + `jumpTo({ elevation })`): otherwise it follows the ground under the centre and the camera jumps over every mountain. `preparer()` flies the track once (every 0.2 s, waiting for tiles) to fill the cache and sample the ground's height, then smooths it (σ 0.6 s). Pins, region names, legends, the watermark and the title scroll are hidden (`display: none`; `visibility` let the region names' inner spans show).
- **`zoom.js`, "The zoom out"** (Awayuki, PeriTune, from 13.0 s so its rise lands at 7 s; its bars last 1.41 s): it opens close on Itsukushima's 3D torii, orbiting slowly, under a "1 place" counter plate and "I filmed in Japan…". The owner found the first version badly shot (2026-10-10): the pins popped at the frame's edges while the map turned, as 11 px dots. The pull-back now goes by steps of two bars (A to E): a quick move, then an almost still hold (two near-identical keys of `CLES` `[t, lng, lat, zoom, pitch, bearing]`, monotone cubics, so the camera does not turn during holds). The bearing turns to follow Japan's axis (68° over western Honshū, down to 38° for the whole of Japan) only during moves. Holds: Hiroshima Bay (zoom 8.6), western Honshū and Shikoku (7.4), Kyūshū to Kansai (6.3, still above the 6.2 dot threshold, so the pins show their icons), half of Japan (5.3), all of Japan (4.2). Every pin is hidden (`.repere:not(.fm-vu)`) until its head (`.repere-tete`: the marker element itself has no size) is well inside the frame (5% margin); waiting pins light up nearest to the centre first, at most `RYTHME` (42) per second, so each hold gets a cascade; each pops (`scale` animation, which composes with the pins' own `transform`) with a ring in its category colour. Pins keep `--t: 1` and far-view dots are 16 px. The counter plate and texts sit top-left (over the Sea of Japan at that bearing). The total is read from the Categories badge (`#compteur`): "132 places / One map."; when the address pill arrives at the bottom, the camera eases back so Kyūshū clears it. `window.__zoomCamera(t)` gives the camera at film time `t`, for tuning views.
- **`pov.js`, "POV traveller"** (Avenue Cafe, 魔王魂): a TikTok-style video, everything timed on beats (`b(n)`). White-on-black captions ("POV: you plan your Japan trip in 30 seconds"), then 1. favorites: the Categories search typed letter by letter, the Kinkaku-ji result, the heart; Himeji Castle and Kumano Nachi-taisha come by jump cuts (`location.hash` under `sansFilmer`, with a white flash) and a heart each, counted on a chip; 2. the favorites panel and "Draw the route" (the real OSRM route and times); 3. "Guided tour of my favorites" with each place's video (the fake player above) and a 3 s stay (an option added to `#visite-duree` for the film), "next" tapped to skip the overview. Jump cuts keep it short: the first flight is seen leaving, then the clock pauses until the place's video plays, and again each time the video frame closes (the frame keeps `joue` while closing: wait for `.visite-video.visible.joue`). Under each video the place's 3D model and the red route show. The step-3 caption sits under the video frame during the tour. The end card comes over the tour's closing overview.

### Halloween videos (`outils/video/halloween/`, 2026-10-21)

The four motion-design films above did badly on TikTok (about 270 and 300 views; the owner showed two). Asked which skills or tools would make better promo videos, I advised that the format mattered more than the tooling (a real place in the first second, the map as the reveal, shorter, a TikTok sound, topical), and installed the **Remotion** and **video-shotcraft** skills (see the session memory). The owner then asked for three **16:9** videos of at most **20 s** about the Halloween event, attractive from the first second and dynamic: the first on Japan's most haunted places, the other two my own ideas. They also allowed the **Flora** connector (their account) for voice-overs.
- **The pipeline**: `rushes.py` shoots map "rushes" in the real site (night, the whole event on), `preparer.py` gathers the owner's clips, music and sound effects, `voix.py` fetches the voice-overs, `montage/` (a Remotion project, `npm install` once) edits them, `finir.py` renders each video with and without music. Everything generated goes to `videos/halloween/` (git-ignored); `videos/halloween/public` is Remotion's public dir (`remotion.config.ts`).
- **Rushes** (`rushes.py` + `plans.js`): like `tourner.py`, headless Edge with the real GPU, `ralenti.js` (×0.1) and one CDP capture each time the plan's clock passes 1/30 s, but in 16:9: a 1280 × 720 page at ×1.5 (1920 × 1080), the close shots on a 960 × 540 page at ×2 (the yōkai bigger), the cobweb/cat shot at ×3 to crop. **Not above ×2 for the map**: `qualite.js` caps its pixel ratio at 2, so a ×6 page gave a blurry map and holes in the foreground relief. The quality level is forced to fine (`localStorage.qualite`), `Math.random` is seeded (the same parade path, bats and hitodama every time), Halloween is cut for the day shot by routing `config.js` to `public: false`. A plan is a duration, camera keys `[t, lng, lat, zoom, pitch, bearing]` (monotone cubics, frozen and smoothed centre height as in `voyage.js`, scouted first) and actions at times. The plans add `body.en-visite` so the parade never starts on its own (`hyakki.js` skips tours), with CSS undoing its other effects; the season's particles stay visible while the camera moves (`.saison.repos` overridden). The parade is launched from the Halloween panel's button; `suivre` makes the camera follow it (the procession's middle, or one member after another `[[t, rank], …]`, smoothed in plan time: in slow motion the page draws far more frames per film second), after `avance` real seconds of walking. With `positions`, each member's box per frame is saved to `rushes/<plan>.json` so the edit can frame each yōkai.
- **The edit** (`montage/src`, React, `@remotion/media`, Google Fonts Zen Antique / IM Fell English / Noto Sans): shared parts in `commun.tsx` (a rush with an animated crop, the owner's clip full-frame or as a photo card, the numbered label, titles, lightning, vignette and grain, the map's sources always shown on map shots, the end card with the address ribbon and "link in bio", sound effects, music with ducking under the narrator). Cuts land on the music's beats (tempo measured with numpy: spectral-flux autocorrelation and the first attacks).
  1. `HauntedPlaces` (20 s, Kengeki · PeriTune): the Meiji Utsunoya tunnel and the title from the first frame, then Nagoro (the scarecrow classroom), Mount Osore (the jizō and pinwheels; the yūrei of the hunt on the map), Hell Valley (steam, a daytime shot slowed to 0.4: the clip's night shots carry the owner's "HOKKAIDO" title), Himeji Castle (its silhouette; Okiku on the map): each a night dive on the map with the owner's clip in a photo card; the end: all on the map, 7 yōkai to find. The opening lightning flashes from frame 4, not 0: a white first frame made a washed-out grey thumbnail.
  2. `NightParade` (17.6 s, 民族09「揺れる提灯」· 魔王魂): the lantern ghost in close-up ("Every night until Halloween… this walks across my map"), the whole procession with a 百鬼夜行 stamp, seven close-ups on the beat with a red seal and the yōkai's name (cropped from `v2-proche`, where the camera follows one member after another, by the saved boxes, 1150 px wide, with a radial spotlight on the named one: a first, wider crop showed several yōkai and you could not tell which was named), the legend, the end. A shot of the Halloween panel was dropped: its interface was too small at 16:9.
  3. `SpookyMap` (16.9 s, 月と狼 · 魔王魂, taken from 21.2 s so its entry lands on the lightning at 1.6 s): the day map with its cranes, then the same camera path at night (a match cut): bats, hats; the hats, the jorō spider, the cat, the parade; the hunt (six found, Enma tapped: the bubble, "Hunt complete!", the seven drawings of `halloween-dessins.js` counted 6/7 → 7/7); the end.
- **Voice-overs** (`voix.py`): ElevenLabs v3 through Flora, voice "George" (a deep British storyteller), English, with tags such as `[mysteriously]`, `[whispers]`; about $0.12 a line from the owner's Flora balance. The narrator takes his time, so each line is tightened (silences to 0.25 s, `atempo` 1.1) into `voix/court/`. Also from Flora: a thunder crack and a wind-and-whispers bed. Videos 1 and 2 are narrated; video 3 rides its music.
- **Sound effects**: only the video-shotcraft files whose Mixkit source is recorded in its `ATTRIBUTION.md` (free licence, no credit). Its BGM folder is not used (unclear rights).
- **Output** (`finir.py`): `videos/halloween/halloween-lieux-hantes.mp4`, `halloween-defile.mp4`, `halloween-carte.mp4`, each also `-sans-musique.mp4` (narrator and sound effects kept, for a TikTok sound); loudness −14 LUFS (the same gain for both versions), BT.709 TV range. Rendering one video takes about 1.5 min.
- **Vertical 9:16 for TikTok** (asked the same day): the same three videos at 1080 × 1920, same timeline, voices and sounds (compositions `HauntedPlacesV`, `NightParadeV`, `SpookyMapV`; files `-vertical.mp4` and `-vertical-sans-musique.mp4`). Not crops of the 16:9 ones: `rushes.py --vertical` reshoots every plan on a phone-shaped page (720 × 1280 at ×1.5, close shots 540 × 960 at ×2, so yōkai and pins keep their on-screen size; the phone layout of the site) into `public/rushes-v/`, and `plans.js` takes each plan's `v` settings (`window.__VERTICAL`). The components read the format (`useFormat` in `commun.tsx`) and keep text out of what TikTok covers in vertical (tabs at the top, about 170 px; caption at the bottom, from about 1450 px; buttons on the right): labels, names and the hunt row sit at `bottom` ≈ 470, the end card is raised 130 px, titles go to the top when the subject is at the bottom. Video 1's places use a camera `marge` (padding) so the place sits bottom-left, under the photo card (top right). **The parade path**: `hyakki.js` draws it from one screen edge to the other, so on a narrow page it was too short and the members left the frame before their close-up; `lancerDefile(large)` makes `map.getContainer`/`project`/`unproject` behave, during the click only, as a screen `large` px wide centred on the page (`cheminLarge`: 960 for `v2-proche`, as in 16:9, so the follow schedule is unchanged; 1280 for `v2-large`, at zoom 10.55 so the whole procession fits). In `v3-coin` the cobweb gets its desktop size and the title scroll (with the cat) is lifted to 34 % from the bottom, out of TikTok's caption.

### The 30 s promo (`outils/video/promo30/`, 2026-10-21)

The owner asked for a "very professional" 30 s motion-design promo of the map, as if for my own studio: the site's and logo's colours, the features and the Halloween event (until 31 October), a professional female ad voice-over, ultra-dynamic transitions, a sound effect for every animation and click, Flora allowed. First asked in 16:9, then corrected mid-work to **9:16 for TikTok**: the vertical one (`Promo30V`) is the deliverable, the 16:9 one (`Promo30`) a bonus. Made with the **video-shotcraft** skill in its autonomous mode (its pipeline, shot cards and aesthetic rules; its Ink Press template is for software products and was not used).
- **Direction.** The look comes from DESIGN.md and the logo (the paper diorama): parchment, sepia ink, cartographer's red, Zen Antique / IM Fell / Noto Sans, the double-ruled cartouche, red hanko seals; things are pressed, stamped, inked, unrolled. Tokens and the beat grid in `montage/src/theme.ts`.
- **Music**: "Amenoshita2" (PeriTune, CC BY 4.0, from `site/musique`), 142.4 BPM (numpy spectral-flux grid, phase 85.044 s), taken from 81.6 s so its breakdown carries the logo opening and its re-entry lands at 3.44 s (`b(n)` = frame of beat n). Every shot starts on a beat (`SHOTS` in `Promo.tsx`, the single source of timing; voice and effects are written relative to it).
- **Voice** (`voix.py`): ElevenLabs v3 through Flora, voice "Sarah" (warm, confident), English, one generation per line (a single take of the whole script could not be split reliably without speech recognition, and ran 29.8 s), tightened and sped up ≤ 1.15. **Sound effects**: video-shotcraft files with a recorded Mixkit source (`keyboard`, `pop`, `riser-cine`, `sparkle`, `whoosh-big` have none: not used) plus six made with Flora for this video (hanko stamp, dice on wood, riser, taiko hit, brush stroke, map unrolling; the brush is recorded quiet, hence its volume > 1) and the Halloween thunder and wind. About $2.1 of the owner's Flora balance.
- **Pictures.** Map shots (`rushes.py` + `plans.js`, on the Halloween shooting engine, which now exposes `window.__planMoteur` and takes `scripts`, `sortie` and `date`; `--vertical` for 9:16): the dive from all Japan onto Himeji Castle, the sea with its typhoon, the same Kyoto view in four seasons (summer at night: `plans.js` keeps the fireworks playing, the site pauses them while the map moves; winter over Iiyama, Kyoto has almost no snow). Interface stills (`captures.py`): the real page at ×3 (1280 × 720) or the phone site at ×4 (540 × 960, `--vertical`), each scene as a full image, the same without the animated element, the element cut out and a `layout.json` (Himeji's card, Kinkaku-ji's card, the dice panel, the favourites' OSRM route with its polylines in page px, Orochi's bubble). The Halloween shots are copied from `videos/halloween/public/rushes(-v)`. The owner's clips: Himeji under the cherry blossoms, Kinkaku-ji's pavilion.
- **The edit** (`montage/`, Remotion): `Page` is a 2.5D camera over a capture (CSS `zoom`, not `transform: scale`, so text stays sharp; the camera centre is kept far enough from the edges never to show past the capture: `cx ∈ [W/2/Z, page − W/2/Z]`); scenes: logo opening (letterpress name, 旅 seal, push into the paper Japan, light leak into the dive), the dive with the places counter and the voice's words, the card flying in (16:9) or sliding up as on a phone (9:16) and playing the owner's clip, the dice (a 3D die, the result card flipping in: drawn in screen space, inside the 3D page it went behind the capture), the route drawn in ink with hearts and travel times, legends / living sea / four seasons in a quad split, day→night on a lightning flash, HALLOWEEN slammed over beat cuts, two parade close-ups, the hunt 6/7 → 7/7, and a "group photo" ending (one element of each feature flies around the logo, which lands like a seal while the voice says the name; hold > 1 s). `Vertical.tsx` holds the 9:16 versions of the scenes whose layout changes; Halloween scenes read the format (`useFormat`).
- **Mix** (measured on the render): music about 10 dB under the voice while she speaks, about 3 dB under between lines; voice and effects turned down rather than the music up (it would clip); everything one frame early for the AAC delay. The first render had the music 20 dB under: check `musique seule` by subtracting the no-music version.
- **Output** (`finir.py`): `videos/promo30/promo-carte-japon-tiktok.mp4` (1080 × 1920) and `promo-carte-japon-16x9.mp4`, each with `-sans-musique`; −14 LUFS, BT.709 TV range.

### The funnel video (`outils/video/tunnel/`, 2026-10-21)

The owner pasted a brief: a 30 s vertical 3D UI animation (1080 × 1920, **60 fps**) of the conversion funnel: a TikTok post plays → tap on the account name → the profile → tap on the bio link → the camera dives *through* the link into the mobile map → three features → outro (logo in a white disc, a statement, an italic line word by word from a blur at 27.5 s). Interface on separate 3D layers with depth and soft shadows, exponential and spring curves (no linear or soft moves, no fade to black), depth of field, motion blur, Flora voice and sound design. The owner sent their profile and the Aogashima post **as seen from another account, in English** (TikTok blocks bots; never fetch TikTok pages), picked Aogashima or Etajima-so (I took Aogashima: it is on the map, so its card can close the loop), validated the "nervous" spring and asked for a slightly faster dive; I wrote the voice text.
- **Curves** (`montage/src/theme.ts`): a damped spring solved analytically, x(t) = 1 − e^(−ζωt)(cos ω_d t + ζω/ω_d sin ω_d t), stiffness 380, damping 18 (20 % overshoot, settled in 0.45 s), for every element that arrives (also sampled into a CSS `linear()` easing for the map pins, in the page itself); exponential S curves for slides and whip pans; the dive multiplies the zoom: ×0.96 (a 0.3 s pull-back) then × 50^E(u), E(u) = (e^(4u) − 1)/(e^4 − 1), over 0.85 s.
- **The TikTok part** (`TikTok.tsx`, `Telephone.tsx`): the post and the profile are rebuilt in HTML from the screenshots (`videos/tunnel/sources/`, not in git) at 1080 × 2340 (the screenshot × 1.17), TikTok Sans and Noto Color Emoji, icons redrawn as SVG (`Icones.tsx`); only the profile grid thumbnails are crops of the screenshot. As a visitor who does not follow yet: a red "Follow" button; the sound disc shows the account's photo (the real post shows another artist's cover). Each element is a `Calque` at its own `translateZ` (the pages are never clipped: `overflow` or `clip-path` would flatten the 3D). One continuous camera (perspective 2600 px): it looks at the post, pushes to the name, the pages slide (post out left, profile in from the right), it floats, goes to the link, pulls back and dives; the layers flatten (`kz` → 0) before the dive, since a `translateZ` scaled ×50 would pass the camera. The link's text stays readable up to about ×5, then its cyan pill turns white and fills the screen, which then opens on the map through a growing rounded rectangle. The background is the post's video, blurred and darkened.
- **The map** (`rushes.py` + `plans.js` on the Halloween shooting engine, now at 60 fps: `tourner(..., ips=60)`): three takes on a 540 × 960 phone page at ×2, day, no Halloween (the video must outlive 31 October): `tn-japon` (all Japan and its dots, which fade while the camera rushes to central Honshū, then the pins pop from the centre outwards with the spring), `tn-aogashima` (a dive onto the island, then an orbit), `tn-filtres` (west Japan: every pin shrinks away at "Hide all", the shrines pop back, then all pins return as the camera pulls back to Japan). Pins pop with Web Animations on `.repere-tete` (`scale`/`translate` individual properties, origin at the tip); animations on pins that the filter takes off the page must be cancelled by reference (they are no longer in `document`) or they keep their last, invisible frame when they come back. Each take's actions are timed to the edit (`temps.ts`).
- **Interface stills** (`captures.py`): the phone site on a 432 × 768 page at ×5 with the map hidden and a transparent page (`omit_background`): each button cut out with its shadow, Aogashima's card, and the categories menu in three states (all, none, shrines only) with its rows' boxes; the mouse is moved off the page first (a hovered row stays highlighted).
- **The edit** (`Carte.tsx`): the takes on a 3D plane that sways, lands with the spring and whips from one take to the next; the buttons pop on their own layers above it; a tilt-shift (`backdrop-filter` blur masked to the top and bottom of the frame) that blurs everything behind the card, which unfolds from a bottom hinge facing the camera; the menu falls in slices (its header and four rows; the map stays visible below, where the pins change); a finger with a ripple for each tap; short labels for viewers without sound; the map credits at the bottom. Motion blur (`@remotion/motion-blur`, `CameraMotionBlur`, 8 samples, 14 during the dive) only in the fast windows.
- **Sound** (`voix.py`, `Son.tsx`): nine lines by "Sarah" (ElevenLabs v3 through Flora), first person avoided ("every place, one link", "a place from the videos"); four effects made with Flora (tap, dive whoosh with its impact at 0.45 s, card pop, a cascade of pops for the pins), the others reused from earlier videos (`preparer.py`); the music "Amenoshita3" (PeriTune, CC BY 4.0, from 30 s) halved under the voice. About $1.67 of the owner's Flora balance.
- **Rendering pitfalls.** Use `OffthreadVideo` (Remotion's compositor), not `@remotion/media`'s `Video`: under the motion blur's many `Freeze` copies, the latter silently drew a black map for 8 s of a full render (short ranges were fine). The compositor in turn stopped with "Output changed" when the dive's 14 samples all asked for the post's clip: the clip is re-encoded with explicit colour tags and a keyframe every 15 frames, and the phone's blurred background uses a small pre-blurred copy (`aogashima-flou.mp4`) that is no longer drawn once the profile covers the screen (7.4 s). With the default concurrency (one tab per two cores, 10 here) the compositor ran out of memory: `finir.py` renders with `--concurrency=4` and a 2 GB video cache. Every clip and take must last until the last frame that shows it, motion-blur samples included (one frame ahead).
- **Output** (`finir.py`): `videos/tunnel/carte-japon-tunnel-tiktok.mp4` and `-sans-musique.mp4`, −14 LUFS (the music about 8.5 dB under the voice while she speaks), BT.709 TV range, 60 fps.

### `outils/`

The scripts that built the initial Sheet (My Maps KML import, TikTok comparison, geocoding, the `.xlsx` uploaded as the Google Sheet) and their JSON sources were removed on 2026-10-12: the Sheet is the source now, and they are in git history if ever needed. To refresh the backup CSVs, use `sauvegarder_tableau.py`.

### Review of 2026-10-24 (Vercel's guidelines)

The owner installed Vercel's agent skills and asked to review the map's interface with `web-design-guidelines` and `writing-guidelines` (rules fetched from `vercel-labs/web-interface-guidelines` and `vercel-labs/writing-guidelines`; the documentation-only rules, like Title Case and page plans, were left out). Fixed: the closed card is `inert`; `theme-color` follows night; `preconnect` (with `crossorigin`: modules and `fetch` are uncredentialed) to `cdn.jsdelivr.net`, `docs.google.com` and `tiles.mapterhorn.com`; `touch-action: manipulation` on `a, button, label, input, select, summary` (it can only remove double-tap zoom: the effective touch-action is the intersection with the ancestors, and the map's container is `none`) and no tap highlight; a real `h1` for the title (same look); names for the search field and the zoom buttons in the three languages; a visible focus ring in the category sub-lists; « label : value » screen-reader names per language (`deuxPoints`). Texts: the English is American spelling (Favorites, colors, license, traveler), curly quotes, error messages say what to do next, « Quand pars-tu ? » (the only « vous » left), « Lire la vidéo TikTok de chaque lieu » (« passer » read as « skip »).

### Cleanup of 2026-10-12

The owner asked to reread the whole code and remove what is useless. Method, to reuse: a Playwright tour of the whole map (desktop and phone, four seasons, developer mode, dive, game, tour, route, legends, music) with CDP precise coverage (`Profiler.startPreciseCoverage`, taken before each navigation, or it is lost with the page) and CSS rule usage (`CSS.takeCoverageDelta`); then a manual review of what never ran. Result: every CSS rule was still used (hover, multiplayer, bravo, Japanese, reduced motion…); the dead code was three unused builders in `modeles3d.js` (`caillou`, `toit`, `pin`) and a duplicate `aDejaJoue` in `amis.js`. The rest was duplication, now in `commun.js`, `PANNEAUX` and the `data-t` attributes (see UI).

## Gotchas

- Photos hosted by Google My Maps cannot be shown cross-site (CORP), so they are kept as local files in `site/photos/`.
- `color-relief` palette: many mobile GPUs read the filtered DEM texture as float16, so the sea (exactly 0 m) decodes to about −0.5 m. The palette stops are read exactly, though. So never put stops within about 1 m of 0: stops at ±0.02 m once turned the whole sea green on phones. Headless Edge screenshots (SwiftShader) cannot catch this, because they decode exactly.
- Noto Sans JP renders `ō`/`ū` with a misplaced macron. `--police` therefore lists `Noto Sans` first for Latin text. The display face Zen Antique (`--police-titre`) renders them correctly.
- Mapterhorn returns 404 for some open-ocean DEM tiles (most sea tiles exist; the holes are patches of deep ocean, e.g. east of Honshū, around Ogasawara, south of Okinawa). MapLibre handles a 404 silently (it keeps the parent tile, and the sea colour is the background layer), but the browser logged each one in red in the console; the owner saw them on 2026-10-04.
  - Both DEM sources now load through the `relief://{z}/{x}/{y}` protocol (`site/tuiles-relief.js`, `brancherTuilesRelief`). It never requests a tile listed in `site/data/tuiles-vides.json` or under one (an empty tile has only empty children), and throws an error with `status: 404` instead, which MapLibre's `TileManager._loadTile` treats exactly like a real 404 (no error event). A 404 it did not know is remembered in `localStorage.tuilesVides`.
  - `outils/tuiles_vides.py` builds the list with HEAD requests: every existing tile is explored down to zoom 10 (about 26,000 requests), then zooms 11 and 12 only along the edges of the holes (about 13,000). Responses are cached in the system temp folder (`carte-japon-tuiles`). Result: 229 tiles, 2.9 KB. Rerun it only if 404s come back often.
  - A first version refined zooms 10–12 only near places; the owner then saw 404s around Minami-Torishima (154° E), where there is no place. The holes roughly follow 1° × 1° squares without land, but not exactly: predicting them from one probe per square marked 165 existing tiles as empty, so the list only holds tiles actually seen as 404 (a wrong entry would erase real relief).
  - `precharge.js` gets the same helper (`tuiles`): it builds real URLs with `tuiles.adresse`, skips empty tiles, and reads a missing z12 tile as 0 m.
- The live site is https://map.randomjapanplace.com/ (repo `RandomJapan/Random-Japan-Maps`, Pages build type "workflow"). The old https://randomjapan.github.io/Random-Japan-Maps/ redirects there. These hold absolute URLs to that address, to update if the address changes: the canonical link, `og:url` and `og:image` in `index.html`; `ADRESSE` in `outils/fabriquer_pages.py`; and the three share pages `site/legendes/{en,fr,ja}.html`. All other paths in `site/` are relative, so the site works under any path.
- The domain randomjapanplace.com was bought by the owner at OVH on 2026-10-02 (renewal every October, no payment method on file: they pay OVH's invoice). Its OVH DNS zone holds `map` CNAME `randomjapan.github.io.` and a root TXT `google-site-verification=…` (Search Console Domain property). The custom domain is set in the repo's Pages settings (`gh api -X PUT repos/RandomJapan/Random-Japan-Maps/pages -f cname=…`): with workflow builds, a `CNAME` file is ignored. The root domain is kept for the planned Shopify shop.
- Commit as `RandomJapan <334664814+RandomJapan@users.noreply.github.com>` (already set in the repo's local git config) so the owner's personal email never lands in public history.
